Version 1.23.0 — Project Naming Cleanup and New Good Baseline
============================================================
DATE
----
September 6, 2026

USER REQUEST / START OF 1.23.x SERIES
--------------------------------------
The user confirmed that the 1.22.x development series has reached a stable
point. The major gameplay, interaction, rendering, sequence, history, and
raycasting bugs addressed during the 1.22.x work are now considered resolved.
The current Bot Engine is also considered good enough for the present release:
it now consistently beats the user in actual play. Further bot development will
continue later, including planned Easy/Hard difficulty modes and additional
strategy work, but those changes are intentionally deferred rather than being
part of this baseline release.

The user designated the resulting 1.23.x starting point as the new good version
to work from. The project will also move to a cleaner set of active source-file
names and will now be committed to the new repository:

    cdubend0/CubeReversi

FILE-NAMING CLEANUP
-------------------
Version 1.23.0 changes the active Bundle filenames to use the complete
"CubeReversi" project name:

    - index.html
    - CubeReversi.css
    - CubeReversi.js
    - CubeReversiBot.js
    - CubeReversi_Log.txt

The HTML CSS and JavaScript references were updated to the new filenames, and
CubeReversi.js now imports the Bot Engine from CubeReversiBot.js. The application
and game terminology remains "Cube Reversi"; this release is a source-file
naming cleanup rather than a terminology change to the game itself.

CURRENT-CODE CLEANUP
--------------------
A review of the 1.22.35 source was performed before establishing the new
baseline. No broad refactor or behavioral redesign was introduced. The cleanup
was deliberately limited to items that were clearly stale or unused:

- The stale top-of-file CubeReversi.js description was updated so it describes
  the current project, including the Bot Engine and optional visual controls,
  instead of saying AI and advanced animation were reserved for later versions.
- The unused intermediate surface-denial calculation in the Bot Engine was
  removed. The calculation produced a temporary leader list that was never
  used because the later corner-priority selection rebuilt the mobility/surface
  criteria. Removing it does not change the bot's selected move.
- The current 3-Axis source comment was updated to use the current "3-Axis"
  wording. Historical versioned comments were intentionally left unchanged.
- No game rules, legal-move calculation, board geometry, raycasting, rendering
  behavior, keyboard controls, history behavior, sequence handling, or Bot
  strategy priorities were intentionally changed.

BOT BASELINE
------------
The current Bot Engine remains the established strategy from the 1.22.x
baseline. It evaluates supplied legal moves using the existing strategic
priorities and uses random selection only among moves tied at the final
tiebreaker. CubeReversi.js remains authoritative for game state, rules, legal
moves, and move commitment; the Bot Engine only selects a move.

Future bot work is intentionally outside Version 1.23.0. The next planned
Bot Engine improvements can add user-selectable difficulty, including Easy and
Hard modes, without disturbing the authoritative game-controller architecture.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
This is Bundle Version 1.23.0. All active version references were updated and
audited to 1.23.0, including:

    - HTML document title
    - visible version label
    - CSS release marker
    - JavaScript release marker and runtime document title
    - Bot Engine release marker
    - CSS cache-buster
    - JavaScript cache-buster

Historical version numbers in the development log and historical implementation
comments remain unchanged.

LOG CLEANUP
-----------
While preparing this baseline, the existing Version 1.22.7 development entry
was found at the end of the log rather than in chronological position among the
other 1.22.x entries. The entry itself was preserved and moved into its proper
location after Version 1.22.8. No historical release content was otherwise
removed as part of this cleanup.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- Started directly from the confirmed Version 1.22.35 Bundle.
- Renamed all five active Bundle files to the new CubeReversi filenames.
- Confirmed all HTML local CSS/JavaScript references point to the renamed files.
- Confirmed CubeReversi.js imports CubeReversiBot.js.
- JavaScript syntax was checked with Node for CubeReversi.js and
  CubeReversiBot.js.
- Confirmed all five required Bundle files are present and nonzero.
- Audited active version references and confirmed they are synchronized to
  Version 1.23.0.
- Confirmed the HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters are both 1.23.0.
- Confirmed the runtime document title and visible version label are 1.23.0.
- Confirmed no unintended old active filenames remain in the five source files.
- Final ZIP will be checked to contain exactly the five renamed Bundle files.
- ZIP integrity will be checked before delivery.

BASELINE STATUS
---------------
Version 1.23.0 is the new good baseline for continued Cube Reversi
development. The user will commit this Bundle to cdubend0/CubeReversi and
perform live testing on laptop and iPhone. Future development should branch
from this version rather than reopening the completed 1.22.x work unless a
regression is discovered.

Version 1.22.35 — Sidebar Toggle Order

- Moved “Wireframe” above “Green Cubes” in the sidebar so the sidebar order matches the Help menu order.
- No behavior, defaults, keyboard shortcuts, raycasting, rendering, or game logic changed.
- Version 1.22.34 remains the fallback for this update.

Version 1.22.34 — Compact Final Action Controls

- Moved the Resign and New Game sidebar buttons into a side-by-side action row to reduce vertical control-panel height.
- Preserved the existing button behavior and IDs.
- No game rules, raycasting, Green Cubes, Blue Cubes, Pulse, Wireframe, 3-Axis, Auto-Rotate, or keyboard behavior was changed.
- Version 1.22.33 remains the fallback for this update.

Version 1.22.33 — Optional Blue Cube Visibility

- Added a new sidebar toggle labeled “Blue Cubes”, positioned directly below “Green Cubes”.
- Blue Cubes is OFF by default, so legal-move blue surfaces are hidden unless the option is enabled.
- When Blue Cubes is OFF, the existing golden legal-move hover highlight remains visible and continues to identify the legal move under the pointer.
- Added keyboard shortcut B to toggle Blue Cubes.
- Added “B: Blue Cubes” to the Help menu directly below “G: Green Cubes”.
- The blue visibility toggle changes only the visual visibility of the legal-move surfaces; game rules, raycasting, hover detection, highlighting, and move selection are unchanged.
- Pulse behavior remains available independently; its animation state is preserved even while Blue Cubes are hidden.
- Version 1.22.32 remains the fallback for this update.

Version 1.22.32 — Green Wireframe and Completion Reveal

- Changed the optional Wireframe line color from gray to green while keeping
  the wireframe limited to the twelve outer edges of the main cube.
- When a game reaches Game Complete through normal play, Green Cubes are
  automatically turned ON/visible for the final board view if they were OFF.
- This is only a normal toggle-on action: the Green Cubes option is not locked
  and the user can turn it OFF again afterward. If Green Cubes were already ON,
  they remain ON.
- No raycasting, Pulse, Auto-Rotate, 3-Axis, keyboard, layout, or game-rule
  behavior was otherwise changed.
- Version 1.22.31 remains the fallback for this update.

Version 1.22.31 — Pulse and Green Cubes Default Off

- Changed the default state of the “Pulse” sidebar toggle to OFF.
- Changed the default state of the “Green Cubes” sidebar toggle to OFF, so green cube surfaces are hidden when a new game starts.
- The existing Pulse and Green Cubes toggles, keyboard shortcuts, and all underlying behavior are unchanged; users can turn either option back on at any time.
- No game rules, raycasting behavior, Wireframe, 3-Axis, Auto-Rotate, layout, or other interaction behavior was changed.
- Version 1.22.30 remains the fallback for this default-state-only update.

Version 1.22.30 — Simplified Toggle Wording

- Renamed the sidebar toggle “Show 3-Axis” to “3-Axis”; it remains unchecked by default.
- Renamed “Hide Green Cubes” to “Green Cubes”; Green Cubes is now checked by default, with the checked state meaning the green cube surfaces are visible.
- Renamed “Show Wireframe” to “Wireframe”; it remains unchecked by default.
- Updated the Help menu wording to match the sidebar: “X: 3-Axis”, “Z: Wireframe”, “G: Green Cubes”, and “P: Pulse”.
- Preserved the existing G, X, Z, and P keyboard controls and all underlying behavior.
- No game rules, raycasting behavior, Pulse behavior, Auto-Rotate behavior, wireframe geometry, or layout behavior was otherwise changed.
- Version 1.22.29 remains the fallback for this wording-only update.

Version 1.22.29 — Simplified Wireframe and Compact Sequence Controls

- Revised the optional “Show Wireframe” display so it now shows only the twelve outer edges of the main 3D cube.
- Removed the interior cell-boundary grid lines that made the wireframe visually too busy on larger boards.
- The wireframe remains gray, off by default, controlled by the existing Z shortcut and sidebar toggle, and excluded from raycasting.
- Kept the existing wireframe behavior otherwise unchanged.
- Placed the “Move Sequence” and “Load Sequence” sidebar buttons side by side to recover vertical space after adding the wireframe option.
- No game rules, raycasting behavior, Pulse behavior, Auto-Rotate, 3-Axis behavior, or keyboard controls other than the existing wireframe control were changed.
- Version 1.22.27 remains the established fallback for this feature series.

Version 1.22.28 — Optional 3D Wireframe Toggle
Date: 2026-09-06
- Added a new sidebar toggle labeled “Show Wireframe”, positioned below “Show 3-Axis”.
- The toggle is unchecked by default.
- When enabled, the active cube displays a gray 3D grid using lines at every cell boundary along all three axes.
- The wireframe is rebuilt automatically for the selected 4×4×4, 6×6×6, or 8×8×8 board size.
- Added keyboard shortcut Z to toggle Show Wireframe.
- Added “Z: Show Wireframe” to the Help menu directly below “X: Show 3-Axis”.
- The wireframe is visual-only and is excluded from the cube raycasting interaction model.
- All existing Hide Green Cubes, Pulse, Auto-Rotate, 3-Axis, layout, and game behavior is preserved.

Version 1.22.27

4×4×4 HIDDEN-GREEN RAYCASTING CONFIRMATION
---------------------------------------------
This release confirms the intended Hide Green Cubes raycasting behavior after
clarifying the desired 4×4×4 behavior. When Hide Green Cubes is ON, occupied
cells are ignored by getFrontmostLegalHit() on all board sizes, including
4×4×4. The pointer ray therefore passes through hidden occupied cells and can
reach the nearest legal blue cube on the opposite side of the main cube.

The legal cube behind hidden occupied cells can be highlighted, display its
coordinate, and be clicked/selected. The black/white spheres remain visible.

When Hide Green Cubes is OFF, the established raycasting behavior remains
unchanged, including the 4×4×4 occupied-cube anti-click-through behavior.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No game rules or legal-move calculation changed.
- No green-cube geometry or visibility behavior changed.
- No Pulse, Auto-Rotate, 3-Axis, keyboard, mobile layout, or coordinate-entry
  behavior changed.
- No bot behavior changed.

FALLBACK
--------
Version 1.22.24 remains the immediate known-good fallback for the surrounding
Hide Green Cubes/raycasting work.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
This is Bundle Version 1.22.27. Active project-owned version references and
the HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters are synchronized to 1.22.27. Historical
log entries retain their original release versions.

Version 1.22.26

CORRECTION TO VERSION 1.22.25
------------------------------
Version 1.22.25 was intended to make the raycaster ignore occupied cells when
Hide Green Cubes was enabled, but testing showed that the intended behavior was
not actually effective for 4×4×4: the hidden green surfaces disappeared
visually, but an occupied cell could still prevent a legal blue cube behind it
from being highlighted or clicked.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.22.26 makes the Hide Green Cubes state an explicit first branch in
getFrontmostLegalHit(). When Hide Green Cubes is ON:
- Occupied cells are ignored by the raycasting obstruction test.
- The nearest legal cell along the pointer ray is returned.
- This applies to 4×4×4 as well as 6×6×6 and 8×8×8.
- Legal blue cubes visible through the cube can now be highlighted, show their
  coordinate, and be clicked/selected.

When Hide Green Cubes is OFF, the established raycasting behavior is left
unchanged:
- 4×4×4 retains the occupied/green-cube anti-click-through behavior.
- 6×6×6 and 8×8×8 retain their established interior legal-move behavior.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No game rules or legal-move calculation changed.
- No green-cube visibility or geometry changed.
- No black/white sphere behavior changed.
- No Pulse, Auto-Rotate, 3-Axis, keyboard, mobile layout, or coordinate-entry
  behavior changed.
- No bot behavior changed.

FALLBACK
--------
Version 1.22.24 remains the immediate known-good fallback for the surrounding
Hide Green Cubes/raycasting work.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
This is Bundle Version 1.22.26. Active project-owned version references and
the HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters are synchronized to 1.22.26. Historical
log entries retain their original release versions.

Version 1.22.25

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Preserve the established raycasting behavior when green occupied cubes are
visible, while adding a separate raycasting mode for Hide Green Cubes. When
Hide Green Cubes is enabled, occupied green cells should no longer obstruct the
pointer ray, allowing the nearest legal move to be highlighted and selected
through the spaces where the green surfaces would otherwise be visible.

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.22.24
------------------------
The user confirmed Version 1.22.24 is working well and expects to use the game
frequently with Hide Green Cubes enabled. The user then reported that the
raycasting behavior was no longer quite right when the green cubes are hidden.
They approved a targeted change that keeps the established green-visible
raycasting behavior intact while adjusting only the green-hidden case.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.22.25 makes getFrontmostLegalHit() aware of the Hide Green Cubes
toggle.

When Hide Green Cubes is ON:
- Occupied green cells are ignored as raycasting obstacles on all supported
  board sizes.
- The nearest legal cell encountered along the pointer ray is returned.
- Hover highlighting and click selection therefore see through the absent green
  surfaces to legal cells behind them.
- Black and white spheres remain visible and their rendering is unchanged.

When Hide Green Cubes is OFF:
- The established raycasting behavior is preserved exactly.
- On 4×4×4, an occupied/green cell still blocks access to anything behind it.
- On 6×6×6 and 8×8×8, occupied cells still do not terminate the raycast,
  preserving the established interior legal-move access.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No game rules or legal-move calculation changed.
- No board geometry changed.
- No occupied green-surface geometry or appearance changed.
- No black/white sphere visibility or appearance changed.
- No blue Pulse behavior changed.
- No Hide Green Cubes toggle behavior changed.
- No keyboard controls changed.
- No camera, Auto-Rotate, 3-Axis, mobile layout, or coordinate-entry behavior
  changed.
- No bot behavior changed.

FALLBACK
--------
Version 1.22.24 remains the immediate known-good fallback if the green-hidden
raycasting adjustment produces unwanted interaction behavior.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
This is Bundle Version 1.22.25. Active project-owned version references and
the HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters are synchronized to 1.22.25. Historical
log entries retain their original release versions.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- Confirmed the targeted raycasting branch is limited to Hide Green Cubes ON.
- Confirmed the established green-visible branches remain in place.
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node --check.
- Confirmed active version references and HTML cache-busters are synchronized
  to 1.22.25.
- Confirmed all five required Bundle files are present and nonzero.
- Confirmed the final ZIP contains exactly index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity checked successfully.

NEXT STEP
---------
Deploy/test Version 1.22.25. If the green-hidden raycasting behavior is not

green-visible raycasting behavior.

2026-09-06 — Version 1.22.17 — Pulse label and keyboard shortcut
======================================================================

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Simplify the sidebar label for the legal-move pulse and add a keyboard
shortcut for toggling the same option.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.22.17 changes the sidebar label from “Pulse Legal Moves” to
“Pulse”.

The keyboard help now lists: 
- P: Pulse

Pressing P toggles the Pulse option when the user is not typing in an
editable control. The keyboard shortcut uses the same toggle behavior as
clicking the sidebar checkbox, so enabling/disabling Pulse preserves the
existing animation and legacy opacity-mode behavior.

All Version 1.22.16 behavior remains unchanged, including the 1-second
curved pulse, 10%–50% active opacity range, 30% timeout opacity, 60-second
inactivity timeout, and immediate stop when the browser tab is hidden.

======================================================================
2026-09-06 — Version 1.22.16 — Optional legacy legal-move opacity mode
======================================================================

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Add a sidebar toggle that allows the user to turn the blue legal-move pulse
off and restore the established game-progress opacity behavior used before
the pulse animation was introduced. The current pulse behavior remains the
default.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.22.16 adds a new sidebar toggle labeled “Pulse Legal Moves”. It is
checked by default, preserving the Version 1.22.15 behavior.

When “Pulse Legal Moves” is enabled:
- the synchronized curved blue opacity pulse remains active;
- the current 1-second cycle and 10%–50% active opacity range are unchanged;
- the 60-second inactivity timeout remains unchanged;
- the timeout opacity remains 30%;
- hiding the browser tab immediately stops the pulse, as established in
  Version 1.22.15;
- interaction with the game restarts the pulse after a timeout or tab switch.

When “Pulse Legal Moves” is disabled:
- the blue legal-move surfaces stop pulsing;
- their opacity returns to the established game-progress schedule from the
  pre-pulse implementation;
- the schedule starts at 0.10 opacity;
- on 6×6×6 and 8×8×8 boards, opacity remains at 0.10 through approximately
  the first quarter of the game, then follows the established 1.75-power
  ramp to 0.50 at the end of the game;
- the established 4×4×4 progression is also restored;
- opacity is recalculated as the game progresses, so the visual treatment
  changes naturally with the number of occupied positions.
\The legacy schedule is restored directly from the established
getLegalSurfaceOpacity() behavior; no new opacity curve was invented for this
mode.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
No changes were made to game rules, scoring, move validation, board geometry,
ray-casting, coordinate entry, bot behavior, Auto-Rotate, 3-Axis behavior,
hover handling, the current blue color, the current pulse timing, or the
browser-tab visibility behavior. Non-legal empty cubes remain completely
invisible.

--------
Version 1.22.15 remains the known-good fallback baseline.

Version 1.22.15 — Slightly Brighter Blue, 30% Timeout Opacity

Changes:
- Changed the blue legal-move surface color from 0x1236b3 to 0x1e46c0, making it slightly brighter while keeping the darker-blue direction requested by the user (approximately 3.5 on the user’s 1–10 blue scale).
- Reduced the steady opacity after the 60-second inactivity timeout from 35% to 30%.
- Preserved the 10%–50% active opacity range, 1-second pulse cycle, squared ease-in curve, and browser-tab visibility behavior.
- Preserved synchronized animation, activity restart behavior, hover behavior, Auto-Rotate behavior, 3-Axis behavior, and render-on-demand performance handling.
- No bot-engine changes.
- Based directly on Version 1.22.14.


============================================================
Version 1.22.14 — Mid-Dark Blue, 1-Second Pulse

Changes:
- Changed the blue legal-move surface color from 0x00008b to 0x1236b3, moving from the darkest blue toward a moderately brighter blue while remaining substantially darker than the earlier 0x2455c3 blue.
- Changed the pulse cycle from 1.25 seconds to 1.0 second.
- Preserved the 10%–50% active opacity range, 35% steady opacity after timeout, squared ease-in curve, 60-second inactivity timeout, and browser-tab visibility behavior.
- Preserved synchronized animation, activity restart behavior, hover behavior, Auto-Rotate behavior, 3-Axis behavior, and render-on-demand performance handling.
- No bot-engine changes.
- Based directly on Version 1.22.13.


============================================================
Version 1.22.13 — Darkest Blue, Lower Opacity, Faster Pulse, Tab Inactivity

Changes:
- Changed the blue legal-move surface color from 0x2455c3 to 0x00008b (dark blue) for a substantially darker cube appearance.
- Reduced the active opacity range from 10%–80% to 10%–50%.
- Reduced the steady opacity after the inactivity timeout from 65% to 35%.
- Changed the pulse cycle from 1.5 seconds to 1.25 seconds.
- Preserved the squared ease-in curve so the pulse spends more of its cycle toward the lower-opacity end.
- When the browser tab becomes hidden, the blue pulse now stops immediately and the legal-move surfaces switch to the 35% steady opacity, matching the timeout state.
- When the game tab becomes visible again, the blue pulse remains stopped until the user interacts with the game, at which point the normal activity restart behavior resumes.
- Preserved the existing 60-second inactivity timeout, synchronized animation, hover behavior, Auto-Rotate behavior, 3-Axis behavior, and render-on-demand performance handling.
- No bot-engine changes.
- Based directly on Version 1.22.12.


============================================================
Version 1.22.12 — Blue Legal-Move Pulse Curve, Faster Cycle, Darker Blue

Changes:
- Changed the blue legal-move surface opacity pulse from a linear ramp to a squared ease-in curve (phase²). This keeps the opacity toward the lower end for more of the cycle and rises more quickly near the high end.
- Changed the pulse cycle from 2.0 seconds to 1.5 seconds.
- Changed the legal-move surface blue from 0x2f80ed to 0x2455c3 for a darker, more royal-blue appearance.
- Preserved the existing 10% minimum, 80% maximum, and 65% steady opacity after the 60-second inactivity timeout.
- Preserved synchronized legal-surface animation, activity restart behavior, hover behavior, Auto-Rotate behavior, 3-Axis behavior, and render-on-demand performance handling.
- No bot-engine changes.
- Based directly on Version 1.22.11.



============================================================
Version 1.22.11 — Blue Opacity Timing / Hover Rendering Correction
============================================================

Purpose
-------
Adjust the blue legal-move opacity animation after Version 1.22.10 testing.
The animation was visibly restarting/interrupted when pointer movement caused
the render-on-demand system to stop the shared continuous-rendering loop while
the pointer was over the cube.

Behavior
--------
- Blue legal-move surfaces remain synchronized as one group.
- Opacity ramps linearly from 10% to 80% over two seconds, then restarts at 10%.
- The 60-second inactivity safeguard remains in place.
- User activity continues to reset the inactivity timer.
- When the animation stops after 60 seconds of inactivity, blue surfaces remain
  at the existing steady 65% opacity.
- Hovering over or moving across the cube no longer interrupts the blue opacity
  animation by stopping the shared continuous-rendering loop.
- Auto-Rotate still pauses over the cube; the render loop remains available for
  the blue opacity animation when it is active.

Scope
-----
- No game rules, board state, Bot Engine, coordinate system, or Auto-Rotate
  timing behavior changed.
- Based directly on Version 1.22.10.
- 1.21.2 remains the fallback baseline.


============================================================
Version 1.22.10 — Blue Legal-Surface Opacity Animation Correction
============================================================

Purpose
-------
Correct Version 1.22.9 after testing showed that the intended animated blue
legal-surface opacity behavior was not actually present in the delivered code.
This release implements the requested animation directly in the rendering loop.

Behavior
--------
- Blue legal-move surfaces animate together as one synchronized group.
- Opacity ramps linearly from 10% to 100% over one second, then restarts at 10%.
- The animation runs only while the user has been active.
- User activity resets the 60-second inactivity timer.
- After 60 seconds without activity, the animation stops.
- When stopped, blue legal-move surfaces remain at a steady 65% opacity.
- Subsequent user activity restarts the animation and its one-second cycle.
- When there are no legal moves, the blue animation is stopped and its timer is
  cleared.
- Continuous rendering is used only while the blue animation or another active
  visual animation requires it.

Scope
-----
- No game rules, board state, Bot Engine, coordinate system, Auto-Rotate behavior,
  or cube rotation speeds changed.
- Based directly on the confirmed Version 1.22.9 Bundle.
- 1.21.2 remains the fallback baseline.



============================================================
Version 1.22.9 — Blue Legal-Surface Opacity Animation
============================================================

Purpose
-------
Improve the visibility of the blue legal-move surfaces, particularly on
smaller mobile screens such as iPhone, while preserving the render-on-demand
performance strategy established in Version 1.21.2.

Behavior
--------
- Blue legal-move surfaces animate as one group, simultaneously.
- Their opacity ramps linearly from 10% to 100% over one second, then resets
  to 10% and repeats.
- The animation uses continuous rendering only while it is active.
- The same 60-second inactivity clock used by Auto-Rotate is used as the
  performance safeguard for the blue opacity animation.
- User activity resets the 60-second inactivity period.
- After 60 seconds without user activity, the blue opacity animation stops.
- A subsequent user interaction resumes the animation when legal blue surfaces
  are present.
- When no legal moves are present, the blue opacity animation stops.

Rationale
---------
The established game-progress opacity ramp improved visibility somewhat but
blue legal surfaces could still be difficult to see, especially on iPhone.
This release tests a more conspicuous animated-opacity treatment while limiting
its performance cost to periods of user activity.

Scope
-----
- No game rules, board state, move logic, Bot Engine, coordinate system,
  Auto-Rotate behavior, or cube rotation speeds changed.
- Based directly on the confirmed Version 1.22.8 Bundle.
- 1.21.2 remains the fallback baseline.


============================================================
Version 1.22.8 — Auto-Rotate Inactivity Timer Robustness
============================================================

Purpose
-------
Correct and harden the Version 1.22.7 Auto-Rotate inactivity timeout after
testing showed that normal game interaction could still allow Auto-Rotate to
turn off at the original 60-second interval.

Behavior
--------
- Auto-Rotate remains enabled while qualifying user activity continues.
- Each qualifying activity event resets the 60-second inactivity countdown.
- Activity is captured at the window level during event capture so game/UI
  handlers cannot prevent it from being recorded.
- Pointer down/move/up, wheel, touch start/move, keyboard, and click activity
  reset the inactivity timer.
- The timeout callback re-checks the most recent activity timestamp before
  disabling Auto-Rotate, preventing a stale timeout from ending active use.
- If no qualifying activity occurs for 60 seconds, Auto-Rotate still turns off
  automatically as the intended performance safeguard.

Scope
-----
- No game rules, board state, move logic, Bot Engine, coordinate system, or
  cube rotation behavior was changed.
- Based directly on the confirmed Version 1.22.7 Bundle.
- 1.21.2 remains the fallback baseline.

Release Integrity
-----------------
All active version references in the five-file Bundle were synchronized to
Version 1.22.8. Historical version references in this development log remain
unchanged.
============================================================
Version 1.22.7 — Auto-Rotate Inactivity Timeout
============================================================

Purpose
-------
Refine the 1.21.2 Auto-Rotate one-minute timeout so it does not interrupt an
active game. The 60-second period now measures user inactivity rather than
elapsed time since Auto-Rotate was enabled.

Behavior
--------
- Enabling Auto-Rotate starts a 60-second inactivity countdown.
- User activity while Auto-Rotate is enabled restarts the full 60-second
  countdown.
- Pointer interaction, pointer movement, mouse-wheel activity, and keyboard
  activity count as user activity.
- If there is no user activity for 60 seconds, Auto-Rotate turns off and its
  checkbox is cleared automatically.
- The existing cursor-over-cube pause/resume behavior remains unchanged.
- Manually turning Auto-Rotate off still cancels the timeout.

Rationale
---------
The fixed one-minute timeout introduced in Version 1.21.2 was useful for
preventing continuous rendering from being left running indefinitely, but it
could interrupt a user who was actively playing. Measuring inactivity preserves
the performance safeguard without stopping Auto-Rotate during active use.

Scope
-----
- No game rules, board state, move logic, Bot Engine, sequence handling,
  Undo/Redo, game clocks, or cube rotation speeds changed.
- Based directly on the confirmed Version 1.22.6 Bundle.
- 1.21.2 remains the fallback baseline.

Version 1.21.2 — Auto-Rotate One-Minute Timeout
====================================================

Purpose
-------
Performance-series follow-up to 1.21.1. Auto-Rotate now automatically stops
after one minute of being enabled, reducing the chance of leaving a continuous
3D render loop running indefinitely on a foreground device.

Behavior
--------
- Enabling Auto-Rotate starts a 60-second timeout.
- Spacebar and the Auto-Rotate checkbox both restart the full 60-second period.
- When the minute expires, Auto-Rotate is turned off and the checkbox is
  cleared automatically.
- The existing cursor-over-cube pause/resume behavior is unchanged.
- F flip behavior is unchanged.
- The timeout is cancelled when Auto-Rotate is manually turned off.

Scope
-----
- No game rules, board state, move logic, Bot Engine, sequence handling,
  Undo/Redo, game clocks, or visual design changes.
- Based directly on confirmed 1.21.1 source.
- 1.20.36 remains the untouched fallback baseline.

Testing
-------
Desktop Chrome/laptop testing required. iPhone/Safari should also be checked,
especially that Auto-Rotate stops at approximately one minute and can be
restarted immediately with Spacebar or the sidebar checkbox.

============================================================
Version 1.21.1 — Auto-Rotate Cursor Resume Repair
============================================================

Purpose
-------
Targeted follow-up to 1.21.0 performance work. No game rules, board state,
move logic, Bot Engine, sequence handling, Undo/Redo, or visual design changes.

Repair
------
- Fixed Auto-Rotate resuming while the pointer remains inside the 3D viewport.
- In 1.21.0, pointermove updated hoveringOverCube but did not start/stop the
  continuous render loop after that state changed. As a result, moving from the
  cube into blank viewport space could leave Auto-Rotate visually stopped until
  another event such as a click or leaving the viewport.
- pointermove now immediately resumes continuous rendering when Auto-Rotate is
  enabled and the cursor is no longer over visible cube geometry.
- pointermove also stops continuous rendering when the cursor reaches the cube,
  preserving the intended inspect/manipulate behavior.

Expected behavior
-----------------
Auto-Rotate ON:
  cursor over cube  -> rotation stops
  cursor off cube, still in viewport -> rotation resumes immediately
  cursor leaves viewport -> rotation resumes

Version integrity
-----------------
- Based directly on confirmed 1.21.0 source.
- This is a 1.21.x performance-series repair only.
- 1.20.36 remains the untouched fallback baseline.

Testing
-------
- Node syntax check required.
- Bundle integrity and exact five-file contents required.
- User should specifically retest Auto-Rotate cursor transitions on laptop and
  later on iPhone Safari.
======================================================================
2026-09-05 — Version 1.21.0 — Render-on-Demand Performance Foundation
======================================================================

USER REQUEST / START OF 1.21.x PERFORMANCE SERIES
-------------------------------------------------
The user confirmed that the project should remain a web application; native-app
development is not being pursued. After observing that a foreground iPhone Safari
session could leave the phone warm during extended idle time, the user agreed to
begin a dedicated performance series. The confirmed Version 1.20.36 Bundle remains
the untouched gameplay fallback.

The new 1.21.x series is dedicated to performance/efficiency work. Bot Engine
development remains deferred until this performance work is complete.

FIRST PERFORMANCE TARGET
-------------------------
Version 1.21.0 introduces a render-on-demand foundation. The goal is to avoid
continuously rendering the Three.js scene when no visual state is changing, while
preserving the existing game behavior and visual animations.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.21.0 replaces the unconditional startup animation loop with a controlled
rendering model:

  - The scene can be rendered on demand through requestRender().
  - ArcballControls change events request a render when the camera changes.
  - Board/piece/legal-surface/last-move-marker visual updates request a render.
  - Resize and keyboard view changes request a render.
  - Auto-Rotate uses continuous rendering only while it is actively rotating.
  - F flip uses continuous rendering only for the duration of its animation.
  - Pointer interaction temporarily enables continuous rendering so camera
    manipulation remains responsive, then returns to demand-driven rendering.
  - The existing Auto-Rotate pause over the cube is preserved; continuous
    rendering is stopped while the pointer is resting over the cube and resumes
    when Auto-Rotate is allowed to rotate again.
  - The Page Visibility API is used so a hidden tab/page stops the renderer
    completely. Returning to the foreground requests a fresh render and resumes
    an active visual animation if one is still applicable.

The game clock is now independent of the rendering architecture. Timed games
use a lightweight 100 ms JavaScript ticker for clock updates and timeout handling,
so stopping 3D rendering does not stop the game clock.

IMPORTANT PERFORMANCE NOTE
---------------------------
The animated last-move marker's dash offset remains part of the existing animation
loop when continuous rendering is active. Version 1.21.0 does not redesign that
visual behavior or add an inactivity timeout. Those are separate future performance
questions and should be evaluated only after this first render-on-demand build is
user-tested.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi game rules changed.
- No legal-move calculation changed.
- No PASS, Game Complete, Undo, or Redo behavior intentionally changed.
- No loaded-sequence behavior intentionally changed.
- No Bot Engine strategy or decision behavior changed.
- No board geometry, coordinate system, or piece appearance intentionally changed.
- No Auto-Rotate speed values changed.
- No F flip duration or easing values changed.
- No keyboard zoom behavior changed.
- No start-menu behavior changed.
- No inactivity timeout was added.
- No native app work was started.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- Version 1.20.36 was used as the exact starting source.
- Version 1.20.36 remains the untouched fallback.
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Active version references and cache-busters synchronized to 1.21.0.
- Final ZIP must contain exactly index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity must be checked before release.
- Live laptop/iPhone testing is required because this release changes rendering
  lifecycle behavior.

NEXT STEP
---------
User will test Version 1.21.0 carefully on laptop/desktop and iPhone. If the
render-on-demand foundation behaves identically to 1.20.36, later 1.21.x releases
can separately address inactivity-based Auto-Rotate shutdown and other background
performance refinements.

VERSION 1.20.7 — Loaded Sequence Clock Removal
======================================================================

USER REQUEST
------------
After a move sequence is loaded, the previous game's start-menu settings
should not carry over as though the loaded position were a newly started
clocked game. In particular, the game clock cannot meaningfully continue
because the elapsed time associated with the loaded sequence is unknown.

LOADED-SEQUENCE CLOCK BEHAVIOR
------------------------------
Version 1.20.7 removes the game clock whenever a move sequence is loaded.
The loaded position is treated as untimed regardless of the game-duration
setting used by the game that was active before loading.

The loaded-sequence player-choice dialog remains the only gameplay choice
presented after loading: Black (vs Bot), White (vs Bot), or Play as Both.
Once the choice is confirmed, the loaded position remains untimed and the
clock panel stays hidden.

STARTED GAMES
-------------
Starting a normal new game still restores the clock panel and uses the
selected game-duration behavior from the start screen. Showing the start
screen also hides the clock while no active game is being played.

======================================================================
VERSION 1.20.2 — Active-Game End Confirmation
======================================================================

USER TEST RESULT FROM VERSION 1.20.1
-------------------------------------
The user tested Version 1.20.1 and confirmed that the page now loads normally
and the previous start-menu/blank-cube initialization problem is resolved.
The Show 3-Axis repair is therefore ready for the next feature change.

USER REQUEST
------------
When the user attempts an action that would end or replace a game that is
currently in progress, Cube Reversi should ask for confirmation before
proceeding. The user specifically requested this behavior for the New Game
button and wants the same protection used for other destructive game-ending
actions.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.20.2 adds a reusable in-game confirmation dialog for actions that
would end the active game. A new game-in-progress state tracks whether a game
has actually started and is still active, including games that have no moves
yet and games paused at a PASS message. Completed, resigned, or timed-out
games are not treated as active for this confirmation.

The New Game button now shows a small confirmation dialog when a game is
active. Choosing Continue proceeds to the existing start-game screen; choosing
Cancel leaves the current game untouched.

The same confirmation protection is also applied to Load Sequence, because
loading a move sequence replaces the current game state. The existing browser
confirmation for loading a sequence has been replaced by the shared in-game
dialog. If the user cancels the sequence-loading dialog itself, the current
game remains intact.

Resign continues to use its existing dedicated resignation confirmation, since
resignation has its own specific wording and action.

The game-in-progress state is included in game snapshots so Undo/Redo and
sequence-load rejection can restore the correct active/completed state.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules or legal-move generation changed.
- No bot evaluation criteria or strategy changed.
- No coordinate labels, axis assignments, or board-index mappings changed.
- No board geometry or piece appearance changed.
- No Show 3-Axis behavior changed beyond the already-tested 1.20.1 repair.
- No Auto-Rotate behavior changed.

======================================================================
VERSION 1.20.1 — Show 3-Axis Initialization Crash Repair
======================================================================

USER TESTING OBSERVATION FROM VERSION 1.20.0
----------------------------------------------
After loading the Version 1.20.0 page, the start menu did not appear and the
game area was blank with no cube rendered. This was a release-blocking
initialization failure discovered immediately during user testing.

ISSUE FOUND
-----------
The 1.20.0 Show 3-Axis repair introduced update3AxisVisibility() and called
it from updateOrientationMarkerVisibility(). The latter was invoked before
the const orientationEdges declaration had been initialized. Because
orientationEdges is a const in the JavaScript temporal dead zone at that
point, page initialization threw a ReferenceError before the start screen and
board could be initialized.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.20.1 moves the first visibility update until after orientationEdges
has been created and added to the board group. The shared visibility path is
then safe to use for the Show 3-Axis toggle and later viewport updates.
The axis-line depth settings from 1.20.0 are retained.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules or legal-move generation changed.
- No bot evaluation criteria or strategy changed.
- No coordinate labels, axis assignments, or board-index mappings changed.
- No board geometry or piece placement changed.
- No Auto-Rotate behavior changed.
- No Undo/Redo or pass-state behavior changed.
- No intended change was made to the Show 3-Axis feature itself beyond fixing
  its initialization order.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- Version 1.20.0 was used as the starting Bundle.
- User immediately reported the blank-page/start-screen failure.
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Active version/cache-buster references synchronized to 1.20.1.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity verified.

======================================================================
VERSION 1.20.0 — Show 3-Axis Persistence / Visibility Repair
======================================================================

USER TESTING OBSERVATION FROM VERSION 1.19.3
----------------------------------------------
The user enabled the Show 3-Axis toggle, played a game, then started a new
game. The Show 3-Axis toggle remained enabled, but the three-axis guide was
no longer visibly present after the new game began.

ISSUE FOUND
-----------
The Show 3-Axis state was stored in the checkbox, but the main three-axis
lines were not managed through one centralized visibility update. In addition,
the axis lines used normal depth testing, so after a new-game view reset the
lines could become visually buried by the board's transparent surfaces. The
result was that the control could remain checked while the guide appeared to
have disappeared.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.20.0 introduces a centralized update3AxisVisibility() path tied to
the existing Show 3-Axis state. The guide visibility is reapplied whenever the
orientation-marker visibility is updated, including viewport changes and other
view/state refreshes. On desktop it follows the checkbox state; on mobile it
remains hidden consistently with the existing responsive behavior.

The three axis-line materials now disable depth testing and depth writing so
the orientation guide remains visibly in front of the transparent board
surfaces. This preserves the guide as an orientation aid instead of allowing
the board geometry to visually occlude it after a reset/new game.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules or legal-move generation changed.
- No bot evaluation criteria or strategy changed.
- No coordinate labels, axis assignments, or board-index mappings changed.
- No board geometry or piece placement changed.
- No Auto-Rotate behavior changed.
- No Undo/Redo or pass-state behavior changed.
- The Show 3-Axis checkbox remains the user control for the feature.

IMPORTANT COORDINATE/BOT DEPENDENCY NOTE
-----------------------------------------
The previously recorded coordinate-system dependency remains in force. This
release changes only the visibility/rendering of the orientation guide and
does not change the underlying coordinate system or numeric board indices used
by bot position analysis.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- Version 1.19.3 was used as the starting Bundle.
- User reported the Show 3-Axis persistence/visibility problem during live
  testing.
- JavaScript syntax will be checked with Node before release.
- All five required Bundle files must be present and nonzero.
- Active version/cache-buster references will be audited and synchronized to
  1.20.0.
- Final ZIP must contain exactly index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.

======================================================================
2026-09-05 — DEVELOPMENT NOTE — Coordinate System / Bot Dependency
======================================================================

The bot does not rank moves by comparing the human-readable coordinate
strings directly. Reversi.js represents each candidate move internally as
its three numeric board indices [x, y, z], and the bot receives those move
arrays along with the calculated evaluation values. The current corner
analysis also operates directly on those numeric coordinates/indices.

IMPORTANT FUTURE DEPENDENCY
---------------------------
Any future change to the Cube Reversi coordinate system — including changes
to axis assignment, axis direction, coordinate labels, index-to-coordinate
mapping, or the meaning of a coordinate such as A-1-W — may also change how
the bot evaluates and ranks positions if the underlying board-index
interpretation changes.

Therefore, coordinate-system changes must be treated as potentially
bot-strategy-affecting changes, not merely visual/UI changes. After any
coordinate-system change, the bot's corner detection, surface detection,
and any future position-based evaluation should be reviewed and retested.

This note does not itself change game behavior or bot behavior.

======================================================================
VERSION 1.19.2 — Pass-State Repair and Auto-Rotate Timing
======================================================================

PURPOSE
-------
Repair a pass-state/input problem discovered during Version 1.19.1 testing
and make Auto-Rotate timing independent of animation frame rate.

USER TESTING OBSERVATION FROM 1.19.1
-------------------------------------
User tested 4×4×4 in Bot Mode and reported that late in the game, after Black
passed, the pass message appeared. After dismissing it, White could highlight
a legal move but could not click/select it. The user also observed that the
pass turn did not show 0 Available moves. The user additionally reported that
Auto-Rotate felt slower, without being certain.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
For a human-visible pass, the passing player is now made the active player while
the pass message is displayed. This allows updateStatus() to correctly show
0 Available moves. Dismissing the pass message now advances to the other player
and immediately refreshes legal moves/status and schedules a bot turn if needed.
Bot-mode passes remain automatic and non-blocking.

Auto-Rotate now uses elapsed animation time rather than a fixed increment per
frame. The rates are calibrated to the established 60fps increments, so the
apparent rotation speed remains consistent when frame rate changes. This is
particularly useful on larger boards where bot evaluation can temporarily use
more CPU.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules or legal-move generation changed.
- No bot evaluation criteria changed.
- No corner strategy changed.
- No board geometry or rendering design changed.
- Existing Auto-Rotate rates are preserved at the 60fps reference speed.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- Version 1.19.1 was used as the starting Bundle.
- User reported the late-game pass/input problem and possible Auto-Rotate
  slowdown during live testing.
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Active version/cache-buster audit completed.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.

-------

on an 8×8×8 board. The bot could have no legal move after the human played,

of automatically passing the bot turn back to the human.

USER TESTING OBSERVATION FROM 1.18.12
-------------------------------------
User tested 1.18.12 on an 8×8×8 board and reported the sequence:
F-6-X, F-3-X, F-4-X, F-4-W, E-3-W, F-6-U. The user was playing White and
reported that after White played F-6-U, Black did not make a move and the user
could not make another move either. The user did not press Undo or Redo.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.18.13 repairs automatic Bot-mode passes. When the human completes a
move and the bot has no legal move while the human still has legal moves, the
controller now records the PASS and immediately keeps the turn with the human.
The transient pass overlay is suppressed in this case so it cannot block input.

This fixes a distinct issue from the 1.18.12 Undo repair. No bot evaluation
criteria, board geometry, or legal-move rules were changed.

snapshot correctly represented the board before the bot move but also restored
the bot as currentPlayer, which blocked the human player from selecting the
next move.

USER TESTING OBSERVATION FROM 1.18.11
-------------------------------------
User tested 1.18.11 on an 8×8×8 board. The bot appeared to behave normally and
showed the intended corner/outer-position behavior. During a later sequence,
legal move cubes could be hovered and highlighted but could not be selected.
The user reported that pressing Undo temporarily allowed play as Black and
then prevented play as White, indicating that the current-player state had
become inconsistent with the human player's configured color.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.18.12 repairs Bot-mode Undo without changing game rules or bot
strategy. After restoring the snapshot for an undone bot move, if the restored
currentPlayer is the bot's color, the controller now returns control to the
humanPlayer while preserving the restored board position. This matches the
intended meaning of undoing the bot's most recent move: the human can continue
from the position immediately after their own preceding move.

Redo behavior remains unchanged: restoring the saved post-move snapshot returns
the game to the state that existed before Undo, including the correct current
player. Undoing a human move still restores the human's turn normally.

======================================================================
2026-09-04 — Version 1.18.10 — Larger-Board Corner Awareness
======================================================================

USER TEST FEEDBACK FROM VERSION 1.18.9
--------------------------------------
User tested Version 1.18.9 on an 8×8×8 board and reported that the bot appeared
to be deliberately establishing edge positions and then expanding along an
edge once it gained a foothold. The user also described an important strategic
hypothesis: on larger boards, corners should eventually be valuable, but moves
immediately adjacent to a corner can be dangerous because they may give the
opponent access to that corner. The user specifically noted that this strategy
should vary with board size.

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Begin testing corner awareness on the larger supported boards without
assuming that conventional 2D Othello corner rules transfer perfectly to 3D.
Keep the experiment small and isolated so the effect can be observed.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.18.10 adds a corner-position tiebreaker for 6×6×6 and 8×8×8 only.
For each candidate move:

    true corner                         = +2
    immediately adjacent to a corner   = -1
    all other positions                 = 0

A candidate's corner value is considered only after the existing mobility and
opponent-surface-access criteria have tied. Thus corner awareness cannot
override a stronger mobility result in this experiment. A 4×4×4 game disables
this factor because the board is much more compact and the current experiment
is specifically intended to test the larger-board hypothesis.

The bot's current decision order is now:

    1. Minimize opponent legal moves.
    2. Maximize the bot's own legal moves.
    3. Minimize opponent legal moves on the outer surface.
    4. On 6×6×6 and 8×8×8, prefer corner positions and avoid positions
       immediately adjacent to corners.
    5. Maximize immediate pieces flipped.
    6. Randomly choose among remaining ties.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules or board geometry changed.
- No look-ahead, minimax, or difficulty levels added.
- No general weighted scoring system introduced yet.
- 4×4×4 behavior is not affected by the new corner factor.
- Reversi.js remains authoritative for board state, rules, legal moves, and
  candidate-position analysis.
- ReversiBot.js remains responsible for choosing among supplied candidates.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- Version 1.18.9 was used as the starting Bundle.
- User confirmed Version 1.18.9 worked and reported apparent edge establishment
  and edge expansion behavior on 8×8×8.
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Active version/cache-buster audit completed.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity checked successfully.
- Live browser/iPhone testing was not performed in this environment.

NEXT STEP
---------
User will test Version 1.18.10, particularly on 8×8×8, to see whether the bot
shows useful corner-seeking behavior while avoiding immediate corner-adjacent
moves when the stronger mobility criteria do not distinguish the choices.

2026-09-04 — Version 1.18.9 — Opponent Surface Access
======================================================================

USER TEST FEEDBACK FROM VERSION 1.18.7 / 1.18.8
------------------------------------------------
User tested Version 1.18.7 on an 8×8×8 board and reported that, despite many
outer moves being available, the bot often appeared to remain near the core.
User also confirmed that Version 1.18.8 corrected the stale browser tab-title
version and that the active version references now look correct.

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Test a different interpretation of the observed core-versus-surface behavior:
rather than rewarding the bot for taking surface positions, measure whether a
move can deny the opponent access to the outer surface. This is a controlled
tiebreaker experiment and does not assume that surface squares are inherently
good or bad.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.18.9 adds opponentSurfaceMoves to each candidate position. This is the
number of the opponent's legal moves that lie on any outer face, edge, or corner.
The bot's decision order is now:

    1. Minimize opponent legal moves.
    2. Maximize the bot's own legal moves.
    3. Minimize opponent legal moves on the outer surface.
    4. Maximize immediate pieces flipped.
    5. Randomly choose among remaining ties.

This replaces the previous surface-position tiebreaker. The bot is no longer
explicitly rewarded for making its own move farther toward the surface. Instead,
this release tests whether denying surface access to the opponent is useful.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules or board geometry changed.
- No look-ahead, minimax, or difficulty levels added.
- No weighted scoring system introduced yet.
- Reversi.js remains authoritative for board state, legal moves, and candidate
  analysis.
- ReversiBot.js remains responsible for choosing among supplied candidates.

2026-09-04 — Version 1.18.6 — Add Own Mobility as a Secondary Criterion
==========================================================================

USER TEST RESULT / NEXT STEP
----------------------------
The user tested Version 1.18.5 and reported that it is working normally. The
user tested on an 8×8×8 board and observed that the bot appears somewhat more
strategic. The user also noticed that when an edge or outer-layer move becomes
available, the bot sometimes appears to prefer it. This observation is useful
but is not yet being treated as proof of a positional preference; it may be a
consequence of the mobility-based evaluation.

The user also confirmed that the development log should continue to be kept
up to date throughout the bot-engine series.

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Add the bot's own resulting mobility as a secondary evaluation factor. The
experiment tests whether, after restricting the opponent, it is useful for
the bot to preserve more legal choices for itself.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.18.6 keeps opponent mobility as the primary criterion. Among moves
that leave the opponent with the same minimum number of legal moves, the bot
now prefers the move that leaves the bot itself with the greatest number of
legal moves. Among those remaining ties, it prefers the move that flips the
most pieces. Complete ties remain random.

EVALUATION ORDER IN 1.18.6
--------------------------
    1. Minimize opponent legal moves.
    2. Maximize own legal moves among opponent-mobility ties.
    3. Maximize immediate flips among complete mobility ties.
    4. Random choice among complete ties.

This is intentionally a lexicographic experiment rather than a weighted score.
No arbitrary numerical weights were introduced.

ARCHITECTURE
------------
Reversi.js remains authoritative for game state, rules, legal-move generation,
rendering, turn management, candidate analysis, and actually committing moves.
ReversiBot.js remains responsible for choosing among the supplied candidates.
Reversi.js now supplies both opponent-mobility and own-mobility results for each
candidate move.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules or capture directions changed.
- No board geometry, piece appearance, or rendering behavior changed.
- No difficulty levels were added.
- No look-ahead or minimax/search was added.
- No positional edge/corner weighting was added.
- No game-clock behavior changed.
- No Undo/Redo behavior changed.
- No iPhone interaction behavior changed.
- No Two Player behavior changed.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
Active project-owned version references were synchronized to 1.18.6 in
index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js, and ReversiBot.js. Historical version
references in Reversi_Log.txt and older implementation comments remain unchanged.

2026-09-04 — Version 1.18.5 — Make Opponent Mobility the Primary Criterion
======================================================================

USER TEST RESULT / NEXT STEP
----------------------------
The user tested Version 1.18.4 and reported that it is working normally. The
user observed that the bot appears somewhat more strategic, although the effect
is still difficult to judge. The user also noted an important strategic
observation from playing Cube Reversi: in standard Othello/Reversi, limiting
the opponent's available moves early in the game is generally valuable and can
mean taking fewer pieces at first. The user suspects this principle may carry
over to the 3D game.

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Test opponent mobility as the bot's primary evaluation criterion rather than
using it only as a tie-breaker behind immediate piece gain. This is a focused
experiment to see whether the early-game principle of restricting the
opponent's choices produces more sensible play in Cube Reversi.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.18.5 changes only the ordering of the existing two evaluation
factors. Reversi.js continues to calculate authoritative flip counts and the
number of legal moves available to the opponent after each candidate move.
ReversiBot.js now first selects the candidate moves that leave the opponent
with the fewest legal moves. Among those tied candidates, it prefers the move
that flips the most pieces. If both values are still tied, the bot chooses
randomly among the remaining candidates.

No new weighting, look-ahead, search, or positional assumptions were added.
This release is intended as a clean experiment: mobility is now primary,
immediate piece gain is secondary.

ARCHITECTURE
------------
Reversi.js remains authoritative for game state, rules, legal-move generation,
rendering, turn management, candidate analysis, and actually committing moves.
ReversiBot.js remains responsible for choosing among the supplied candidates.

Decision flow:

    Reversi.js → candidate analysis → ReversiBot.js → selected move → Reversi.js

EVALUATION ORDER IN 1.18.5
--------------------------
    1. Minimize opponent legal moves.
    2. Maximize immediate flips among mobility ties.
    3. Random choice among complete ties.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules or capture directions changed.
- No board geometry, piece appearance, or rendering behavior changed.
- No difficulty levels were added.
- No look-ahead or minimax/search was added.
- No arbitrary numerical weights were added.
- No game-clock behavior changed.
- No Undo/Redo behavior changed.
- No iPhone interaction behavior changed.
- No Two Player behavior changed.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
Active project-owned version references were synchronized to 1.18.5 in
index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js, and ReversiBot.js. Historical version
references in Reversi_Log.txt and older implementation comments remain unchanged.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- Version 1.18.4 was used as the starting Bundle.

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Add a second measurable bot evaluation factor without introducing arbitrary
weighting or look-ahead: when candidate moves produce the same immediate piece
gain, prefer the move that leaves the opponent with fewer legal moves.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.18.4 keeps immediate piece gain as the primary evaluation criterion.
For each legal bot move, Reversi.js now calculates the resulting number of
legal moves available to the opponent and supplies that authoritative value to
ReversiBot.js. ReversiBot.js first selects the candidates with the highest
immediate flip count. Among those tied candidates, it selects the ones that
leave the opponent with the fewest legal moves. If those values are still tied,
the bot chooses randomly among the remaining candidates.

This is intentionally a lexicographic decision rather than a weighted formula.
No arbitrary numerical balance between piece gain and mobility has been
introduced yet.

ARCHITECTURE
------------
Reversi.js remains authoritative for game state, rules, legal-move generation,
rendering, turn management, and actually committing moves. ReversiBot.js remains
responsible for deciding which supplied candidate move to return. The new
opponent-mobility value is calculated by Reversi.js because it depends on the
authoritative game rules and board state.

Decision flow:

    Reversi.js → candidate move analysis → ReversiBot.js → selected move → Reversi.js

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules or capture directions changed.
- No board geometry, piece appearance, or rendering behavior changed.
- No difficulty levels were added.
- No look-ahead or minimax/search was added.
- No arbitrary evaluation weights were added.
- No game-clock behavior changed.
- No Undo/Redo behavior changed.
- No iPhone interaction behavior changed.
- No Two Player behavior changed.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
Active project-owned version references were synchronized to 1.18.4 in
index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js, and ReversiBot.js. Historical version
references in Reversi_Log.txt and older implementation comments remain unchanged.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- Version 1.18.3 was used as the starting Bundle.
- User tested Version 1.18.3 and reported normal operation.
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Active version/cache-buster audit completed.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity checked successfully.
- Live browser/iPhone testing was not performed in this environment.

NEXT STEP
---------
User will test Version 1.18.4 and observe whether the bot's choices now show
a useful preference for restricting the opponent's available moves. Further
evaluation factors should remain incremental and should be informed by the
user's observations from actual 3D play.

2026-09-04 — Version 1.18.3 — First Bot Evaluation Factor: Immediate Piece Gain
======================================================================

USER TEST RESULT
----------------
The user tested Version 1.18.2 and confirmed that it is working normally.
The user is ready to begin the first actual bot-strategy experiment.

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Begin bot strategy incrementally with one simple, measurable evaluation
factor rather than adding a full strategic system. The first factor is
immediate piece gain: prefer a legal move that flips more opponent pieces
on the current move.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.18.3 changes the data supplied by Reversi.js when it asks
ReversiBot.js to choose a move. For every legal move, Reversi.js calculates
the authoritative number of pieces that move would flip and supplies that
number to the bot engine.

ReversiBot.js now evaluates those supplied flip counts and selects a move
with the highest immediate flip count. If multiple legal moves have the
same highest count, the bot chooses randomly among those tied moves.

This is deliberately a very small first strategy step. It does not attempt
to understand corners, edges, mobility, future positions, or 3D-specific
strategic value yet.

ARCHITECTURE
------------
Reversi.js remains authoritative for game state, rules, legal-move
generation, flip calculation, rendering, turn management, and committing
moves. ReversiBot.js owns the decision about which supplied move to return.

Current path:

    Reversi.js → calculate legal moves/flip counts → chooseMove() →
    ReversiBot.js → selected move → Reversi.js commits the move

The bot does not mutate the board and does not independently reimplement
Reversi's capture rules.

STRATEGY NOTE
-------------
Immediate piece gain is being introduced as an experimental baseline, not
as an assertion that taking the most pieces is generally the best strategy.
The purpose is to establish a simple measurable behavior that can be tested
against actual Cube Reversi play. Future evaluation factors should be added
individually so their effects can be observed and compared.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules or legal-move calculation changed.
- No board geometry, piece appearance, or rendering behavior changed.
- No game-clock behavior changed.
- No Undo/Redo behavior changed.
- No iPhone interaction behavior changed.
- No Two Player behavior changed.
- No difficulty levels or look-ahead/minimax search were added.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
Active project-owned version references were synchronized to 1.18.3 in
index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js, and ReversiBot.js. Historical version
references in Reversi_Log.txt remain unchanged.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- Version 1.18.2 was used as the starting Bundle.
- User tested Version 1.18.2 and confirmed expected behavior.
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Active version/cache-buster audit completed.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity checked successfully.
- Live browser/iPhone testing was not performed in this environment.

NEXT STEP
---------
User will test the immediate-piece-gain bot and observe whether the new
behavior makes intuitive sense in actual games. The next strategy factor
should be chosen only after this baseline has been tested.

2026-09-04 — Version 1.18.2 — Prepare Position-Aware Bot Interface
======================================================================

----------------

tested an 8×8×8 board. The user reported that everything is working normally.

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------

context from Reversi.js, without introducing strategy yet. The purpose is to
create a clean path for future move evaluation while keeping Reversi.js as
the authoritative source of game state and rules.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.18.2 extends chooseMove() in ReversiBot.js to accept an optional
context object. Reversi.js now supplies the current board size and current
player when requesting the bot's decision. The bot intentionally ignores
that context in this release and continues selecting uniformly at random
from the supplied legal moves.

This is groundwork only. Bot playing behavior is unchanged.

ARCHITECTURE
------------
Reversi.js remains responsible for authoritative game state, rules, legal
move generation, rendering, turn management, and committing moves.
ReversiBot.js remains responsible for deciding which supplied move to return.
The planned future direction is for Reversi.js to supply whatever
authoritative position information the bot needs, while ReversiBot.js owns
the evaluation and selection logic.

Current path:

    Reversi.js → chooseMove(moves, context) → ReversiBot.js → selected move
             → Reversi.js commits the move

2026-09-04 — Version 1.18.0 — Separate Bot Engine from game controller
======================================================================

USER REQUEST / START OF 1.18.x SERIES
--------------------------------------
The user approved the planned Bot Opponent architecture after completing the
1.17.x mobile/header cleanup series. From this point forward, the Bundle contains
five files rather than four:

    - index.html
    - Reversi.css
    - Reversi.js
    - ReversiBot.js
    - Reversi_Log.txt

Version matching and release-integrity rules now apply to all five active Bundle
files and their active version/cache-busting references.

The user and assistant agreed that Reversi.js should remain the authoritative
game controller while a separate ReversiBot.js should contain the bot's
decision-making engine. The engine should not directly manipulate game state.

PLANNED BOT DEVELOPMENT DIRECTION
-----------------------------------
The 1.18.x series will develop the bot incrementally:

1. Separate the existing random-move decision into ReversiBot.js without
   changing its behavior.
2. Add basic tunable move evaluation.
3. Test and tune 3D-specific strategic factors.
4. Add difficulty as a parameter to the same underlying bot framework.
5. Add look-ahead/search after the evaluation system is understood and tested.

The intended architecture is: Reversi.js determines when the bot must move and
provides the legal moves; ReversiBot.js chooses a move; Reversi.js remains the
only layer that commits the move through the authoritative game path.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.18.0 creates ReversiBot.js and moves the existing random legal-move
selection into its exported chooseRandomMove() function. Reversi.js now imports
that function and uses it during scheduleBotTurn(). The selected move is still
committed through the existing playMove(..., true) path.

The bot's behavior is therefore intentionally unchanged: it still selects one
move uniformly at random from the currently available legal moves. No strategy,
position evaluation, difficulty system, look-ahead, minimax, or other AI logic
has been added in this release.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules or legal-move calculation changed.
- No board geometry, piece appearance, or rendering behavior changed.
- No Bot strategy beyond the existing random legal-move selection changed.
- No difficulty levels or search were added.
- No game-clock behavior changed.
- No Undo/Redo behavior changed.
- No iPhone interaction behavior changed.
- No Two Player behavior changed.
- No 6×6×6 or 8×8×8 behavior changed.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
This is Bundle Version 1.18.0. Active project-owned version references and
HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters are synchronized to 1.18.0. ReversiBot.js is
also explicitly versioned 1.18.0. Historical log entries retain their original
release versions.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- Confirmed ReversiBot.js is included as the fifth Bundle file.
- Confirmed Reversi.js imports chooseRandomMove() from ReversiBot.js.
- Confirmed scheduleBotTurn() still supplies the current legal-move list and
  commits the selected move through playMove(..., true).
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node for Reversi.js and ReversiBot.js.
- Confirmed all five required Bundle files are present and nonzero.
- Confirmed active version references and cache-busters are synchronized to
  1.18.0 across the five-file Bundle.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly: index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity verified.
- Live browser/laptop and iPhone testing was not performed in this environment.

FALLBACK
--------
Version 1.17.11 remains the known-good 1.17.x baseline. It should remain
unchanged in the user's archive.

NEXT STEP
---------
User will test that Bot Opponent still behaves like the prior random bot. Once
that plumbing is confirmed, the next Bot-engine change can begin adding the first
tunable evaluation factors.

2026-09-04 — Version 1.17.11 — Prevent header text selection and iPhone pull-to-refresh
======================================================================

USER FEEDBACK / REQUEST
-----------------------
The user confirmed Version 1.17.10 produced the intended mobile header layout.
Before beginning Bot Opponent development, the user reported two small iPhone
interaction problems:

1. Header text was occasionally being selected while the user was trying to
   tap or drag the cube.
2. The iPhone browser pull-down gesture could accidentally trigger page refresh.

The requested change is to make the header text unselectable and prevent
accidental pull-to-refresh behavior without interfering with normal cube
interaction.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.17.11 makes the entire game header (`.topbar` and its descendants)
non-selectable using both `user-select: none` and the WebKit-prefixed form,
and disables the iPhone touch callout for that header area. This prevents
accidental text selection during game interaction.

The page body now also uses `overscroll-behavior-y: none` to discourage browser
overscroll/refresh behavior. In Reversi.js, an additional touch guard detects a
downward touch gesture that begins outside the 3D game surface while the page
is already at the top and calls `preventDefault()` on that gesture. The guard
uses a non-passive `touchmove` listener so the browser can honor the prevention.

The 3D game surface is deliberately excluded from that document-level guard.
The existing `#scene canvas { touch-action: none; }` behavior remains responsible
for the cube's touch/drag interaction, so the new refresh protection does not
interfere with dragging the cube.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No game rules, move validation, scoring, or board geometry changed.
- No Bot strategy or behavior changed.
- No clocks or clock behavior changed.
- No Undo/Redo behavior changed.
- No header layout or positioning changed.
- No cube materials, colors, opacity, lighting, or rendering behavior changed.
- No Auto-Rotate behavior changed.
- No six- or eight-board-size behavior changed.
- No intentional changes were made to laptop/desktop appearance.

IPHONE INTERACTION NOTE
-----------------------
The pull-to-refresh prevention is implemented as a defensive combination of
CSS overscroll control and a targeted JavaScript touch guard. Because browser
vendors retain control over some system-level gestures, live iPhone testing is
still required to confirm the behavior on the user's device.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING WORKFLOW
---------------------------------
This is Bundle Version 1.17.11. All active release/version references in
index.html, Reversi.css, and Reversi.js were audited and synchronized to
1.17.11. The HTML title, visible version text, CSS/JavaScript release markers,
and both CSS/JavaScript cache-busters must match before release. Historical
version references in the development log and older CSS/JavaScript section
comments remain preserved as historical records. Version synchronization
remains a release-blocking check.

TESTING / RELEASE CHECKS
------------------------
- Active version references audited and synchronized to 1.17.11.
- Confirmed header selection-prevention rules are present.
- Confirmed pull-to-refresh protection is present in both CSS and JavaScript.
- Confirmed the document touch guard excludes the 3D game surface.
- JavaScript syntax check passed with Node.
- All four required Bundle files are present and nonzero.
- Final ZIP contains exactly index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js, and
  Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity verified.
- Live browser/iPhone testing was not performed in this environment.

FALLBACK
--------
- 1.17.10 remains the immediate prior confirmed header-layout build.
- 1.15.12 remains the established pre-1.16 known-good fallback.
