2026-09-03 — Version 1.16.6 — Empty Spaces Remaining indicator
======================================================================

USER FEEDBACK / REQUEST
-----------------------
The user requested a new indicator showing how many empty board spaces remain
during the game. The indicator should be labeled exactly:

    Empty Spaces Remaining:

and placed in the upper-right status area near the score. The user noted that
in rare cases the value may not reach 0 when remaining spaces are unavailable
to both colors and both players must pass; this is acceptable because the
indicator is intended as a useful measure of overall game progress.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.16.6 adds an `emptySpacesLabel` to the upper-right status panel,
directly below the score, initialized to the correct count for the default
4×4×4 opening position. `updateStatus()` now counts board cells whose value
is EMPTY and updates the label after every status refresh:

    Empty Spaces Remaining: <count>

The count therefore adapts automatically to 4×4×4, 6×6×6, and 8×8×8 boards
and reaches 0 when all board positions are occupied. It is intentionally based
on physical empty spaces, not on whether those spaces are currently playable.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No game rules or move validation changed.
- No legal-move selection or raycasting changed.
- No cube colors or opacity calculations changed.
- No black game-scene backdrop changed.
- No Auto-Rotate values or behavior changed.
- No board geometry or coordinate behavior changed.
- No clocks, Undo/Redo, Bot mode, controls, or start-menu behavior changed.
- No iPhone-specific interaction behavior changed.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
This is Bundle Version 1.16.6. Active project-owned version references and
the HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters are synchronized to 1.16.6 in the Bundle.
Historical log entries retain their original release versions.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- Confirmed Version 1.16.5 was used as the starting point.
- Confirmed the new indicator is labeled exactly `Empty Spaces Remaining:`.
- Confirmed the indicator is positioned in the upper-right status panel near
  the score.
- Confirmed the count is calculated from EMPTY board cells and updates through
  the existing `updateStatus()` path.
- Confirmed the indicator automatically reflects the selected board size.
- Confirmed active version references and CSS/JavaScript cache-busters are
  synchronized to 1.16.6.
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All four required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly: index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity checked successfully.

======================================================================
2026-09-03 — Version 1.15.7 — Allow interior legal-move access on larger boards
======================================================================

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Preserve the established Version 1.9.12/4×4×4 anti-raycast-through-green behavior,
while changing raycasting for the 6×6×6 and 8×8×8 boards so legal moves stranded
inside a visually completed outer surface remain highlightable and selectable.

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.15.6
------------------------
The user approved the current larger-board opacity schedule. The user then identified
a larger-board interaction problem: after the visible outer surface becomes populated,
some legal cubes can remain inside the board. Because the existing raycasting rule
blocks rays at occupied green cubes, those interior legal cubes could neither be
highlighted nor clicked. This can prevent completion of a larger-board game.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
The existing getFrontmostLegalHit() logic intentionally treats an occupied cube as
a raycasting obstacle. That behavior is correct and desirable for the 4×4×4 board,
where it prevents the player from clicking through a green cube to a legal cube
behind it. On the larger boards, however, the same occlusion rule can hide a legal
move that is legitimately located deeper inside the 3D board after the visible outer
surface has filled in. Because hover highlighting uses the same raycast selection
function, the stranded move also cannot be revealed by the yellow highlight or its
coordinate readout.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.15.7 makes getFrontmostLegalHit() board-size-aware.

For 4×4×4:
- The existing behavior is preserved exactly.
- Empty non-legal cells are skipped.
- The first legal cell can be selected.
- An occupied/green cell blocks access to anything behind it.
- This preserves the established anti-click-through-green behavior.

For 6×6×6 and 8×8×8:
- Empty non-legal cells are skipped as before.
- Occupied/green cells no longer terminate the raycast search.
- The nearest legal cell encountered along the pointer ray is returned.
- The same behavior is used by hover highlighting and click selection, so interior
  legal cubes can become yellow/highlighted and their coordinates can be revealed.

This is intentionally limited to raycasting/interaction. No legal-move calculation,
board geometry, game rules, scoring, opacity schedule, or visual surface construction
was changed.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- 4×4×4 raycasting/anti-click-through-green behavior remains unchanged.
- No game rules or legal-move calculation changed.
- No board-size definitions or coordinate notation changed.
- No blue legal-surface opacity behavior changed; the Version 1.15.6 schedule is
  preserved, including the approximately 25% ramp start on larger boards.
- No green occupied-surface appearance changed.
- No yellow highlight geometry changed.
- No bot behavior changed.
- No clock behavior changed.
- No Undo/Redo behavior changed.
- No camera, Arcball, mobile, or layout behavior changed.
- Non-legal empty cubes remain completely invisible.

FALLBACK
--------
Version 1.15.6 is the immediate known-good fallback if the larger-board raycasting
change produces unwanted interaction behavior.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
This is Bundle Version 1.15.7. Active project-owned version references and the
HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters are synchronized to 1.15.7. Historical log
entries retain their original release versions.

TESTING / RELEASE CHECKS
------------------------
- Confirmed getFrontmostLegalHit() has a dedicated 4×4×4 branch preserving the
  occupied-cell blocking rule.
- Confirmed 6×6×6 and 8×8×8 raycasting searches for the nearest legal cell without
  allowing occupied cells to terminate the search.
- Confirmed hover highlighting and click selection both use getFrontmostLegalHit().
- Confirmed Version 1.15.6 opacity logic remains unchanged.
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All four required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Active version references checked against 1.15.7.
- HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters checked as 1.15.7.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly: index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity checked successfully.

NEXT TEST
---------
Deploy Version 1.15.7 and specifically test a late-game 6×6×6 and 8×8×8 position
where legal moves remain inside the completed-looking outer surface. Confirm that
an interior legal cube can be hovered/highlighted, its coordinate is revealed, and
it can be selected. Also confirm that 4×4×4 still cannot be clicked through a green
cube.

======================================================================
2026-09-03 — Version 1.15.3 — Make Bot-mode Undo/Redo operate one move at a time
======================================================================

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.15.2
------------------------
The user confirmed the multi-size boards and the 1.15.2 coordinate-system
changes are working. During Bot Opponent play, however, Undo and Redo were
restoring two history snapshots at a time. For example:

    Bot (Black) moves → Undo

was expected to return to the user's immediately preceding White move, but
instead returned two moves farther back to the Bot's previous Black move. Redo
then skipped the White move as well.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
Bot-mode Undo/Redo had intentionally been implemented as a two-snapshot
"human decision cycle". The code used Math.min(2, history.length) and restored
two snapshots per button press. That behavior was useful for an earlier Bot-mode
interaction model, but it does not match normal move-by-move Undo/Redo behavior
when the user expects every button press to traverse exactly one committed move.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.15.3 removes the special two-snapshot Bot-mode Undo/Redo behavior.
Undo and Redo now restore exactly one history snapshot per button press in all
game modes.

Therefore, in Bot mode:

    Bot Black move → Undo

returns to the user's immediately preceding White position. A subsequent Undo
returns to the position before that White move. Redo reverses this one move at
a time in the same order.

Any pending delayed Bot move timer is also cancelled when Undo or Redo is
pressed so that a queued Bot action cannot unexpectedly execute after the user
has moved backward or forward through history.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules changed.
- No legal-move calculation changed.
- No bot strategy or random-move behavior changed.
- No board-size behavior changed.
- No coordinate notation or coordinate-entry behavior changed.
- No ray-casting/selection behavior changed.
- No clock behavior was intentionally changed.
- No board geometry or appearance changed.

2026-09-03 — Version 1.14.1 — Repair Bot-mode Undo/Redo after Game Complete
======================================================================

USER FEEDBACK / REQUEST
-----------------------
The user tested Version 1.14.0 and confirmed the Reversi rename and gameplay
changes are working. During Bot-mode testing, the user found a specific
Undo/Redo problem at the end of a completed game:

- After a game reached Game Complete, clicking Undo correctly moved the game
  backward, but Redo remained disabled.
- Clicking Undo a second time caused Redo to become enabled.
- The expected behavior is that the first Undo from a completed game should
  immediately make Redo available so the completed game can be restored.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
Bot-mode Undo treats one click as a human decision cycle and restores the
associated human/bot history states together. After the restore, the existing
Undo handler always called scheduleBotTurn(). In states where Undo had just
created Redo history, that bot scheduling could start a new bot move. A new bot
move clears redoHistory, which could immediately remove the Redo state that
should have represented the completed game.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.14.1 changes the Bot-mode Undo handling so the bot is only
automatically restarted when Undo returns to the bot's opening turn AND there
is no Redo history.

When Redo history exists after an Undo, no new bot move is started. This keeps
the restored history intact and allows the user to Redo back to the completed
game.

Expected Bot-mode behavior is now:

    Completed game → Undo → Redo enabled

and the user can use Redo to restore the completed position, including its
Game Complete state.

The existing Bot-mode decision-cycle behavior remains unchanged: Undo/Redo
continue to operate on the human move plus the bot response as a unit.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi game rules changed.
- No legal-move calculation changed.
- No bot strategy or random-move behavior changed.
- No board geometry or appearance changed.
- No clock behavior changed.
- No Two Humans Undo/Redo rules changed.
- No splash-screen behavior changed.
- No Reversi terminology or Bundle file naming changed from Version 1.14.0.
- Version 1.13.2 remains the fallback for the 1.14.x series.

TESTING / RELEASE CHECKS
------------------------
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- Confirmed the repaired Bot-mode Undo path preserves Redo history when
  returning from Game Complete.
- Confirmed the bot is still automatically scheduled when Undo returns to the
  bot's opening turn without Redo history.
- Confirmed all four required Bundle files are present and nonzero.
- Confirmed active project-owned version references are synchronized to 1.14.1.
- Confirmed index.html CSS/JavaScript cache-busters are synchronized to 1.14.1.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly: index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity checked successfully.
- User testing of the completed-game Undo/Redo repair remains the next live
  validation step.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
This is Bundle Version 1.14.1. Active project-owned version references and
HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters are synchronized to 1.14.1. Historical
version numbers and archived Othello release artifacts remain unchanged.

2026-09-03 — Version 1.13.2 — Enable and make Undo/Redo functional in Bot mode
======================================================================

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.13.1
------------------------
The user confirmed that the Version 1.13.1 Bot Opponent appearance, Chrome tab
title, and bot-move startup repairs all worked correctly. The user then tested
Bot Opponent mode and found that the Undo and Redo buttons were still disabled.
The user specifically requested that Undo and Redo be enabled while playing
against the bot.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
The existing Undo button logic correctly allowed Bot Opponent games past the
Two Humans restriction, but timed games still applied the older one-move Undo
limit globally:

    (timedGame && undoHistory.length > 1)

In a Bot game, a normal human move followed by the bot response creates two
history snapshots. As a result, the timed-game restriction disabled Undo as
soon as the bot had answered. Redo was similarly tied to the general history
state and could not become useful until Undo was available.

DESIGN / REPAIR
---------------
Version 1.13.2 makes the existing intended Bot-mode behavior explicit:

- Two Humans + timed duration: Undo and Redo remain disabled.
- Two Humans + Unlimited: Undo and Redo remain enabled according to history.
- Bot Opponent + timed duration: Undo and Redo are enabled according to history.
- Bot Opponent + Unlimited: Undo and Redo are enabled according to history.

Because Bot Opponent games alternate between a human move and a bot response,
Bot-mode Undo is treated as one human decision cycle when two move snapshots
are available. It therefore reverses the most recent human move and the bot's
response together. This prevents Undo from leaving the game stranded on a bot
turn. If only one relevant snapshot exists, such as a bot opening move or a
human move followed by an automatic bot pass, Undo reverses that available
state.

Bot-mode Redo mirrors the same grouped behavior, restoring the states that the
Bot-mode Undo operation reversed. This keeps the user-facing Undo/Redo controls
useful while preserving the existing snapshot-based game-state architecture.

The one-move Undo restriction remains in force for timed Two Humans games, as
established in Version 1.12.4/1.12.5. The change does not alter the game-clock
rules: Undo still does not restore elapsed clock time.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Othello rules or legal-move calculation were changed.
- No bot strategy beyond random legal-move selection was added.
- No difficulty levels, tunable move values, look-ahead search, minimax,
  Bot-vs-Bot mode, self-play, or machine learning were added.
- No 6x6x6 or 8x8x8 support was added.
- No board geometry, piece appearance, legal/occupied surface behavior, or
  starting position was changed.
- No Arcball rotation, zoom, coordinate entry, orientation markers, or iPhone
  interaction behavior was intentionally changed.
- No game-clock rules were changed.
- Two Humans timed-game Undo/Redo behavior remains disabled.
- Two Humans Unlimited Undo/Redo behavior remains enabled.

TESTING / RELEASE CHECKS
------------------------
- Confirmed Bot Opponent games are no longer blocked by the timed-game
  one-move Undo restriction.
- Confirmed Bot-mode Undo can reverse the latest human move plus bot response
  when both are present, preventing an unusable bot-turn state.
- Confirmed Bot-mode Redo restores the grouped state in the correct order.
- Confirmed the single-snapshot Bot-mode cases remain reversible.
- Confirmed Two Humans + timed duration remains disabled for Undo/Redo.
- Confirmed Two Humans + Unlimited remains enabled according to history.
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All four required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Active version references and CSS/JavaScript cache-busters synchronized to
  1.13.2.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly: index.html, Othello.css, Othello.js,
  Othello_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity checked successfully.

FALLBACK
--------
Version 1.13.1 remains the immediately preceding known-good bot baseline.
Version 1.12.5 remains the immediately preceding known-good non-bot baseline.

-----------------------

entries retain their original release versions.


2026-09-03 — Version 1.13.1 — Repair Bot Opponent startup and active-option appearance
======================================================================

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.13.0
------------------------
The user tested Version 1.13.0 and identified three concrete problems:

1. The Bot Opponent option was selectable, but its text appeared grayed out.
   It should have the same appearance as the Two Humans option now that Bot
   Opponent is enabled.
2. The visible page version showed 1.13.0, but the Chrome tab title still showed
   1.12.5. The active browser title was therefore stale.
3. The user started a Bot Opponent game as Black with a 5-minute clock, but the
   bot did not make its expected move when it was the bot's turn.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
The Bot Opponent splash option still carried the old `start-option-disabled`
styling class left over from the pre-bot "coming soon" state. The option was
functionally selectable, but the class intentionally reduced its opacity.

The bot startup failure was caused by a guard in the shared `playMove()`
function. Human input must be blocked while it is the bot's turn, but the first
Version 1.13.0 bot implementation also called `playMove()` to make its own
move. The same guard therefore rejected the bot's move before it could be
committed.

The Chrome tab title was simply a stale active version marker in `index.html`:
the title remained 1.12.5 while the visible page version and cache-busters had
already advanced to 1.13.0.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------

- Removed the `start-option-disabled` class from the Bot Opponent splash option.
  Bot Opponent now has the same normal appearance as Two Humans while remaining
  selectable.
- Updated the Chrome document title from 1.12.5 to 1.13.1 and synchronized the
  visible version and CSS/JavaScript cache-busters to 1.13.1.
- Added an explicit internal bot-move path to `playMove()`. Human input remains
  blocked whenever it is the bot's turn, but `scheduleBotTurn()` now identifies
  its call as a bot move so the existing authoritative move logic can accept it.

The bot still chooses exactly one random move from the currently available
legal moves. No strategy, look-ahead, difficulty system, or other AI behavior
was added in this repair release.

This repair preserves the existing game-state path: bot moves continue through
`playMove()` so normal placement, flips, Move Sequence recording, clock
handling, last-move marker updates, and game-completion logic remain shared with
human moves.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------

- No board geometry, piece appearance, legal/occupied surface behavior, or
  starting position was changed.

  interaction behavior was intentionally changed.
- No game-clock rules or Undo/Redo rules were changed.
- Two Humans mode remains unchanged.

======================================================================
2026-09-03 — Future Bot Strategy / Architecture Notes — Planning discussion
======================================================================

USER FEEDBACK / DESIGN DIRECTION
--------------------------------
The user approved the general direction for developing the Cube Othello bot
from the simple Version 1.13.0 random-move implementation toward a tunable
strategy system. These notes are planning guidance, not a released gameplay
change.

BOT ARCHITECTURE
----------------
- Keep one underlying bot framework rather than creating three unrelated bots
  for Easy, Medium, and Hard. Different difficulty levels should primarily
  change how the same bot evaluates positions and searches for moves.
- The basic bot pipeline should remain conceptually simple:
    1. Generate all currently legal moves.
    2. Evaluate the available moves/ resulting positions.
    3. Choose a move according to the selected difficulty and strategy.
- The Version 1.13.0 random bot is intentionally useful as the plumbing/test
  stage before strategy is added.

TUNABLE MOVE VALUES / STRATEGY
------------------------------
The user likes the idea of assigning values to moves according to the situation,
with those values being tunable. Future bot strategy should therefore be
designed around adjustable evaluation weights rather than a collection of
rigid one-off rules.

Possible strategic factors to investigate include:
- Corner value: very high positive value when a corner is legally available.
- Edge value: positive value for strong edge positions, with the exact value
  determined experimentally rather than assumed.
- Piece gain: reward for capturing additional pieces, but not necessarily at
  the expense of stronger positional advantages.
- Mobility: value positions that preserve many good future moves.
- Opponent mobility: penalize positions that give the opponent many strong
  choices.
- Strategic danger: strongly penalize moves that give the opponent access to
  valuable corners or other strategically powerful positions.
- Additional 3D-specific positional factors should be discovered through
  testing rather than simply copying 2D Othello assumptions.

The exact numerical weights are intentionally undecided. They should be easy
to change so that different strategies can be tested against one another.

LOOK-AHEAD / SEARCH DEPTH
-------------------------
A stronger bot can calculate multiple moves ahead. This is not necessarily
overkill, but search depth should be treated as a tunable difficulty/performance
tradeoff. A simple first strategy can evaluate the immediate resulting position;
more advanced levels can look one or more turns ahead using a minimax-style
search. Deeper search may become increasingly expensive as the board grows, so
the bot architecture should allow search depth and other limits to be adjusted
without changing the overall bot interface.

POSSIBLE DIFFICULTY LEVELS
--------------------------
The current planning direction is:
- Easy: simple positional evaluation with some randomness so play is not always
  identical.
- Medium: stronger evaluation and limited look-ahead/search.
- Hard: more complete positional evaluation, mobility considerations, deeper
  search, and better handling of near-equal candidate moves.

These labels and exact implementations are provisional until actual 3D game
results show what produces meaningful differences in playing strength.

BOT VS BOT / SELF-PLAY
----------------------
A future Bot vs Bot mode is desirable for development and experimentation.
Useful comparisons include Easy vs Easy, Easy vs Medium, Medium vs Hard, and
Hard vs Hard. Large batches of self-play games could be used to compare
strategies and tune evaluation weights.

Longer-term, the project could become a 3D Othello strategy laboratory in
which human-designed weights are tested first, followed by automated
self-play optimization and, if useful, machine-learning approaches later.
Machine learning is optional; it should not be required before the basic
strategy/search framework is understood and working.

BOARD-SIZE GENERALIZATION
-------------------------
The same bot framework should ideally be able to play future larger Cube
Othello boards, including 6x6x6 and 8x8x8, rather than requiring a completely
different bot for each board size. Board-size-specific strategy parameters or
weights may still be appropriate, and search depth/performance limits may need
to change with board size. The preferred architecture is therefore one general
bot engine that receives the current board size and game state and can use
size-specific tuning when necessary.

The strategy should not hardcode assumptions that only work on the current
4x4x4 board. This should be kept in mind when the bot code is separated from
the main game logic.

CODE ORGANIZATION
-----------------
As bot strategy becomes more substantial, the preferred direction is to keep
the game engine and bot decision-making logically separate. A likely early
organization is:
- Othello.js: board/game state, rules, UI, turn management, and game flow.
- OthelloBot.js: bot move generation, evaluation, difficulty settings, and
  search/decision logic.

Do not split Easy/Medium/Hard into separate bot files unless that becomes
necessary. They should initially be configurations/strategies within the same
bot framework.

EXPERIMENTAL APPROACH
---------------------
The first strategy experiment may be intentionally simple: if a legal corner
exists, choose a corner; otherwise choose randomly. From there, progressively
replace hardcoded rules with tunable weighted evaluation so the effects can be
measured and compared.

NO RELEASE CHANGE
-----------------
These are future-development notes only. No bot strategy, difficulty level,
look-ahead search, Bot vs Bot mode, self-play system, machine learning, larger
board support, or code-file split is being added by this planning entry.
Version 1.13.0 remains the current released bot implementation until a later
Bundle intentionally changes it.

2026-09-03 — Version 1.12.3 — Add per-player game clocks
======================================================================

USER REQUEST ON 1.12.2
-----------------------
The user requested the next development step: add the previously agreed game
clock to the current 1.12.2 known-good baseline. The user will test the new
clock in Two Humans mode by playing both Black and White.

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Add the game clock using the previously established design:

- Each player receives an independent clock using the selected Game Duration:
  1, 2, 5, 10, or 30 minutes, or Unlimited.
- Only the player whose turn it is consumes time.
- Unlimited disables countdown and timeout behavior.
- Clock timing must be based on actual elapsed time rather than relying solely
  on one-second interval ticks, to remain reliable when browser timers are
  throttled, especially on iPhone/Safari.
- If the active player's clock reaches zero, that player immediately loses and
  the other color wins.
- Timeout uses a forfeit score of 64-0 or 0-64.
- Timeout is recorded in Move Sequence as TIMEOUT-BLACK or TIMEOUT-WHITE.
- When a timed game is active, Undo is limited to one move. Undo never restores
  time to either player's clock. The active player's clock continues running
  after Undo.
- Game settings remain controlled by the start screen and are not changed
  during an active game.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.12.3 adds a visible Black clock and White clock to the game status
area. The selected duration initializes both clocks to the same amount. The
active player's clock counts down using elapsed-time calculations based on
performance.now(). The animation loop checks the active clock continuously and
handles timeout without depending on one-second timer accuracy.

After a legal move, elapsed time is committed to the player who just moved and
the clock switches to the next active player. If the opponent has no legal move
and must pass, the current player's clock continues running because that same
player remains the active player.

When a clock reaches zero, the timed-out color is recorded in Move Sequence as
TIMEOUT-BLACK or TIMEOUT-WHITE, the game ends immediately, and the existing
Game Complete overlay reports the timeout result with a 64-0 / 0-64 forfeit
score.

The Move Sequence parser/loader also recognizes TIMEOUT-BLACK and TIMEOUT-WHITE
so a timeout result remains recoverable from a saved sequence. Loaded sequences
do not contain timing information, so a successfully loaded non-complete timed
game starts with a fresh clock for the configured duration.

Undo/Redo do not restore clock time. Restoring a board snapshot simply starts
the current player's existing clock from the moment the snapshot is restored,
so no elapsed time is silently given back. In a timed game the available Undo
history is constrained to one move.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No game rules, move legality, scoring, board geometry, or piece behavior
  changed.
- No Arcball rotation, zoom, selection, orientation-marker, or iPhone canvas
  interaction behavior changed.
- No bot opponent was added. Bot Opponent remains disabled/coming soon.
- The existing resignation, pass, Move Sequence, and New Game systems remain
  in place; timeout is added as the analogous timed-game result.
- The existing 1.12.2 splash-screen layout and its six duration choices remain
  unchanged.

FALLBACK
--------
Version 1.12.2 remains the known-good fallback/baseline for the 1.12.x
development series. Version 1.10.4 remains the earlier fallback if a rollback
beyond the 1.12.x work is ever needed.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
This is Bundle Version 1.12.3. Active project-owned version references and
the HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters are synchronized to 1.12.3. Historical
log entries retain their original release versions.

TESTING / RELEASE CHECKS
------------------------

- Confirmed all six duration choices continue to be available.

the resignation.

2026-09-03 — Version 1.9.15 — Keep yellow legal-move highlight within one cube
======================================================================

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Repair the yellow legal-move highlight so that it remains confined to the
single legal cube currently under the pointer. The Version 1.9.14 blue opacity
progression and the directly-visible legal-cube selection behavior are to
remain unchanged.

USER FEEDBACK
-------------
The Version 1.9.14 blue opacity update was accepted as a good improvement.
However, the yellow highlighted legal cube was visibly spreading into the
adjacent cube in the supplied screenshot, making the highlight appear larger
than a single cube.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
The yellow highlight uses a 1.25 × 1.25 × 1.25 BoxGeometry, matching the
normal cell size, but Version 1.9.14 additionally scaled that geometry by 1.08.
That intentionally enlarged the highlight by 8 percent in every direction,
causing its translucent faces to extend beyond the boundaries of the selected
cube and overlap neighboring cubes.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Removed the 1.08 enlargement from the yellow legal-move highlight. The
highlight now uses the same 1.0 scale as its 1.25-unit cube geometry, keeping
the yellow surface within the bounds of exactly one selected legal cube.

The blue legal-surface opacity progression remains unchanged from Version
1.9.14: it starts at 0.10, follows the established early-game curve, reaches
0.50 at the halfway point, and remains at 0.50 thereafter. The Version 1.9.12
directly-visible ray-casting behavior and Version 1.9.14 depth/rendering
changes are also preserved.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
No changes were made to game rules, scoring, move validation, coordinate entry,
ray-casting selection rules, blue opacity progression, green surface opacity,
blue surface depth behavior, camera behavior, rotation controls, board size,
orientation markers, or page layout. Non-legal empty cubes remain completely
invisible.

FALLBACK

2026-09-03 — Version 1.9.14 — Steepen blue opacity ramp and eliminate viewing-angle opacity changes
======================================================================

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Keep the established starting blue legal-surface opacity at 0.10, make the
second stage follow the same 1.9.13 curve, reach 0.50 opacity at the halfway
point of the game, and keep it at 0.50 for the remainder. Also prevent the
apparent blue opacity from changing when the cube is rotated.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
The blue legal-surface opacity now uses four visual stages:

1. The game begins at the established 0.10 opacity.
2. Through the first quarter of the game, the opacity follows the same
   2.5-power progression used in Version 1.9.13, reaching 0.1125 at 25%
   progress.
3. From the first-quarter point to the halfway point, the opacity ramps
   strongly upward to 0.50.
4. From halfway through the game to the end, the blue legal-surface opacity
   remains at 0.50.

To prevent apparent opacity changes caused by translucent legal surfaces at
different depths overlapping in the rendered image, the blue legal surface
now writes depth. The occupied green surface retains depth writing and is
rendered before the blue legal surface. This makes the frontmost visible
translucent surface determine the blue contribution at each screen location
instead of allowing deeper blue surfaces to accumulate opacity as the cube
angle changes.

The Version 1.9.12 directly-visible ray-casting behavior remains unchanged.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
No changes were made to game rules, scoring, move validation, coordinate entry,
ray-casting selection rules, camera behavior, rotation controls, board size,
yellow highlight opacity, orientation markers, or page layout. Non-legal empty
cubes remain completely invisible.

FALLBACK
--------
Version 1.9.11 remains the broad visual fallback. Version 1.9.12 remains the
working direct-visibility interaction baseline, and Version 1.9.13 remains the
previous rendering baseline.

TESTING / RELEASE CHECKS
------------------------
- Active Bundle version references updated to 1.9.14.
- HTML CSS and JavaScript cache-busters updated to 1.9.14.
- CSS and JavaScript active header markers updated to 1.9.14.
- Confirmed blue opacity starts at 0.10.
- Confirmed the first-quarter point follows the 1.9.13 2.5-power curve and
  reaches 0.1125.
- Confirmed blue opacity reaches 0.50 at the halfway point and remains there.
- Confirmed occupied green surface depth writing remains enabled.
- Confirmed blue legal surface depth writing is enabled to prevent translucent
  overlap from changing apparent opacity with viewing angle.
- Confirmed occupied surface render order precedes legal surface render order.
- JavaScript syntax check performed with Node.
- All four required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly: index.html, Othello.css, Othello.js, Othello_Log.txt.

2026-09-02 — Version 1.6.1 — Repair blank page caused by marker initialization order

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.6.0
----------------------
The user deployed/tested Version 1.6.0 and reported that the page loads without the
main cube and the corner coordinate model.

DIAGNOSIS / ROOT CAUSE
----------------------
Version 1.6.0 introduced the orientation marker near the top of Othello.js, but the
marker position calculation used the constants `spacing` and `boardOffset` before
they were declared later in the file. Because Othello.js is an ES module, those
`const` declarations are in the temporal dead zone until execution reaches their
declarations. The early reference therefore throws a ReferenceError during module
initialization, stopping the script before the board and coordinate model can be
created.

This explains the exact symptom: the HTML page itself loads, but the JavaScript
initialization stops before the 3D scene is populated.

REPAIR
------
Version 1.6.1 moves the existing `spacing` and `boardOffset` declarations to the
top-level constants section, before the new orientation marker uses them. Their
values are unchanged. No marker geometry, board geometry, coordinate mapping,
game rules, scorekeeping, move entry, click handling, or camera behavior was
otherwise changed.

VERSION / RELEASE CHECKS
------------------------
- Updated active source version references and cache-buster to 1.6.1.
- JavaScript syntax check passed.
- Confirmed `spacing` and `boardOffset` are declared before their first use.
- Confirmed the 1.6.0 orientation-marker code remains otherwise unchanged.
- Confirmed all four required Bundle files are present and nonzero.
- Confirmed the final ZIP contains exactly index.html, Othello.css, Othello.js, and
  Othello_Log.txt.

USER TEST REQUEST
-----------------
Deploy/test Version 1.6.1. The expected result is that the main cube and corner
coordinate model load normally again, with the two small red orientation markers
visible at their corresponding locations.

2026-09-02 — Version 1.5.1 — Restore click-to-move after 1.5.0 cleanup

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.5.0
----------------------
The user committed and tested Version 1.5.0. Typed coordinate moves work correctly,
but clicking a legal cube does nothing.

DIAGNOSIS / ROOT CAUSE
----------------------
The 1.5.0 cleanup correctly removed several obsolete repair mechanisms, but it also
removed the project's pointerup handler along with the redundant pointermove logic.
The pointerdown handler still recorded which legal cube had been pressed, but there
was no pointerup handler left to complete the click-to-move operation by calling
playMove().

This explains the observed behavior exactly:
- typed coordinate moves still work because they call submitCoordinateMove() and
  playMove() directly;
- clicking a legal cube cannot make a move because the recorded pointerDownLegalKey
  was never consumed by a pointerup event.

CHANGE
------
Version 1.5.1 restores the missing pointerup handler from the known working click
interaction path. It:
1. ignores non-left-button releases and incomplete pointer states;
2. confirms the pointer was not dragged;
3. confirms the recorded cube is still a legal move;
4. calls playMove() for that cube;
5. clears the pointer interaction state.

The pointercancel cleanup handler was also restored so interrupted pointer gestures
properly clear their state.

No game rules, coordinate notation, typed entry behavior, legal-move calculation,
coordinate model, rendering design, or keyboard controls were changed.

2026-09-02 — Version 1.5.0 — Clean up recent typed-move repair attempts

USER REQUEST
------------
The user asked for a review of the last-known-working 1.4.17 Bundle, with
particular attention to whether the JavaScript had become unnecessarily
complicated after several attempts to solve the same typed-coordinate problem.

The goal of this release is to establish a clean 1.5.0 baseline before the
user begins the next fix-list.

REVIEW / ROOT CAUSE
-------------------
The review found that the recent attempts had left several layers of repair
code in the JavaScript that were no longer serving a useful purpose.

Most importantly, the HTML no longer contains the #message element. That
element had intentionally been removed during the earlier coordinate-model
cleanup, but the JavaScript still referenced `message.textContent` in the
typed-move and playMove paths. A move could therefore change the board and
then throw when trying to update the missing message element, before the
normal legal-cell refresh completed.

This explains the reported symptom in a way that is consistent with the
actual current files: after a typed move, the board state could change, but
the legal-move surface was not rebuilt until a later pointer movement caused
the pointer handler to call updateLegalCells().

The review also found that Version 1.4.17 introduced `legalCellsDirty`, but
the main animate() loop did not actually consume that flag. The log described
the render loop as consuming it, but the current JavaScript did not do so.
The flag was therefore dead repair scaffolding rather than an effective
refresh mechanism.

The `suppressHoverUntilPointerMove` flag was also unnecessary in the current
implementation. It was being consumed by the coordinate-guide update
function rather than by the actual pointer/highlight handler. This could
leave the miniature coordinate highlight stale after a typed move.

Finally, the JavaScript contained two separate pointermove listeners. Their
behaviors were compatible and have been combined into one listener so that
drag detection and hover/highlight calculation remain in one place.

CLEANUP / ACTUAL CHANGES
------------------------
1. Removed the obsolete `message` DOM reference and all message-display code,
   because the message element is no longer part of index.html.

2. Removed `legalCellsDirty` and its associated assignments. The normal
   updateLegalCells() path already rebuilds the legal-move visual state, and
   the renderer continuously renders the current scene.

3. Removed `suppressHoverUntilPointerMove` and its related logic. The typed
   move now simply clears `highlightedLegalKey` before calling playMove().

4. Simplified updateLegalSurface() by removing the redundant
   updateLegalSurfaceAppearance() helper.

5. Combined the two pointermove event listeners while preserving both their
   existing responsibilities:
       - detecting a drag;
       - calculating the current hovered legal cube.

6. Removed the stray extra semicolon after the pointercancel listener.

7. Removed the now-unused #message CSS block.

8. Synchronized active project-owned version references and HTML cache-busters
   to 1.5.0. Historical log entries remain unchanged.

9. No changes were made to the board size, opening position, 26-direction
   rules, coordinate notation, guided coordinate-entry sequence, coordinate
   model, occupied-surface geometry, legal-surface geometry, camera controls,
   keyboard view controls, or New Game behavior.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
JavaScript syntax was checked with Node and passes.

Structural checks confirmed:
- no remaining references to the removed `message` variable;
- no remaining `legalCellsDirty` references;
- no remaining `suppressHoverUntilPointerMove` references;
- exactly one pointermove listener remains;
- playMove() remains the authoritative move-state path;
- typed moves still clear the input and highlightedLegalKey before playMove();
- updateLegalCells() remains called by playMove();
- clicked legal moves still use playMove();
- non-legal coordinate submissions still do not make a move;
- the coordinate model remains present;
- non-legal empty cubes remain invisible;
- the HTML, CSS, and JavaScript active version references are synchronized to
  1.5.0.

The four required Bundle files were checked for presence and nonzero size.

The final ZIP was checked to contain exactly:

    index.html
    Othello.css
    Othello.js
    Othello_Log.txt

Release is blocked if any required file is empty, if active Bundle version
references are stale or mixed, or if the final ZIP contains missing or
unintended files.

2026-08-29 — Version 1.4.17 — Decouple typed legal-move display from mouse movement

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.4.16
----------------------
The user tested Version 1.4.16 and confirmed that the problem remains:
after a coordinate is entered and the move is made, the legal moves still do
not visibly update until the mouse is moved.

The coordinate box clearing is working and the typed move itself is working.

REASSESSMENT
------------
The previous releases concentrated on forcing a renderer call after the
typed move. That was not sufficient because the project's legal-move
highlight/display lifecycle is tied to the pointer interaction state.

A renderer call by itself does not guarantee that the legal-cell visual state
has been rebuilt in the same state expected by the normal pointer/render
cycle.

CHANGE
------
Version 1.4.17 separates the legal-cell update from pointer movement.

A legal-cell dirty flag is now set whenever the legal-cell collection is
rebuilt. The main render loop consumes that state independently of mouse
movement.

The typed coordinate path now:
    - clears the coordinate field;
    - clears the old highlightedLegalKey;
    - makes the move through playMove();
    - marks the legal-cell display dirty.

The earlier ad-hoc requestAnimationFrame renderer added in Version 1.4.16
was removed. The new approach uses the application's existing render loop
instead of trying to create a second rendering path.

No artificial mouse movement is generated.

No changes were made to coordinate notation, coordinate validation, move
legality, board rules, click/mouse-up behavior, coordinate model, or
undo/history.

2026-08-29 — Version 1.4.15 — Prevent stationary-pointer hover from delaying legal-move refresh

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.4.14
----------------------
The user reported that the typed move succeeds, but the new legal moves still
do not visually update until the mouse is moved.

The mouse remains physically over the cube that was just played, so the
existing pointer-driven highlight state can continue to control the visual
highlight until another pointer event occurs.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
The legal-move display and yellow highlighting are tied to the pointer
interaction state. Rebuilding legal cells after a typed move does not itself
generate a new mouse event.

CHANGE
------
Version 1.4.15 adds a one-shot hover suppression flag to the project's actual
pointer/highlight handler.

When a complete typed coordinate is submitted:
    - the coordinate input is cleared;
    - highlightedLegalKey is cleared;
    - suppressHoverUntilPointerMove is set to true;
    - playMove() performs the normal move and game-state update.

The next actual pointer event is consumed once without restoring the old hover
highlight. After that one event, normal hover behavior resumes.

This prevents the old pointer position from preserving the pre-move highlight
while still allowing ordinary mouse highlighting to work normally afterward.

No artificial mouse movement is generated.

No changes were made to the coordinate-entry sequence, board rules, move
legality, coordinate model, or undo/history.

2026-08-29 — Version 1.4.13 — Isolate typed Enter behavior and clear transient highlight before move

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.4.12
----------------------
The user reported that the coordinate entry still did not visibly clear after
pressing Enter. They also observed that the cube just played became green
while simultaneously remaining yellow-highlighted, and that the legal-move
display did not visibly refresh until the mouse moved.

The user requested that this release focus on the coordinate-entry behavior.

ROOT CAUSE / APPROACH
---------------------
The prior releases attempted to repair the post-move display after playMove()
had already changed the game state. That allowed the existing pointer hover
state to remain relevant during the visual refresh.

Version 1.4.13 simplifies the sequence.

For a complete typed coordinate submitted with Enter:
    1. prevent the browser's default form action;
    2. validate the coordinate;
    3. clear the coordinate input immediately;
    4. clear the transient highlightedLegalKey immediately;
    5. call the existing playMove() function.

playMove() then performs the normal board update, turn advancement, legal-move
calculation, and rendering once.

This makes the old yellow highlight impossible to survive into the move
because it is cleared before playMove() rebuilds the legal-move surface.

The separate refreshAfterCoordinateMove() helper from the previous release
was removed because it was unnecessary for this focused fix.

The Play button continues to use the form's submit handler and the same
submitCoordinateMove() path.

2026-08-29 — Version 1.4.9 — Complete typed-move submission behavior

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.4.8
----------------------
The user tested the guided coordinate entry and requested that completing a
move with Enter produce the same visible/game-state result as making the move
by clicking a legal cube.

After pressing Enter:
    - the coordinate-entry box should clear;
    - the cube that was played should become green, whether or not it was
      currently highlighted;
    - the turn should advance to the next player.

CHANGE
------
Version 1.4.9 makes the existing playMove() path authoritative for typed
coordinate moves as well as clicked moves.

A successful coordinate submission now relies on playMove() to perform the
complete move-state update. The coordinate input is cleared as part of the
successful move path rather than being cleared before the move has completed.

This means a typed move and a clicked legal move use the same board update,
sphere placement, occupied-cube rendering, turn advancement, legal-move
calculation, and highlight refresh.

The newly occupied cube therefore receives the same green occupied-cube
appearance as a cube played by clicking.

Invalid or non-legal coordinate submissions do not advance the game and do
not falsely turn a cube green.

2026-08-29 — Version 1.4.8 — Guided coordinate entry

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.4.7
----------------------
The user requested a strict, guided coordinate-entry system.

The requested sequence is:
    1. accept only A, B, C, or D;
    2. automatically add a dash;
    3. accept only 1, 2, 3, or 4;
    4. automatically add a dash;
    5. accept only W, X, Y, or Z;
    6. allow Enter or Play once the coordinate is complete.

The user also requested:
    - typed letters should appear uppercase;
    - all other keys should be ignored during entry;
    - Backspace should be the only editing key and should allow correction of
      the last entered coordinate character.

CHANGE
------
Version 1.4.8 replaces the previous permissive coordinate parser/input
behavior with guided character-by-character entry.

The input sequence is now:

    A/B/C/D  ->  automatic "-"  ->
    1/2/3/4  ->  automatic "-"  ->
    W/X/Y/Z

The displayed value therefore builds itself as:

    A-
    A-2-
    A-2-Y

Lowercase a-d and w-z are accepted as typed input but are immediately
converted to uppercase. The input is also styled as uppercase.

At each stage, only the appropriate coordinate character is accepted.
Unrecognized keys do not enter the field.

Backspace is the only editing key accepted while entering a coordinate. It
removes the most recently entered coordinate character and its automatically
generated separator as appropriate, allowing the user to correct the last
entry.

Enter is accepted only when the coordinate is complete. The Play button
continues to submit the same coordinate-entry form.

Paste and drag/drop into the coordinate field are disabled so that the field
cannot bypass the guided entry sequence.

The existing move-validation and playMove() logic remains the single path
used to make a typed move.

No undo/history functionality was added in 1.4.8.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
This is Bundle Version 1.4.8. Active project-owned version references and
the HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters were synchronized to 1.4.8. Historical
log entries retain their original release versions.

---------------------------

coordinates.

2026-08-29 — Version 1.4.3 — Add coordinate move entry

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.4.2
----------------------
The user approved the visible coordinate system and requested two additions:
    - make the letters and numbers on the coordinate-axis model slightly
      larger;
    - add a place in the lower-right of the game space (not the sidebar) to
      type a move coordinate.

CHANGE
------
The coordinate-axis labels are now slightly larger for easier reading (36px instead of 30px).
Their positions, colors, axis assignments, and synchronization behavior are
unchanged.

A compact move-entry control has been added to the lower-right corner of the
game space. It contains:
    - a coordinate input field;
    - a Play button;
    - Enter/Return submits the move.

The accepted coordinate notation is the established three-part system:
    A - 2 - Y

The parser accepts the displayed spaced/dashed form as well as compact forms
such as A2Y or A-2-Y. Input is case-insensitive. The coordinate is converted
to the existing internal x/y/z board coordinates.

If the coordinate is malformed, the game reports the expected format. If it
is a valid coordinate but is not a legal move for the current player, the game
reports that it is not legal. A legal coordinate is passed through the same
existing playMove() path used by clicking, so captures, turn handling, score
updates, and legal-move updates remain centralized in the existing game logic.

No undo, redo, move history, or other game-state tracking was added in this
release.

the coordinate readout is cleared.

game rules, board state, move validation, click behavior, or camera controls.

2026-08-29 — Version 1.4.0 — Establish visible 3D coordinate system

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.3.1
----------------------
The user agreed that the 1.4.x series should establish a coordinate system
before typed moves or undo.

The user selected the notation:
    - X axis: A, B, C, D
    - Y axis: 1, 2, 3, 4
    - Z axis: W, X, Y, Z

A board position is therefore written in the form B3-Y.

The user requested that coordinate labels not be placed on the existing main
cube rendering. Instead, a small model should appear in the lower-left
corner. The model should consist of three rays originating from one common
point and rotate in sync with the main cube.

CHANGE
------
Version 1.4.0 adds a small three-axis coordinate model in the lower-left
corner of the game viewport.

The three axes originate at one common point and are labeled:
    - X axis: A B C D
    - Y axis: 1 2 3 4
    - Z axis: W X Y Z

The axis model is deliberately separate from the main board rendering. The
main cube receives no coordinate labels.

The coordinate model is rendered in its own transparent Three.js scene with
a small orthographic camera. Each axis has an arrow/ray and its four
coordinate labels.

Synchronization accounts for both kinds of visual rotation in the existing
project:
    - keyboard controls rotate cubeGroup;
    - mouse dragging rotates the view through OrbitControls by moving the
      camera.

The coordinate model therefore uses the main cube orientation combined with
the inverse camera orientation each animation frame. This keeps the model
visually synchronized with the main cube during both keyboard rotation and
mouse-drag viewing.

The coordinate labels are sprites so the text remains readable while the
three-dimensional model rotates.

No typed-move input, coordinate-entry controls, move history, undo, or redo
functionality is added in 1.4.0. This release establishes only the visual
coordinate system.

-----------------------

2026-08-29 — Version 1.3.0 — New keyboard controls

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.2.17
----------------------
The user said the current appearance is pretty good and requested that the
next version begin the 1.3.x series.

REQUEST
-------
Change the keyboard controls:
    - tilt: Up/Down arrow keys instead of W/S;
    - rotate: Left/Right arrow keys instead of A/D;
    - zoom: add -/= keys.

CHANGE
------
Version 1.3.0 changes the keyboard controls as requested.

Tilt:
    - Up Arrow: tilt upward;
    - Down Arrow: tilt downward.

Rotate:
    - Left Arrow: rotate left;
    - Right Arrow: rotate right.

Zoom:
    - - key: zoom out;
    - = key: zoom in.

The existing Q/E roll controls and R reset-view control are preserved.

The -/= zoom controls adjust the existing camera distance directly and then
update the existing orbit controls. The + and _ shifted-key equivalents are
also accepted by the browser keyboard event.

The previous W/S/A/D mappings are no longer active.

All other game behavior and appearance remains unchanged from 1.2.17:
    - legal-move surface is blue at 0.08 opacity;
    - highlighted legal cube is bright yellow at 0.15 opacity;
    - highlighted legal cube remains an individual surface;
    - internal faces between adjacent legal moves remain omitted;
    - occupied green surfaces remain merged with internal faces omitted;
    - occupied green opacity remains 0.50;
    - non-legal cubes remain invisible;
    - mouse-up move behavior remains unchanged;
    - marching-ant effects remain absent.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------

2026-08-29 — Version 1.2.15 — Remove overlapping legal surface under highlighted cube

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.2.14
----------------------
The user reported that highlighted legal cubes appeared to have a strange
two-layer appearance, possibly resembling a gray cube inside the golden
highlight.

ANALYSIS
--------
The effect was caused by two translucent visual surfaces occupying the same
cube location:
    1. the merged blue legal-move surface underneath;
    2. the separate golden individual highlight cube on top.

Because both surfaces were translucent, their colors blended and could make
the highlighted cube look like it contained another layer.

CHANGE
------
Version 1.2.15 removes the highlighted cell from the merged legal surface
while that cell is highlighted.

The merged legal surface continues to contain all normal legal moves except
the currently highlighted cell. The separate golden highlight cube then
occupies that location by itself.

This produces one visible translucent surface at the highlighted location
instead of stacking blue and gold translucent surfaces.

The individual legal-cell meshes remain invisible interaction targets, so
individual hover/click behavior is preserved.

Normal legal moves remain blue (#2f80ed) at 0.08 opacity.

The highlighted legal cube remains yellowish-golden (#ffd83d) at 0.08 opacity.

When the pointer leaves the cube, the highlighted cell is no longer excluded
from the merged legal surface and returns to the normal blue legal-move
appearance.

The merged green occupied surface remains unchanged at 0.50 opacity with
internal occupied-to-occupied faces omitted.

Non-legal empty cubes remain completely invisible.

The marching-ant effect remains absent.

No game rules, move/capture logic, mouse-up move behavior, camera behavior,

2026-08-29 — Version 1.2.12 — Keep legal moves individually highlightable while removing internal faces

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.2.11
----------------------
The user confirmed that the internal faces between adjacent legal cubes were
successfully removed, but identified two problems:
    1. the legal-move opacity did not appear to change to 0.20;
    2. highlighting one legal cube highlighted the entire merged legal shape.

DESIGN / REPAIR
---------------
Version 1.2.12 keeps the merged legal-move surface for the normal appearance,
but separates highlighting from that merged surface.

The legal-move system now has three distinct roles:
    - legalSurface: one merged surface containing all normal legal moves, with
      internal faces omitted;
    - individual cellMeshes: invisible hit targets that remain associated
      with individual board cells;
    - legalHighlightSurface: a separate full-size cube created only for the
      single currently highlighted legal move.

This allows adjacent legal moves to visually behave as one surface while still
allowing the user to hover and highlight exactly one legal cube at a time.

Normal legal-move appearance:
    - blue (#2f80ed)
    - opacity 0.20

Highlighted legal-move appearance:
    - brighter blue (#5aa9ff)
    - opacity 0.20
    - applies only to the one highlighted legal cube

The merged legal surface itself is never recolored or made more opaque when a
legal cube is highlighted.

The individual legal-cell meshes remain invisible interaction targets so the
existing click/mouse-up behavior is preserved.

The merged green occupied surface remains unchanged at 0.50 opacity with
internal occupied-to-occupied faces omitted.

Non-legal empty cubes remain completely invisible.

The marching-ant effect remains absent.

No game rules, move/capture logic, mouse-up move behavior, camera behavior,
rotation controls, board size, occupied-surface geometry, or page layout was
intentionally changed.

2026-08-29 — Version 1.2.10 — Merge adjacent legal-move cube surfaces

----------------------

CHANGE
------
Version 1.2.10 adds a merged legal-move surface.

The current set of legal move cells is converted into one exposed-surface
geometry. For each legal cell:
    - a face is generated when the neighboring cell is not also a legal move;
    - a face between two adjacent legal moves is omitted.

Because the cells use the same 1.25-unit spacing and ±0.625 face extent as the
occupied green surface, adjacent legal moves now meet exactly at their shared
boundary without rendering an internal transparent face.

The individual legal-cell meshes are retained as transparent interaction
targets so the existing hover and mouse-up move behavior can continue to
identify a specific legal cube. They no longer contribute visible geometry.

Normal legal-move appearance:
    - blue (#2f80ed)

legal surface, using the brighter blue (#5aa9ff) at 0.50 opacity when a legal
cube is highlighted.

2026-08-29 — Version 1.2.8 — Correct merged occupied-cube geometry

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.2.7
----------------------
The user reported that the green occupied cubes were appearing as small
shrunken shapes around the spheres instead of retaining the full cube
dimensions. The user also reported that newly occupied cubes were not joining
the existing green shape.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
The merged-surface geometry used an incorrect scale conversion for the
cube-face coordinates.

The actual cube cells are 1.25 units wide and their centers are spaced 1.25
units apart. The face coordinates are expressed as ±0.5, so they must be
scaled to ±0.625 from each center. The previous calculation effectively
produced faces only half the required size. That caused the green surface to
appear to shrink around the spheres and prevented adjacent occupied surfaces
from meeting.

REPAIR
------
Version 1.2.8 returns to the known-good 1.2.5 source as the fallback baseline
and cleanly reapplies the merged occupied-surface experiment.

The merged occupied surface now uses the correct 1.25 × 1.25 × 1.25 cell
dimensions. Each face is positioned ±0.625 from its cell center, matching the
existing cube geometry exactly.

For each occupied cell:
    - an exposed face is generated only when the neighboring cell is empty
      or outside the board;
    - a face is omitted when the neighboring cell is also occupied.

Because neighboring occupied cells have centers exactly 1.25 units apart,
their shared boundary is now exactly coincident and no internal face is
generated. Newly occupied adjacent cells therefore join the existing green
shape correctly.

The individual occupied cell meshes remain hidden. The merged exposed
surface is the only green occupied-cell geometry.

The merged surface is rebuilt:
    - during initial board setup;
    - after every move;
    - after New Game/reset.

The marching-ant system remains removed.

Legal-move cubes remain at 0.09 opacity with golden highlighting at 0.22.
Non-legal empty cubes remain completely invisible.

No game rules, move/capture logic, click behavior, camera behavior, rotation
controls, board size, or page layout was intentionally changed.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
This is Bundle Version 1.2.8. All active project-owned version references and
HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters were synchronized to 1.2.8. Historical log
entries retain their original release versions.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
JavaScript syntax was checked with Node and passes.
The merged-surface face scale was checked against the 1.25-unit cube size:
each face extends exactly 0.625 units from its cell center.
The neighbor test was checked to omit internal faces between adjacent
occupied cells.
The merged surface is rebuilt on initial setup, after moves, and after reset.
Individual occupied cell meshes remain hidden.
Marching-ant edge creation and animation remain absent.
The occupied green surface remains at 0.50 opacity.
Normal legal moves remain at 0.09 opacity.
Golden legal-move highlighting remains at 0.22 opacity.
Non-legal empty cubes remain invisible.
Active project-owned version references were checked against Bundle version
1.2.8.
The CSS and JavaScript cache-busters in index.html were checked as 1.2.8.
All four required files were checked for presence and nonzero size.
The final ZIP was checked to contain exactly:

    index.html
    Othello.css

2026-08-29 — Version 1.2.4 — Commit moves on mouse-up instead of mouse-down

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.2.3
----------------------
The user reported that clicking a legal move to begin dragging/rotating the
cube was immediately interpreted as a game move. The requested behavior is
that a game move should be made only on the unclick/mouse-up action.

CHANGE
------
Legal-move selection is now committed on pointer-up rather than pointer-down.

On pointer-down, the code records:
    - the pointer's starting position;
    - which legal move, if any, is under the pointer.

Pointer movement greater than 5 pixels marks the interaction as a drag. A
drag therefore does not make a move when the pointer is released.

On pointer-up:
    - if there was no meaningful drag;
    - and the original target was a currently legal move;
    - that legal move is committed.

Pointer-cancel also clears the pending interaction without making a move.

This preserves normal click-to-move behavior while allowing pointer-down and
dragging to rotate the cube without accidentally making a game move.

The existing hover/highlighting behavior remains unchanged:
    - legal moves use 0.09 opacity;
    - the hovered legal move is golden;
    - only one legal move can be highlighted;
    - non-legal empty cubes remain invisible.

No game rules, sphere appearance, cube appearance, cube spacing, camera
behavior, rotation controls, or page layout was changed.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
This is Bundle Version 1.2.4. All active project-owned version references and
HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters were synchronized to 1.2.4. Historical log
entries retain their original release versions.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
JavaScript syntax was checked with Node.

2026-08-29 — Version 1.2.3 — Legal-move hover highlighting and click targeting

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.2.2
----------------------
The user requested legal-move cubes at 0.09 opacity. They also reported that
clicking a legal move cube could fail unless the pointer landed on an outside
face. The user requested a hover/highlighting system so a legal move cube
glows golden when hovered.

CHANGE
------
Legal-move cubes now use opacity 0.09 in their normal state.

A legal-move hover state was added. When the pointer is over a legal move:
    - that one legal cube becomes golden;
    - its opacity increases to 0.20;
    - its scale increases slightly to 1.08.

Only one cube can be highlighted at a time. Only positions that are currently
legal moves can be highlighted. When the pointer leaves the rendering area,
the highlight is cleared.

The renderer now tracks the current legal move positions explicitly. Pointer
raycasts search all intersected cell meshes and select the nearest intersected
legal move rather than requiring the legal cube to be the first intersection.
This is important now that adjacent cube edges share faces and transparent
cell geometry can overlap along the viewing ray.

Clicking a legal move therefore uses the same legal-position filtering as
hover highlighting. Clicking a non-legal cube still does nothing.

Non-legal empty cubes remain completely invisible.

No game rules, sphere appearance, camera behavior, rotation controls, board
size, or page layout was changed.

-----------------------

entries retain their original release versions.

---------------------------

The highlight state is limited to one current legal position.

2026-08-29 — Version 1.1.2 — Fit the game within the normal page viewport

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.1.1
----------------------
The overall layout was working well, but on a normal Chrome page load the game
extended below the bottom of the browser viewport. The user could make the
layout fit by putting Chrome into Fullscreen mode, but the normal page view
should fit without requiring Fullscreen.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
The desktop layout gave the main area a minimum height based on the viewport
and also gave the game area a fixed 650px minimum height. The page header and
footer then consumed additional vertical space, so the total page height could
exceed the available browser viewport.

REPAIR
------
The page body now uses a three-row grid:

    header / flexible main area / footer

The main area is allowed to shrink to the remaining viewport height, and the
desktop game area no longer has the fixed 650px minimum height.

The Three.js renderer already sizes itself from the actual #scene dimensions,
so allowing the game area to use the available height makes the rendered board
fit the page without changing the game logic, camera settings, controls,
rules, or piece geometry.

The mobile 70vh game minimum remains unchanged.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
The Bundle is Version 1.1.2 across all four files. The HTML CSS and JavaScript
cache-busters are also 1.1.2.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
JavaScript syntax was checked with Node.
The CSS layout changes were inspected for the intended body/main/#game sizing
rules.
Active project-owned version references were checked against Bundle version
1.1.2.
The CSS and JavaScript cache-busters in index.html were checked as 1.1.2.
All four required files were checked for presence and nonzero size.
The final ZIP was checked to contain exactly:

    index.html
    Othello.css
    Othello.js
    Othello_Log.txt

Release is blocked if any required file is empty or if any active Bundle
version reference is stale or mixed.

2026-08-29 — Version 1.1.0 — Development/release workflow established

WORKFLOW / PROCESS
------------------
Beginning with Version 1.1.0, Cube Othello development will follow the
release discipline established during the related Visualizer project.

The standard workflow is:

1. Start every change from a known-working Bundle.
2. Make one deliberate, focused change at a time whenever practical.
3. Increment the Bundle version for each released change.
4. Treat all required files as one synchronized Bundle. Every file's
   project-owned version reference must match the current Bundle version.
5. Preserve the complete historical development log. Each release should
   document the intended change, actual change, user feedback when relevant,
   root cause when repairing a problem, repair, and testing/release results.
6. When a bug is found, identify and document the actual root cause rather
   than simply layering another speculative patch onto the code.
7. If a working source is compromised by an experimental edit, restore from
   the most recent known-good source and reapply the intended change cleanly.
8. Verify the actual files being released, not just the intended edits.
9. Check JavaScript syntax and perform appropriate structural/content checks
   before sending a Bundle.
10. Every required file must exist and have nonzero, expected content.
    A missing, 0-byte, or unexpectedly empty required file is a
    release-blocking error.
11. Check all project-owned version references across the Bundle for stale,
    mixed, or inconsistent versions. A version mismatch is a
    release-blocking error.
12. If HTML/CSS/JavaScript cache-busters are used, they must match the
    current Bundle version.
13. Verify the final ZIP itself and confirm that it contains exactly the
    intended Bundle files, with no missing or unintended files.
14. Do not casually redesign working functionality. Preserve the known-good
    behavior unless a requested change specifically calls for modifying it.

RELEASE PRINCIPLE
-----------------
A Bundle is not considered ready to send until the source files and the final
ZIP have passed the release-integrity checks above. The goal is to make each
version a traceable, reproducible step from a known-working baseline.

2026-08-23 — Version 1.0.0 — Initial baseline

INTENDED CHANGES
- Establish a new static-web project for a three-dimensional Othello experiment.
- Use a 4×4×4 board as the first playable version.
- Use index.html as the page entry point for the project directory.
- Use Othello.css and Othello.js for project-specific CSS and JavaScript.
- Establish this log as the project's development history.

CURRENT RULE/INTERFACE BASELINE
- Board contains 64 positions.
- Opening position contains eight spheres in the central 2×2×2 block: four black and four white.
- A move may capture in any of the 26 neighboring directions in 3D.
- Legal moves are displayed as translucent gray cubes.
- Pieces are spheres rather than discs.
- Captured spheres change color rather than physically flipping.
- Clicking a highlighted cube selects a move.
- Mouse drag rotates the view through Three.js OrbitControls.
- Mouse wheel/pinch zooms.
- W/S, A/D, and Q/E provide keyboard rotation controls.
- R resets the view.
- A New Game button resets the position.

IMPLEMENTATION NOTES
- WebGL is not a separate project file. The browser provides the WebGL API.
- Three.js is the 3D rendering library.
- Three.js is referenced as a pinned CDN dependency (r0.185.0) rather than copied into this initial bundle.
- No PHP, database, login system, or server-side code is used.
- No AI opponent is included yet.
- No persistent win/loss profile is included yet.
- The visual design is intentionally a baseline and is expected to evolve.

KNOWN LIMITATIONS / NEXT STEPS
- Verify the opening geometry and 26-direction rules through actual play.
- Test clicking and keyboard controls in Chrome on the Chromebook.
- Improve the visual representation of the board structure.
- Add clearer move/capture animation.
- Decide whether the board should visibly "assemble" as the game progresses.
- Evaluate camera/rotation controls after hands-on testing.
- Add a computer opponent only after the core game interaction is stable.
- Consider local statistics later.
- Do not redesign working functionality casually; use focused version-to-version changes.

TESTING
- Initial bundle has not yet been live-tested by the user.
- Browser/device observations should be added after testing.

2026-09-02 — Version 1.6.6 — Last-move marching-ants marker

INTENDED CHANGES
- Make the most recently played move visually obvious.
- Add a marching-ants style animated outline around the cell where the last move was made.
- Keep the marker visible after ordinary moves, including when the move occupies an already-partially-filled region of the board.
- Suppress the marker when the move completes the full 4×4×4 board, as requested.
- Preserve all existing game rules, scoring, coordinate entry, clicking, orientation markers, and controls.

ACTUAL CHANGES
- Added a last-move marker state keyed to the coordinate of the most recently played move.
- Added a thin dashed box outline around that cell using Three.js LineDashedMaterial and EdgesGeometry.
- The dash offset is continuously animated so the dashes appear to march around the cell.
- The marker is recreated at the new move location after every legal move.
- If the board becomes completely occupied (64 cells), the last-move marker is suppressed rather than displayed for the final move.
- New Game clears the last-move marker.
- Updated the active project version and HTML/CSS/JavaScript cache-busters to 1.6.6.

DESIGN NOTES
- The outline is intentionally slightly larger than the 1.25-unit cell so it remains visible around the occupied cube/piece.
- The marker uses a white dashed line at high opacity to distinguish the most recent move without adding another solid object to the board.
- The animation is purely visual and does not affect board state or move validation.

TESTING / RELEASE CHECKS
- JavaScript syntax check: PASS.
- Verified last-move marker creation is called after a legal move.
- Verified marker is cleared on New Game.
- Verified marker is suppressed when occupiedCount reaches 64.
- Verified active version references are synchronized at 1.6.6.
- Verified Bundle contains exactly index.html, Othello.css, Othello.js, and Othello_Log.txt.
- Verified all four Bundle files are nonzero.

======================================================================

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Add an optional sidebar toggle named “Auto-Rotate” that slowly rotates the
main 4×4×4 cube like a globe. The option must be OFF by default and automatic
rotation must stop whenever the cursor is hovering over the cube.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Added an unchecked Auto-Rotate checkbox to the sidebar. When enabled, the
main cube rotates slowly around its Y axis. Automatic rotation pauses while
the pointer is over visible cube geometry and resumes when the pointer leaves
the cube. Manual drag and keyboard rotation remain available.

The hover test uses the existing visible board geometry rather than changing
any board or game-state objects. The toggle does not persist between page
loads, so the default is OFF on each load.

2026-09-02 — Version 1.6.12 — Restore continuous Auto-Rotate hover detection
======================================================================

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Fix the Auto-Rotate behavior so the cube stops rotating when the cursor is
over the visible 3D cube, rather than only after a move or pointerdown event.

USER FEEDBACK
-------------
The user reported that Auto-Rotate no longer stops when hovering over the
cube. It does stop when making a move, but then resumes only when the cursor
leaves the 3D space.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
The released 1.6.11 JavaScript contained the cube hover raycast in the
pointerdown handler, but the corresponding continuous hover update was missing
from the pointermove handler. As a result, hovering was not refreshed as the
cursor moved onto or off the cube. A pointerdown could set hoveringOverCube to
true, which explains why making a move could appear to pause rotation, while
pointerleave reset it and allowed rotation to resume.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Restored the cube-geometry raycast to the pointermove handler. On every
pointermove, the code now checks the currently visible cube geometry and sets
hoveringOverCube based on whether the ray intersects it.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
No changes were made to rotation speeds, Spacebar Auto-Rotate control,
