2026-09-02 — Version 1.7.0 — Add player pass message
======================================================================

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Handle the rare Othello situation in which the player whose turn would be
next has no legal move. Display a centered message reading either
"Black passes" or "White passes" and require the player to click the message
before continuing to the next move.

USER FEEDBACK / TESTING CONSTRAINT
----------------------------------
The user requested this behavior and noted that a pass is rare enough that
there was no known test sequence available to them. The user asked whether it
was reasonable to trust the implementation rather than manually reproduce a
pass during normal play.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Added a pass-message overlay inside the game area. When a completed move
leaves the opponent with no legal move, the opponent is identified as the
passing player and the message is shown. The current player remains the
active player, because that player is the one who receives the next move.
The overlay blocks interaction with the board until it is clicked.

The pass message is only shown when the current player still has at least one
legal move. If neither player has a legal move, no pass message is shown,
leaving the existing end-of-game behavior unchanged.

The overlay is also dismissed when New Game is selected. A defensive guard in
playMove prevents a move from being made while the pass message is active.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
No changes were made to the game board geometry, game rules, scoring,
coordinate-entry format, move validation, Auto-Rotate behavior, Spacebar
shortcut, hover-to-pause behavior, last-move marker, orientation markers,
manual rotation controls, or starting position.

------------------------

- JavaScript syntax check passed.
- Confirmed all four Bundle files are present and nonzero.
- Confirmed final ZIP contains exactly index.html, Othello.css, Othello.js,
  and Othello_Log.txt.
- Independently exercised the 4×4×4 Othello move logic with a deterministic
  legal-move simulation and found a concrete pass sequence: after 55 legal
  moves, White has no legal move while Black does, so White passes and Black
  continues.
- The pass sequence was used to validate the underlying pass-condition logic
  without requiring the user to discover a rare position manually.
- Confirmed the pass overlay is dismissed by click and that New Game clears
  any active pass message.

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------

legal move, including positions where one or more empty cubes remain.

the message before continuing. Keep the final board fully interactive for
viewing and rotation after the message is dismissed.

-------------------------

game. Near the end of that game, exactly one empty cube remained, but neither
color could legally play there. The existing legal-move highlighting correctly

Othello end condition and requested an explicit end-of-game indicator.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Added a Game Complete state using the existing centered message overlay.
After a move, if the opponent has no legal moves, the game now checks whether
the current player has any legal moves. If the current player also has no
legal moves, the overlay displays "Game Complete" instead of a pass message.
This applies whether the board is full or empty positions remain.

The game-complete overlay must be clicked through before the player can\continue interacting with the game. Once dismissed, no new moves are accepted
because neither player has a legal move, but the cube, pieces, camera, and
manual/automatic rotation remain available so the final position can be
examined.

A defensive game-complete guard was added to the move handler. New Game
clears the game-complete state through the existing overlay dismissal path.

======================================================================
2026-09-02 — Version 1.7.3 — Coordinate-entry Backspace and separator repair
======================================================================

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Improve the guided coordinate-entry field when it receives keyboard focus
through Tab or when the player backs through an entered coordinate.

USER FEEDBACK / REQUEST
-----------------------
The user reported two related coordinate-entry problems:

1. If Tab focuses the coordinate field while a complete coordinate is already
   present, the browser may select the whole coordinate. Pressing Backspace
   should clear the field, but previously nothing happened.

2. If Backspace is used to remove an automatically inserted hyphen, the user
   can become stuck because the coordinate-entry handler expects the hyphen to
   be inserted automatically and the '-' key is also a global zoom-out
   shortcut.

The user asked whether the separators should be changed to slashes or commas
and whether Backspace could clear a fully selected coordinate.

DESIGN DECISION
---------------
Keep the existing hyphen separators.

The hyphen itself is not the underlying problem: while the coordinate field is
being edited, its key handler already prevents the global '-' zoom shortcut
from acting. The actual issue is that the custom Backspace behavior could
leave the field in an intermediate state in which the automatic separator
could no longer be reconstructed.

Keeping the established A-1-W coordinate notation avoids an unnecessary
format change while fixing the input-state behavior directly.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.7.3 changes only the coordinate-entry Backspace handling:

- Detects a non-empty text selection in the coordinate field. Backspace now
  clears the entire field when the whole coordinate has been selected.
- Backspace continues to be intercepted by the coordinate-entry handler, so
  the global zoom-out shortcut does not interfere while the field is focused.
- Backspacing through the coordinate now removes one coordinate component at
  a time while preserving the automatic hyphen structure.
- The player therefore cannot get stuck with a missing separator; typing the
  next coordinate component automatically restores the appropriate hyphen.
- The existing hyphen-based A-1-W notation remains unchanged.

BEHAVIOR
--------
Examples:

    A-2-Y + whole-field selection + Backspace  -> empty field
    A-2-Y + Backspace                          -> A-2-
    A-2-  + Backspace                          -> A-
    A-  + Backspace                            -> empty field
    A- + 2                                     -> A-2-
    A-2- + Y                                   -> A-2-Y

No change was made to the coordinate notation, legal-move validation,
Unavailable Move message, pass handling, Game Complete behavior, board,
rotation, highlighting, or other game rules.

TESTING / RELEASE CHECKS
------------------------
- Version references updated to 1.7.3.
- JavaScript syntax check passed.
- Confirmed full-selection Backspace clears the coordinate field.
- Confirmed Backspace removes coordinate components without leaving a missing
  separator state.
- Confirmed automatic hyphens remain generated by the existing entry flow.
- Confirmed the global '-' zoom shortcut remains available outside the
  coordinate field and is not invoked by Backspace while editing the field.
- Confirmed the existing Unavailable Move behavior remains in place.
- Confirmed the Game Complete coordinate-submission guard remains in place.
- All four Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly: index.html, Othello.css, Othello.js,
  Othello_Log.txt.

Version 1.8.0 — Move Sequence foundation
Date: 2026-09-02

INTENDED CHANGE
- Begin the 1.8.x series by adding a functional, copyable move-sequence record.
- Add a sidebar button labeled “Move Sequence”.
- Show the current game sequence in a non-editable field that can be selected/copied.
- Represent normal moves by their three-dimensional coordinates and represent passes explicitly as PASS.

DESIGN DECISION
- The move record is intentionally simple and human-readable: a comma-separated sequence of coordinate values and PASS entries.
- Player color and move numbers are not stored because the rules and starting player determine them during replay. A PASS entry preserves the information needed when the same player moves again.
- This establishes a foundation for a future saved .txt game record and for future systems that can replay the sequence to reconstruct any position in the game.

ACTUAL CHANGE
- Added the Move Sequence sidebar button.
- Added a Move Sequence overlay containing a read-only textarea. The sequence can be selected and copied but cannot be edited.
- Added Copy and Close controls. Clicking outside the sequence panel also closes the viewer.
- Added an internal moveSequenceHistory array.
- Every successful move appends its coordinate notation (for example A-3-Y).
- When a player has no legal move and passes, PASS is appended to the sequence.
- New Game clears the sequence.
- The displayed sequence updates immediately after moves and passes.
- The underlying game rules, move validation, coordinate entry, pass handling, Game Complete behavior, Auto-Rotate, Show 3-Axis, last-move marker, board geometry, and existing controls were otherwise preserved.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
- No Undo functionality was added in this release.
- No saved .txt file generation was added yet.
- No player/color or explicit move-number fields were added to the sequence because they can be derived during replay.

TESTING / RELEASE CHECKS
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- Confirmed the Move Sequence control and viewer elements are present.
- Confirmed the sequence field is read-only.
- Confirmed successful moves append coordinate notation.
- Confirmed PASS is explicitly recorded when a pass occurs.
- Confirmed New Game clears the move sequence.
- Confirmed active version references and cache-busters are synchronized to 1.8.0.
- All four required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Final ZIP will be checked to contain exactly index.html, Othello.css, Othello.js, and Othello_Log.txt.

Version 1.8.3 — Undo Move
Date: 2026-09-02

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.8.2
----------------------
The user confirmed that Version 1.8.2 was working well and designated it as
the current fallback version. The next requested change was to add an Undo
Move button, using the established coordinate-based move sequence as the
foundation.

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
- Add an Undo Move button to the sidebar.
- Use the existing move history as the basis for reversing moves.
- Preserve explicit PASS entries and correct turn handling when undoing.
- Keep the change focused and avoid redesigning existing game behavior.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
- Added an "Undo Move" button to the sidebar.
- The button is disabled when there are no moves to undo.
- Each legal move now records a complete pre-move game snapshot containing:
    - the full 4×4×4 board state;
    - the current player;
    - the move sequence, including any PASS entry already present; and
    - the last-move marker position.
- Undo restores the most recent snapshot and removes the corresponding move
  from the active move sequence.
- If the move being undone was the move that caused an opponent PASS or
  Game Complete state, the overlay is dismissed and the game returns to the
  exact pre-move state. This also removes the PASS entry that was generated
  by the undone move.
- Undo refreshes pieces, occupied/legal surfaces, legal-move state, score/turn
  status, last-move marker, coordinate entry, and Undo-button availability.
- New Game clears the undo history as well as the move sequence.
- Added a small disabled-button style so an unavailable Undo control is
  visually distinct without changing the sidebar layout.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- The underlying Othello move rules and legal-move calculations were not
  changed.
- The coordinate system was not changed.
- The Move Sequence format remains compact coordinates separated by comma-space,
  with PASS used for passes.
- Move Sequence remains read-only and copyable.
- Auto-Rotate, Show 3-Axis, camera/view controls, last-move marker behavior,
  board geometry, and Game Complete scoring were not intentionally redesigned.
- No external saved-game file format was added in this release.

UNDO DESIGN NOTE
----------------
The Undo history is intentionally an internal game-state history rather than
a reconstruction from only the coordinate text. This makes Undo reliable even
when a move flips multiple pieces and when PASS/Game Complete state is involved.
The portable Move Sequence remains the simpler coordinate/PASS record discussed
for future analysis and saved-game functionality.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
This is Bundle Version 1.8.3. Active project-owned version references and the
HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters were synchronized to 1.8.3. Historical log
entries remain unchanged.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- Confirmed Undo is disabled on a new game.
- Confirmed each legal move creates one undo history entry.
- Confirmed Undo restores the board and current-player state to the previous
  position.
- Confirmed the move sequence is shortened when a move is undone.
- Confirmed PASS entries are restored/removed consistently when undoing across
  a pass-triggering move.
- Confirmed Game Complete overlay state is cleared when undoing the move that
  triggered game completion.
- Confirmed New Game clears Undo history.
- Confirmed all four required Bundle files are present and nonzero.
- Confirmed active version references and HTML cache-busters are 1.8.3.
- Final ZIP checked for integrity and exact four-file contents.


2026-09-02 — Version 1.8.4 — Add Redo Move

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Add a Redo Move button to the sidebar so a player can move forward again after
using Undo Move. This supports accidental undo recovery and studying a game by
stepping backward and forward through its history.

CHANGE
------
Version 1.8.4 adds a disabled-by-default Redo Move button beside the existing
Undo Move button.

Undo now places the current state onto the redo history before restoring the
previous state. Redo performs the inverse operation: it places the current state
onto the undo history and restores the next state from redo history.

The redo history is cleared whenever a new move is made after undoing, which
prevents an obsolete future branch from being replayed. New Game clears both
undo and redo histories.

Snapshots now preserve pass-message and game-complete state as well as board,
turn, move sequence, and last-move marker state. This allows Undo/Redo to move
through those states consistently when they occur.

No game rules, coordinate notation, Move Sequence format, board geometry, camera
behavior, or existing controls were intentionally changed.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
This is Bundle Version 1.8.4. Active project-owned version references and the
HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters were synchronized to 1.8.4. Historical log
entries retain their original release versions.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
JavaScript syntax was checked with Node.
Confirmed index.html contains the Redo Move button and synchronized 1.8.4
version references.
Confirmed Undo/Redo history structures and handlers are present.
Confirmed new moves clear redo history.
Confirmed New Game clears both histories.
Confirmed all four required files are present and nonzero.
Confirmed the final ZIP contains exactly:
    index.html
    Othello.css
    Othello.js
    Othello_Log.txt

Release is blocked if any required file is empty or if any active Bundle
version reference is stale or mixed.


2026-09-02 — Version 1.8.5 — Repair Pass/Game Complete overlay click-through

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.8.4
----------------------
The user successfully tested Undo and Redo through a complete game. During
that test, however, the end-of-game Game Complete overlay could not be clicked
through and remained on screen until the page was reloaded. The user also found
a real Black Pass situation and reported that the pass message could not be
clicked through to continue the game.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
The shared dismissPassMessage() handler was incorrectly referencing a variable
named `snapshot`, which does not exist in that function's scope. The handler
therefore threw an error when the Pass/Game Complete overlay was clicked instead
of clearing the transient message state. Because the overlay remained visible,
it blocked further interaction with the board.

REPAIR
------
Version 1.8.5 repairs dismissPassMessage() so a click on the Pass/Game Complete

2026-09-03 — Version 1.8.8 — Load Move Sequence

USER REQUEST ON 1.8.7
----------------------
The user requested a way to paste a previously copied Cube Othello Move
Sequence and have the game reconstruct the corresponding board position. If a
game is already in progress, the user should be warned that loading the new
sequence will replace the current game. The loaded sequence should use the
existing coordinate format and explicit PASS entries.

CHANGE
------
Version 1.8.8 adds a Load Sequence button to the sidebar. It opens an editable
Move Sequence input where a copied sequence can be pasted and loaded. The input
accepts comma-separated coordinates and the explicit PASS token.

The loader starts from the standard initial 4×4×4 position and replays the
sequence according to the existing Cube Othello rules. It validates coordinates,
move legality, PASS placement, and attempts to continue after Game Complete.
The reconstructed position becomes the active board state. The imported move
history is also placed into the existing Undo/Redo history model, so Undo and
Redo can be used after loading a sequence.

If a game already contains moves, loading a sequence first asks for confirmation
that the current game will be lost. Invalid sequences are rejected without
replacing the existing game.

PASS is treated as an explicit turn event: it is valid only when the current
player has legal moves and the opposing player has none. A required PASS cannot
be skipped by immediately supplying another coordinate.

The Move Sequence export format itself remains unchanged:
    D-2-X, D-4-W, B-4-X
with PASS used when a player passes. No player/color field is added because the
starting player and PASS events determine the turn sequence.

No game rules, coordinate notation, existing Move Sequence viewer/copy
behavior, Undo/Redo controls, board geometry, camera behavior, or Auto-Rotate
behavior were intentionally changed.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
This is Bundle Version 1.8.8. Active project-owned version references and the
HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters were synchronized to 1.8.8. Historical version
numbers in the development log remain unchanged.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- Confirmed Load Sequence controls and overlay elements are present.
- Confirmed coordinate and PASS parsing/validation paths are present.
- Confirmed an in-progress game is protected by a confirmation prompt before
  replacement.
- Confirmed invalid sequences restore the pre-load game state.
- Confirmed imported moves populate the existing Undo history and clear Redo.
- Confirmed active version references and HTML cache-busters are 1.8.8.
- Confirmed all four required Bundle files are present and nonzero.
- Final ZIP checked for integrity and exact four-file contents.

2026-09-03 — Version 1.9.8 — Dynamic blue legal-move opacity

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.9.6 / 1.9.7
------------------------------
The user confirmed that Version 1.9.6 is the last known working version after testing
Version 1.9.7. Version 1.9.7 made the blue legal-move cubes too opaque during the early
stages of the game, allowing too much of the green occupied center to show through.
The remaining problem in 1.9.6 is the opposite: near the end of the game, the blue legal
moves can become difficult to see against the increasingly occupied green structure.

REQUEST
-------
Improve late-game blue legal-move visibility without darkening the green occupied
surface, because the green opacity must remain low enough to preserve visibility of the
black and white spheres inside the occupied cubes.

CHANGE
------
Version 1.9.8 replaces the fixed 0.15 opacity of the normal merged blue legal-move
surface with dynamic opacity based on the number of occupied board positions.

The normal legal-move surface remains the darker blue #2f80ed. Its opacity now changes
linearly from 0.10 at the initial 8 occupied positions to 0.20 at a completely occupied
64-position board. This provides a subtler legal-move appearance early in the game and
progressively stronger visibility as the board becomes crowded later in the game.

The green occupied surface remains unchanged at 0.50 opacity. The highlighted legal-move
surface remains bright yellow at 0.15 opacity. Non-legal empty cubes remain completely
invisible.

No game rules, move/capture logic, mouse-up move behavior, coordinate entry, undo/redo,
camera behavior, rotation controls, mobile layout, or other appearance behavior was
intentionally changed.

2026-09-03 — Version 1.9.9 — Intensify the dynamic legal-move opacity curve

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.9.8
----------------------
The user confirmed Version 1.9.8 is working correctly, including the dynamic
legal-move opacity. The user requested that the opacity remain exactly the same
at the beginning of the game, but increase more steeply and noticeably as the
game progresses because the 1.9.8 change is too subtle.

CHANGE
------
Version 1.9.9 keeps the same starting opacity of 0.10 and the same maximum
opacity of 0.20, but changes the progression from a linear curve to a stronger
nonlinear curve.

The normalized game-progress value is now raised to the 2.5 power before it is
used to interpolate between the minimum and maximum opacity. This keeps the
early-game appearance essentially at the same starting level while making the
opacity increase progressively more intense later in the game.

The legal-move surface remains blue (#2f80ed).
The occupied green surface remains unchanged at 0.50 opacity.
The yellow highlighted legal cube remains unchanged at 0.15 opacity.
Non-legal empty cubes remain completely invisible.

directly visible along the pointer ray can be highlighted or selected. A legal
cube hidden behind an occupied green cube or another non-legal cell must no
longer respond to hover or clicks through the cube in front of it.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
The existing per-cell meshes are now kept as invisible ray-casting targets for
all 64 board positions, including occupied and non-legal cells. Because the
transparent targets have no visible appearance, the board visuals are unchanged.

Ray-casting now examines the nearest cell intersection only. If that frontmost
cell is a legal move, it can highlight and be selected. If the frontmost cell
is occupied or non-legal, the ray produces no legal highlight/selection even if
a different legal move exists farther behind it.

The same frontmost-cell test is used for both pointer hover and pointer-down
move selection, so the two interaction paths remain consistent.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
No changes were made to game rules, scoring, move validation, coordinate entry,
camera behavior, rotation controls, board size, occupied-surface geometry,
legal-surface appearance, blue opacity progression, orientation markers, or page
layout. Version 1.9.11 remains the known-good visual fallback.

FALLBACK
--------
Version 1.9.11 is the fallback if the new directly-visible legal-cell
ray-casting behavior causes any unwanted interaction or visual regression.

TESTING / RELEASE CHECKS
------------------------
- Active Bundle version references updated to 1.9.12.
- HTML CSS and JavaScript cache-busters updated to 1.9.12.
- CSS and JavaScript active header markers updated to 1.9.12.
- Confirmed all 64 cell meshes remain available as invisible ray-casting targets.
- Confirmed ray-casting uses only the nearest cell intersection for legal
  highlight/selection.
- Confirmed both pointer hover and pointer-down selection use the same
  frontmost-cell test.
- Confirmed Version 1.9.11's blue opacity progression remains unchanged.
- JavaScript syntax check performed with Node.
- All four required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly: index.html, Othello.css, Othello.js, Othello_Log.txt.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.10.1 starts from the known-good Version 1.9.16 source. OrbitControls
has been replaced with Three.js ArcballControls. ArcballControls uses a virtual
trackball interaction intended to provide more intuitive, consistent camera
movement and full touch support, without the polar-angle restriction that
limited the previous OrbitControls interaction.

Rotation remains enabled, while panning, double-click/tap focus, and the
Arcball gizmos are disabled. Zoom remains enabled with the existing minimum
and maximum distances. Keyboard rotation, game rules, move selection, legal
move appearance, board geometry, opacity behavior, orientation markers, and
other game functionality are otherwise unchanged from Version 1.9.16.

The existing pointer click/drag detection remains in place so a drag does not
accidentally make a move, while ArcballControls handles the actual camera
rotation. The scene canvas now explicitly uses touch-action: none so touch
gestures are handled by the 3D controls.

NO-CHANGE AREAS

2026-09-03 — Version 1.10.3 — Prevent iPhone Safari viewport selection during 3D drag
======================================================================

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.10.2
------------------------
The user confirmed that Version 1.10.2 is working very well, but reported
that on iPhone, tapping and dragging in the 3D viewport can sometimes make
Safari interpret the gesture as a browser selection gesture. The result is
that the 3D viewport appears to become selected/highlighted instead of the
interaction remaining entirely within the game.

ROOT CAUSE / OBSERVATION
-------------------------
The 3D canvas already uses touch-action: none, and ArcballControls also sets
touchAction to none on its DOM element. However, Safari has separate text/content
selection and touch-callout behaviors that are not fully disabled by
touch-action alone. CSS user-selection and WebKit drag/callout behavior therefore
need to be explicitly disabled on the interactive canvas.

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Prevent browser content selection, long-press callouts, and native dragging on
the 3D viewport canvas while preserving normal Cube Othello tapping, dragging,
Arcball rotation, and zoom behavior.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.10.3 adds the following to #scene canvas:

    -webkit-user-select: none;
    user-select: none;
    -webkit-touch-callout: none;
    -webkit-user-drag: none;

The existing touch-action: none remains unchanged. These rules are limited to
the 3D canvas so normal browser behavior elsewhere on the page is not disabled.
No JavaScript interaction logic was changed in this release.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No game rules, scoring, move validation, or move sequence behavior changed.
- No board geometry, legal/occupied surface appearance, or opacity behavior changed.
- No Arcball rotation, zoom, gizmo visibility, or keyboard rotation behavior changed.
- No page layout or non-canvas text-selection behavior was intentionally changed.

FALLBACK
--------
Version 1.9.16 remains the known-good fallback. Version 1.10.2 remains the
previous known-good Arcball version if this focused iPhone interaction change
causes any unexpected behavior.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
This is Bundle Version 1.10.3. Active project-owned version references and
the HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters are synchronized to 1.10.3. Historical
log entries retain their original release versions.

TESTING / RELEASE CHECKS
------------------------
- Confirmed #scene canvas retains touch-action: none.
- Confirmed -webkit-user-select: none and user-select: none are present on the canvas.
- Confirmed -webkit-touch-callout: none and -webkit-user-drag: none are present on the canvas.
- Confirmed no JavaScript interaction logic was changed.
- JavaScript syntax check performed with Node.
- All four required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Active version references and HTML cache-busters checked for 1.10.3.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly: index.html, Othello.css, Othello.js, Othello_Log.txt.



======================================================================
2026-09-03 — Version 1.10.4 — Make the entire visible legal cube selectable
======================================================================

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.10.3 / 1.10.2
----------------------------------
The user confirmed that the recent Arcball rotation work is working very well.
The user then reported that the current legal-move selection behavior is
slightly too restrictive: even when a legal cube is golden/highlighted, a tap
must land on an outward-facing side of that cube. The desired behavior is for
the whole legal cube to be selectable, provided that the legal cube is not
behind an occupied green cube from the current viewing direction.

ANALYSIS
--------
This request is not contradictory to the existing anti-raycast-through-green-
cube behavior. The existing getFrontmostLegalHit() logic intentionally uses
the nearest cell intersection and rejects the move when that frontmost cell is
not legal. That preserves the rule that a legal cube cannot be selected through
a nearer occupied/non-legal cell.

The separate restriction was caused by the invisible cell meshes using the
default MeshBasicMaterial face side. Three.js Raycaster performs mesh
intersection testing against faces, and back-face intersections are not
detected unless the material uses THREE.DoubleSide. Consequently, a legal cell
could fail to register when the pointer ray approached it from a direction
that hit the back side of the invisible cell geometry first. Three.js documents
that back-face intersections are not detected unless the material side is
THREE.DoubleSide, while intersection results remain sorted nearest-first.

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Keep the existing nearest-cell occlusion rule, but allow the invisible
raycasting cell geometry to be hit from either side. This makes the complete
legal cube selectable from any visible direction that is not blocked by a
nearer occupied/non-legal cube.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.10.4 changes the material used by createCell() to:

    side: THREE.DoubleSide

No change was made to getFrontmostLegalHit(). The nearest cell still determines
whether a legal move is directly selectable, so a legal cube cannot be
selected through a nearer occupied/non-legal cube. The yellow legal highlight
remains a visual surface only and does not alter the raycasting rule.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No game rules, scoring, move validation, or move sequence behavior changed.
- No Arcball rotation, zoom, gizmo visibility, or iPhone selection fix changed.
- No legal/occupied surface colors or opacity behavior changed.
- No change was made to the nearest-cell occlusion logic.

FALLBACK
--------
Version 1.9.16 remains the known-good fallback. Version 1.10.2 remains the
previous known-good Arcball version, and Version 1.10.3 contains the focused
iPhone Safari selection fix.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
This is Bundle Version 1.10.4. Active project-owned version references and
the HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters are synchronized to 1.10.4. Historical
log entries retain their original release versions.

TESTING / RELEASE CHECKS
------------------------
- Confirmed createCell() uses side: THREE.DoubleSide.
- Confirmed getFrontmostLegalHit() remains unchanged and still uses the nearest
  cell intersection as the visibility/occlusion gate.
- JavaScript syntax check performed with Node.
- All four required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Active version references and HTML cache-busters checked for 1.10.4.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly: index.html, Othello.css, Othello.js, Othello_Log.txt.


======================================================================
2026-09-03 — Version 1.12.0 — Add game-start splash and future game settings
======================================================================

USER REQUEST / DECISION
-----------------------
The user confirmed Version 1.10.4 as the fallback version and requested that the
next development series begin with Version 1.12.x. Before implementing the game
clock itself, the user requested a splash/start screen so a game no longer starts
immediately when the page loads.

The requested start screen asks the player to choose:
    - Opponent: Two Humans or Bot Opponent
    - Your Color: Black or White
    - Game Duration: 1 minute, 2 minutes, 5 minutes, 10 minutes, 30 minutes,
      or Unlimited

The current project does not yet contain a bot engine. Therefore, the Bot
Opponent option is shown as “coming soon” and is disabled rather than presenting
a mode that would appear functional but could not actually make moves. The
Two Humans mode remains the active playable choice. The color and duration
choices are captured in the game configuration for the later game-clock work.

GAME CLOCK DESIGN DISCUSSION TO PRESERVE FOR LATER
--------------------------------------------------
The user and assistant discussed the intended move-timer design before deciding
to build the splash screen first. The agreed design is:

1. Game Duration is a per-player clock, not a total-game clock. If 5 minutes is
   selected, Black receives a 5:00 clock and White independently receives a
   5:00 clock. Only the player whose turn it is counts down.

2. The available duration choices are:
       1 minute
       2 minutes
       5 minutes
       10 minutes
       30 minutes
       Unlimited

3. The timer should use actual elapsed time rather than relying solely on a
   one-second interval counter. This is especially important for iPhone/Safari,
   where browser timer throttling can otherwise make a clock inaccurate.

4. When a player's clock reaches zero before the game is otherwise complete,
   that player loses immediately and the other color wins. The timeout result
   uses a forfeit score of 64-0 or 0-64, not the board's actual piece count.

5. A timeout must be permanently recorded in Move Sequence so the reason for
   the result is recoverable later. The planned tokens are:
       TIMEOUT-BLACK
       TIMEOUT-WHITE

6. The existing resignation design remains analogous: resignation is recorded
   in Move Sequence and produces a 64-0 or 0-64 result.

7. When a game clock is in effect, Undo may undo only one move. Undo never adds
   time back to a player's clock. Whether Undo is used or not, the active
   player's clock continues to run while it is that player's turn.

8. There should be a setting to turn Undo off entirely.

9. Game settings should not be changeable during an active game. Changing the
   game duration or other start settings applies to a new game rather than
   silently altering an ongoing game.

10. The timer implementation should be integrated with the existing game-state
    architecture and carefully tested around moves, passes, resignation,
    timeout, Undo/Redo, New Game, and loaded Move Sequences.

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Add a dedicated splash screen at game load so the player chooses the initial
game configuration before entering the playable board. New Game should return
to the same splash screen rather than immediately resetting the board.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.12.0 adds an in-page start screen containing:
    - Opponent selection: Two Humans; Bot Opponent (coming soon/disabled)
    - Player color selection: Black or White
    - Game Duration dropdown with all six agreed choices
    - Start Game button

The selected opponent mode, human color, and game duration are stored in the
JavaScript game configuration so the upcoming timer and opponent work has a
defined configuration source. The actual countdown timer, bot engine, and Undo
setting are intentionally not implemented in this focused first 1.12.x change.

The New Game button now opens the splash screen. Confirming Start Game resets
the board and begins a new game with the selected configuration. The existing
board/game rules, Arcball rotation, selection behavior, resignation behavior,
Move Sequence behavior, and other gameplay systems are otherwise preserved.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No game-clock countdown was added yet.
- No timeout result was added yet.
- No bot opponent engine was added.
- No Undo/Redo timer behavior was changed.
- No new Undo on/off setting was added yet.
- No game rules or move legality changes were made.
- No Arcball rotation or iPhone interaction changes were made.

FALLBACK
--------
Version 1.10.4 remains the known-good fallback for the 1.12.x development
series.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
This is Bundle Version 1.12.0. Active project-owned version references and
the HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters are synchronized to 1.12.0. Historical
log entries retain their original release versions.

TESTING / RELEASE CHECKS
------------------------
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- Confirmed the start screen is present and initially visible.
- Confirmed the Start Game handler captures opponent, color, and duration
  selections and starts a fresh board.
- Confirmed New Game returns to the start screen instead of immediately
  resetting the current game.
- Confirmed all six game-duration choices are present.
- Confirmed Bot Opponent is clearly marked as coming soon and is disabled
  because no bot engine exists yet.
- Confirmed the existing canvas iPhone-selection CSS remains intact.
- Confirmed all four required Bundle files are present and nonzero.
- Confirmed active version references and HTML cache-busters are 1.12.0.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly: index.html, Othello.css, Othello.js,
  Othello_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity checked successfully.

The user tested Version 1.12.0 and approved the new game-start splash screen,

2. When "Two Humans" is selected, remove the "Your Color" choice. For the

   The color choice should remain available for the future bot/different-device

--------------------------------

- Duration choices: 1, 2, 5, 10, or 30 minutes, or Unlimited.
- A timed game gives each color its own independent clock.
- Only the player whose turn it is consumes time.
- Unlimited means no countdown/time-forfeit behavior.

  new game.
- If a player's clock reaches zero, that player loses immediately and the other
  color wins.
- A timeout result should use a forfeit score of 64-0 or 0-64.

  one-second interval ticks, to remain reliable when browser timers are
  throttled.
- When a game clock is active, Undo should never add time back to a player's
  clock.
- With a game clock active, Undo should be limited to one move.
- The clock continues running on the current player's turn whether or not Undo
  is used.
- There should be a setting to turn Undo off.
- Timer state and the one-move Undo rule should be integrated deliberately with
  the existing game-state/history model.

2026-09-03 — Version 1.15.0 — Add selectable 4×4×4, 6×6×6, and 8×8×8 boards
======================================================================

USER REQUEST / START OF 1.15.x SERIES
--------------------------------------
The user confirmed Version 1.14.1 is working well and designated it as the
known-good fallback before beginning the 1.15.x development series.

The first 1.15.x change is to add two larger board sizes. The start screen
should offer:

    4×4×4
    6×6×6
    8×8×8

with 4×4×4 selected by default.

The selected size must determine the actual playable cube, not merely change
the label. The existing Reversi rules, interaction, clocks, Undo/Redo, bot
plumbing, coordinate entry, and visual behavior should continue to operate on
the selected board size.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.15.0 adds a new Board Size fieldset to the game-start splash screen.
The three choices are 4×4×4, 6×6×6, and 8×8×8. The 4×4×4 option is checked by
default, preserving the existing default game configuration.

The board engine is now dynamically sized at game start. Selecting 6×6×6 or
8×8×8 rebuilds the cell/raycast grid and creates a correspondingly sized board
state before the new game begins.

The initial eight-piece opening position is now generated relative to the
center of the selected even-sized board. This preserves the existing central
2×2×2 opening geometry for all three supported sizes instead of using fixed
4×4×4 coordinates.

The 26-direction Reversi move-generation and flipping logic already uses the
board-size boundary checks, so it now operates over the full selected cube.
All board traversal loops continue to use the active SIZE value.

The main orientation marker, orientation-edge guide, camera distance limits,
and miniature coordinate guide are rebuilt or adjusted for the selected board
size. The camera starts farther back for the larger cubes so the full board
can remain visible.

Coordinate notation and coordinate-entry validation were generalized for the
larger boards. X-axis letters now use A through H as needed, Y-axis numbers
use 1 through 8 as needed, and the Z-axis coordinate letters use W, X, Y, Z,
A, B, C, D as needed. Existing 4×4×4 coordinates therefore retain their
original notation.

Move Sequence loading was also changed to use the same active-size coordinate
validation rather than a hard-coded 4×4×4 regular expression.

Timeout/forfeit score messages are now calculated from the active board size
rather than always reporting a 64-0 result. The full-board position count is
SIZE × SIZE × SIZE, so the supported totals are 64, 216, and 512 positions.

The visible version line now updates to show the active board size while
remaining Version 1.15.0.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules or capture directions were intentionally changed.
- The existing 26 possible capture directions remain in use.
- The existing central 2×2×2 opening color arrangement is preserved.
- No bot strategy change was made; the bot remains the existing random legal-
  move implementation.
- No online or separate-device multiplayer functionality was added.
- No game-clock behavior was intentionally changed.
- No Undo/Redo behavior was intentionally changed.
- No Arcball interaction, click/drag selection model, or mobile interaction
  redesign was made.
- Non-legal empty cubes remain completely invisible.
- The active Bundle continues to contain exactly four files.
- Version 1.14.1 remains the known-good fallback for the 1.15.x series.

COORDINATE NOTATION NOTE
------------------------
The original 4×4×4 notation uses A-D for X, 1-4 for Y, and W-Z for Z. To
support 6×6×6 and 8×8×8 without introducing a multi-character coordinate
component, the Z-axis continues through A-D after W-Z. Thus the Z-axis sequence
for the largest supported board is:

    W X Y Z A B C D

The three coordinate components remain distinguishable by their position in
the coordinate string, and existing 4×4×4 notation remains unchanged.

TESTING / RELEASE CHECKS
------------------------

- Confirmed the start screen contains all three board-size choices.
- Confirmed 4×4×4 is the default selected board size.
- Confirmed the active HTML title and cache-busters are synchronized to 1.15.0.
- Confirmed active source files contain no stale 1.14.1 version markers.
- Confirmed fixed 4×4×4 coordinate validation was removed from move entry and
  Move Sequence loading.

  totals.
- Confirmed the initial-position code now centers the starting 2×2×2 block for
  each supported board size.
- Confirmed the board rebuild creates SIZE³ ray-casting cells for the selected
  size.
- Confirmed the camera and orientation geometry are adjusted when the board
  size changes.
- Confirmed all four required Bundle files are present and nonzero.
- Confirmed the final ZIP contains exactly index.html, Reversi.css,
  Reversi.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity checked successfully.

version numbers and archived Othello release artifacts remain unchanged.

======================================================================
2026-09-03 — Version 1.15.1 — Repair legal-move selection on larger boards
======================================================================

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.15.0
------------------------
The new 6×6×6 and 8×8×8 boards rendered correctly and were playable in
principle. However, when using Bot Opponent and playing as White, the bot
made its opening Black move but the human player generally could not select
a legal move cube. One corner on a 6×6×6 test was selectable once.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
Version 1.15.0 retained the Version 1.9.12 pointer rule that treated the
nearest cell ray intersection as an absolute blocker. That behavior works
acceptably on the 4×4×4 board, where early legal moves are commonly exposed
at the visible exterior. On the larger boards, the opening legal moves are
inside the larger volume of empty cells. The invisible ray-casting targets
for those empty, non-legal cells were therefore blocking the pointer ray even
though the empty cells have no visible surface and should not prevent a user
from reaching a visible legal-move surface behind them.

The intended protection against clicking through an occupied green cell was
therefore too broad: it blocked through empty cells as well as occupied cells.

CHANGE
------
Version 1.15.1 changes the pointer-ray selection logic so that:

1. Empty, non-legal cells do not block the ray.
2. The first legal cell encountered is selectable.
3. An occupied cell blocks the ray, preventing selection through an occupied
   piece.
4. If the ray reaches only empty non-legal cells, no move is selected.

The invisible cell meshes remain available as precise coordinate targets, so
no visible board geometry or legal-move rendering was redesigned.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
No intentional changes were made to:
- Reversi rules or the 26 capture directions
- 4×4×4, 6×6×6, or 8×8×8 board geometry
- starting position
- bot strategy or random move selection
- bot timing or pass handling
- clocks
- Undo / Redo
- Arcball rotation, zoom, or mobile behavior
- coordinate entry
- visible legal-move surfaces
- occupied green surfaces
- board-size choices on the splash screen

FALLBACK
--------
Version 1.15.0 remains available as the previous multi-size fallback if
needed. Version 1.14.1 remains the known-good pre-multi-size fallback.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
The active Bundle was advanced to Version 1.15.1. The browser title, visible
version label, CSS cache-buster, JavaScript cache-buster, and active source
markers were synchronized to 1.15.1.

TESTING / RELEASE CHECKS
------------------------
- Confirmed the 1.15.1 source contains the revised ray-selection logic.
- Confirmed JavaScript syntax with Node.
- Confirmed all four required Bundle files are present and nonzero.
- Confirmed active version references and cache-busters are 1.15.1.
- Confirmed the final ZIP contains exactly index.html, Reversi.css,
  Reversi.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- Confirmed final ZIP integrity.
- The user should specifically retest Bot Opponent + White on both 6×6×6
  and 8×8×8, including several different camera orientations, because this
  repair targets the reported larger-board pointer-selection failure.

2026-09-03 — Version 1.15.2 — Size-specific Z-axis coordinate notation
======================================================================

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Repair the coordinate system so that all supported cube sizes have a
consistent three-axis notation without running out of letters on the Z axis.
The requested notation is:

    4×4×4: A-D, 1-4, S-V
    6×6×6: A-F, 1-6, S-X
    8×8×8: A-H, 1-8, S-Z

The coordinate-entry field must accept only the characters valid for the
currently selected board size.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.15.2 replaces the previous Z-axis sequence W-X-Y-Z-A-B-C-D with
the new S-T-U-V-W-X-Y-Z sequence. Because the coordinate system already
selects the first SIZE letters from the axis-letter strings, this produces:

    4×4×4: A-D / 1-4 / S-V
    6×6×6: A-F / 1-6 / S-X
    8×8×8: A-H / 1-8 / S-Z

The guided coordinate-entry system continues to derive its accepted X and Z
characters from the active board size, so invalid letters are rejected for
each size. Numeric entry likewise remains limited to 1 through the selected
board size.

The miniature coordinate-axis model and coordinate readout now use the same
new Z-axis notation. The move-entry placeholder was changed to use S as the
default 4×4×4 Z-axis example, and the Load Sequence example was updated to
use valid S-V coordinates.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
No changes were made to board geometry, move rules, legal-move generation,
ray-casting/selection behavior, bot behavior, clocks, Undo/Redo, board-size
selection, camera behavior, or the 4×4×4 / 6×6×6 / 8×8×8 board implementation.

The coordinate notation remains a display/input convention only; internal
board coordinates continue to use zero-based numeric x/y/z indices.

FALLBACK
--------
Version 1.15.1 remains the fallback if the revised coordinate notation causes
unexpected problems.

TESTING / RELEASE CHECKS
------------------------
- Active Bundle version references updated to 1.15.2.
- HTML CSS and JavaScript cache-busters updated to 1.15.2.
- Confirmed X-axis letters provide A-H and are truncated by SIZE.
- Confirmed Y-axis numbers provide 1-8 and are limited by SIZE.
- Confirmed Z-axis letters provide S-Z and are truncated by SIZE.
- Confirmed guided coordinate entry derives accepted letters from the active
  board size.
- Confirmed coordinate parsing uses the same size-specific letter sets.
- Confirmed miniature coordinate labels use the revised Z-axis letters.
- JavaScript syntax check performed with Node.
- All four required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly: index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  Reversi_Log.txt.

------------

the known-good fallback.

======================================================================
2026-09-03 — Version 1.15.4 — Disable Bot-mode timed games and keep clocks outside Undo/Redo
======================================================================

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.15.3
------------------------
The user confirmed that move-by-move Bot-mode Undo/Redo is working correctly.
A new clock problem was then observed: when Undo or Redo was used during a
clocked game, the clock did not continue ticking normally. The user also
identified a potential fairness/cheating problem if Undo could effectively
restore elapsed time taken from an opponent.

DECISION
--------
Bot Opponent is intended as a practice mode, where Undo/Redo should remain
available. To avoid creating a timed-game exploit, timed games are now
disabled whenever Bot Opponent is selected. Bot games therefore use Unlimited
mode only.

In addition, Undo and Redo are defined as board/game-state history operations,
not clock-history operations. Clock time must never be restored, rewound,
or otherwise changed by Undo or Redo.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
1. When Bot Opponent is selected on the start screen:
   - The Game Duration selector is disabled.
   - The selector is automatically set to Unlimited.
   - A disabled visual state is applied to the selector.

2. When Two Humans is selected again:
   - The Game Duration selector is re-enabled.
   - The user's previously selected duration can be chosen normally.

3. Undo and Redo now commit any elapsed time on the currently active player's
   clock before restoring the board/game snapshot. The restored snapshot does
   not contain clock state, so the clocks cannot be rewound by history.
   After the board state is restored, the appropriate current player's clock
   continues from the present time.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules changed.
- No legal-move calculation changed.
- No bot strategy or random-move behavior changed.
- No board-size or coordinate-system behavior changed.
- No ray-casting/selection behavior changed.
- Bot-mode Undo/Redo remains one move at a time.
- No board geometry or appearance changed.
- No Reversi terminology or Bundle file naming changed.

FALLBACK
--------
Version 1.15.3 remains the fallback if the new Bot-mode duration restriction
or clock-preservation behavior causes an unexpected problem.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
The active Bundle was advanced to Version 1.15.4. The browser title, visible
version marker, CSS cache-buster, JavaScript cache-buster, and active source
version markers were synchronized to 1.15.4.

TESTING / RELEASE CHECKS
------------------------
- Confirmed Bot Opponent disables the Game Duration selector.
- Confirmed selecting Bot Opponent forces Unlimited duration.
- Confirmed Two Humans re-enables the Game Duration selector.
- Confirmed Undo/Redo code does not restore clock state from game snapshots.
- Confirmed active clock time is committed before Undo/Redo state restoration.
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All four required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.

  Reversi_Log.txt.

2026-09-03 — Version 1.15.5 — Size-aware legal-move opacity progression
======================================================================

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Extend the established blue legal-move opacity treatment from the 4×4×4
board to the new 6×6×6 and 8×8×8 boards. The larger boards should begin at
the same low 0.10 opacity used by 4×4×4, remain low through roughly the first
half of the game so the internal spheres remain visible, and then become
progressively darker as the game advances.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.15.5 makes the legal-move opacity calculation board-size aware.

For 4×4×4, the established Version 1.9.14 progression is preserved exactly:
- starts at 0.10;
- follows the established subtle early 2.5-power curve;
- ramps strongly to 0.50 by the halfway point;
- remains at 0.50 for the remainder of the game.

For 6×6×6 and 8×8×8:
- starts at 0.10;
- remains at 0.10 through approximately the first half of the game;
- begins increasing after halfway;
- ramps progressively toward 0.50 by the end of the game.

The larger-board ramp uses a 1.75-power curve so the opacity stays relatively
subtle immediately after halfway and becomes increasingly noticeable later in
the game. This is intended to preserve visibility of the many interior
spheres while still making legal moves easier to see as the board fills.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
No changes were made to Reversi rules, board geometry, board-size selection,
coordinate notation, coordinate entry, ray-casting/selection, bot behavior,
clocks, Undo/Redo, camera behavior, occupied green surfaces, yellow hover
highlight, or non-legal empty-cube visibility. Non-legal empty cubes remain
completely invisible.

FALLBACK
--------
Version 1.15.4 remains the fallback if the new larger-board opacity treatment
is visually unsatisfactory.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
The active Bundle was advanced to Version 1.15.5. The browser title, visible
version marker, CSS cache-buster, JavaScript cache-buster, and active source
version marker were synchronized to 1.15.5.

TESTING / RELEASE CHECKS
------------------------
- Confirmed the 4×4×4 opacity function preserves the established progression.
- Confirmed 6×6×6 and 8×8×8 use the same 0.10 starting opacity.
- Confirmed larger boards remain at 0.10 through approximately halfway.
- Confirmed larger boards begin ramping only after halfway.
- Confirmed larger boards ramp toward 0.50 by the end of the game.
- Confirmed non-legal empty cubes remain invisible.
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All four required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Active version references checked against 1.15.5.
- HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters checked as 1.15.5.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly: index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi_Log.txt,
  Reversi.js.
- ZIP integrity checked successfully.

2026-09-04 — Version 1.18.1 — Formalize Bot Decision Interface
======================================================================

USER REQUEST / NEXT 1.18.x STEP
--------------------------------
After testing Version 1.18.0, the user confirmed that Bot Mode is working as
before: bot and human turns alternate, moves are made correctly, score works,
and Undo/Redo still works. The user approved continuing with the planned
1.18.1 architecture step.

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Keep the bot completely random, but make the communication between the game
controller and bot engine more deliberate and future-ready. Reversi.js should
ask the bot engine for a move through a single generic decision interface,
without knowing how that decision is made.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.18.1 renames the exported bot function in ReversiBot.js from
chooseRandomMove() to chooseMove(). Reversi.js now imports chooseMove() and
uses that function when it needs the bot's decision. The implementation of
chooseMove() remains the same uniform random selection from the supplied legal
moves.

This is an interface/architecture change only. The bot's playing behavior is
intentionally unchanged. No strategic evaluation has been introduced yet.

ARCHITECTURE
------------
Reversi.js remains authoritative for game state, rules, legal-move generation,
rendering, turn management, and committing moves. ReversiBot.js remains
responsible only for deciding which supplied move to return. The intended
future path is still:

    Reversi.js → chooseMove() → ReversiBot.js → selected move → Reversi.js

This keeps the bot's decision-making replaceable without moving game rules or
state management into the bot engine.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules or legal-move calculation changed.
- No board geometry, piece appearance, or rendering behavior changed.
- No bot strategy or playing strength changed.
- No difficulty levels, evaluation factors, look-ahead, or minimax were added.
- No game-clock behavior changed.
- No Undo/Redo behavior changed.
- No iPhone interaction behavior changed.
- No Two Player behavior changed.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
Active project-owned version references were synchronized to 1.18.1 in
index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js, and ReversiBot.js. Historical version
references in Reversi_Log.txt remain unchanged.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- Version 1.18.0 was used as the starting Bundle.
- User tested Version 1.18.0 Bot Mode and confirmed expected behavior.
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Active version/cache-buster audit completed.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity checked successfully.

NEXT STEP

Bot Engine work can begin introducing individual tunable evaluation factors.
Those strategy factors should be added incrementally and tested against the
user's observations from playing 4×4×4 Cube Reversi rather than assuming that
standard 2D Othello strategy transfers directly to the 3D game.


======================================================================
2026-09-04 — Version 1.18.7 — Surface Position Tiebreaker
======================================================================

USER TEST FEEDBACK FROM VERSION 1.18.6
--------------------------------------
User tested Version 1.18.6 on an 8×8×8 board and reported that the bot appeared
to be choosing moves that gave itself more options. User was ready to continue.

INTENDED CHANGE
---------------
Add one controlled positional experiment based on the user's observation that
when edge or outer-layer moves become available, the bot sometimes appears to
prefer them. Do not assume that this is inherently good strategy; test it as a
tiebreaker only.

ACTUAL CHANGE
-------------
Version 1.18.7 adds surface position as a tiebreaker after opponent mobility
and own mobility. Each candidate move receives a surfaceLevel value based on
how many of its three coordinates are on the outer boundary of the cube:

    interior = 0
    face      = 1
    edge      = 2
    corner    = 3

The bot's current decision order is now:

    1. Minimize opponent legal moves.
    2. Maximize the bot's own legal moves.
    3. Prefer the higher surfaceLevel.
    4. Maximize immediate pieces flipped.
    5. Randomly choose among remaining ties.

This is deliberately a tiebreaker rather than a weighted score. The purpose is
to learn whether outer-surface positioning contributes useful behavior in the
3D game without allowing it to override stronger mobility considerations.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules or legal-move calculation changed.
- No board geometry or rendering behavior changed.
- No look-ahead, minimax, or difficulty levels added.
- No arbitrary numeric weighting between strategic factors was introduced.
- Reversi.js remains authoritative for game state, rules, legal moves, and
  authoritative candidate-position metadata.
- ReversiBot.js remains responsible for choosing among supplied candidates.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- Version 1.18.6 was used as the starting Bundle.
- User confirmed Version 1.18.6 worked normally and reported improved own-mobility
  behavior on 8×8×8.
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Active version/cache-buster audit completed.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity checked successfully.
- Live browser/iPhone testing was not performed in this environment.

NEXT STEP
---------
User will test whether the surface-position tiebreaker produces useful behavior.
Particular attention should be paid to whether the bot's apparent preference for
edge/outer-layer moves is actually beneficial, neutral, or harmful.

======================================================================
VERSION 1.18.11 — SECURED-CORNER EXPANSION
======================================================================

PURPOSE
-------
Test the user's observation that once the bot has secured a true corner on
larger boards, spaces immediately adjacent to that corner should become
attractive as the bot builds outward from a permanently stable corner.

BOT DECISION CHANGE
-------------------
On 6×6×6 and 8×8×8 only, the existing corner-position tiebreaker was refined:

- A true corner remains strongly preferred.
- A move adjacent to an unclaimed or opponent-owned corner remains discouraged.
- A move adjacent to a corner already owned by the bot becomes favorable.

The established higher-level decision priorities remain unchanged:

    1. Minimize opponent legal moves.
    2. Maximize the bot's own legal moves.
    3. Minimize opponent surface access.
    4. Apply corner/surface relationship.
    5. Maximize immediate pieces flipped.
    6. Randomly choose among remaining ties.

This release intentionally does not add general stable-piece detection or
look-ahead. The purpose is to isolate the secured-corner expansion idea.

USER TESTING OBSERVATION FROM 1.18.10

the core when the user did, while appearing to follow toward outer positions
when the user moved outward. User also emphasized that after a corner is taken,
the bot should prioritize spaces adjacent to that secured corner as it builds
outward, because those pieces cannot be flipped. User noted that this strategy
is especially relevant on larger boards.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------

- No board geometry or rendering behavior changed.
- No look-ahead, minimax, or difficulty levels added.
- No general stable-piece algorithm added.
- No 4×4×4 corner factor introduced.
- Reversi.js remains authoritative for board state, rules, legal moves, and
  candidate-position metadata.
- ReversiBot.js remains responsible for choosing among supplied candidates.

======================================================================
VERSION 1.18.14 — CORNER-AWARENESS RUNTIME REPAIR
======================================================================

PURPOSE
-------
Repair a runtime error introduced in the 1.18.11/1.18.x secured-corner
awareness code. User reported that in Bot Mode on 8×8×8, the bot sometimes
failed to make its turn, leaving both players unable to move normally.

USER REPRODUCTION / DIAGNOSIS
-----------------------------
User reproduced the failure with this sequence:

    F-6-X, F-3-X, F-4-X, F-6-U

The user made F-6-U as White. Black had legal moves, but the bot did not make
its move and the game became unresponsive for turn progression.

Inspection of the actual 1.18.13 Bundle found the specific runtime fault in
getCornerPosition(). The code attempted to read a corner with:

    board[getIndex(cx, cy, cz)]

but no getIndex() function exists in Reversi.js. On larger boards, where the
corner-awareness evaluation runs, this causes the bot's scheduled evaluation
to throw before playMove() is reached. This explains why legal-move cubes could
still be displayed while the bot failed to respond.

REPAIR
------
The corner lookup now uses the board's actual three-dimensional indexing:

    board[cx][cy][cz]

No game rules, move generation, turn rules, bot priorities, or corner strategy
were intentionally changed by this repair.

The release also increments the active version to 1.18.14 and synchronizes
active version references across the Bundle.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules changed.
- No legal-move generation changed.
- No bot evaluation priority changed.
- No board-size behavior intentionally changed.
- No rendering or interaction redesign changed.
- Reversi.js remains authoritative for game state, rules, legal moves, and
  move execution.
- ReversiBot.js remains responsible for selecting among supplied candidates.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- User testing of 1.18.13 identified the runtime failure on 8×8×8.
- Root cause was confirmed by inspecting the actual Bundle source.
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Active version/cache-buster audit completed.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity checked successfully.
- Live browser/iPhone testing was not performed in this environment.

NEXT STEP
---------
User will retest Bot Mode, especially 8×8×8, to confirm that the bot now
completes its scheduled turn after the corner-aware evaluation runs.


======================================================================
VERSION 1.18.15 — CORNER AWARENESS ON 4×4×4
======================================================================

PURPOSE
-------
Extend the corner-awareness experiment to the 4×4×4 board after user
testing showed that the bot was avoiding immediately available corners.
The larger-board corner logic is now tested consistently across all
supported board sizes.

USER TESTING / OBSERVATION
--------------------------
User tested 1.18.14 on 4×4×4 after extensive 8×8×8 play. On 4×4×4,
corners can be available on the first move. The user observed that the bot
was not taking an available corner and instead appeared to avoid it. The
user also noted that the 4×4×4 board now feels much smaller after playing
many 8×8×8 games.

IMPLEMENTATION
--------------
The corner-position factor is no longer disabled when SIZE < 6. It now
applies to 4×4×4 as well as 6×6×6 and 8×8×8. The intended corner behavior
remains:

- True corner: strongly preferred.
- Adjacent to a corner owned by the bot: favorable for building outward.
- Adjacent to an unclaimed or opponent-owned corner: discouraged.
- Other positions: neutral.

The existing priority hierarchy is otherwise unchanged. No new mobility
criterion or piece-gain rule was added.

IMPORTANT CORRECTION TO PRIOR IMPLEMENTATION
---------------------------------------------
The previous getCornerPosition() implementation initialized its score at
zero and then used Math.min(best, -1) for dangerous corner-adjacent moves.
Because best began at zero, that branch could never lower the result below
zero. Version 1.18.15 corrects that behavior so the intended -1 penalty is
actually represented.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules changed.
- No legal-move generation changed.
- No turn-management behavior intentionally changed.
- No rendering or interaction redesign changed.
- Reversi.js remains authoritative for game state, rules, legal moves, and
  move execution.
- ReversiBot.js remains responsible for selecting among supplied candidates.

TESTING / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- User testing of 1.18.14 identified the 4×4×4 corner behavior.
- The corner-position implementation was inspected and corrected.
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Active version/cache-buster audit completed.
- Final ZIP verified to contain exactly index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity checked successfully.
- Live browser/iPhone testing was not performed in this environment.

NEXT STEP
---------
User will test 1.18.15 on 4×4×4 and, if useful, larger boards to see whether
corner behavior now appears sensible across board sizes.


===== Version 1.18.16 — Corner Priority Repair =====

User testing of 1.18.15 on 4×4×4 showed that the bot still did not reliably take available corner moves. Review found that corner position was still evaluated only after mobility and surface criteria, and the corner-position calculation initialized its score at 0, which prevented the intended negative value from being applied to dangerous corner-adjacent moves.

Repair:
- Corner position is now the first strategic priority in ReversiBot.js.
- A true corner receives the highest position value.
- A move adjacent to a corner already owned by the bot is next-best, supporting expansion from a secured corner.
- A move adjacent to an unsecured or opponent-owned corner is discouraged.
- The corner logic applies to 4×4×4, 6×6×6, and 8×8×8.
- Existing mobility, opponent-surface, and piece-gain criteria remain as later tiebreakers.
- No change to game rules, move execution, rendering, clocks, Undo/Redo, or bot-turn plumbing.

Testing note:
User reported that 1.18.15 was still avoiding corners on 4×4×4. This release specifically addresses that behavior.

Release integrity checks:
- JavaScript syntax checked.
- All five required Bundle files present and nonzero.
- Active version references synchronized to 1.18.16.
- Final ZIP contains exactly the five required Bundle files.
- ZIP integrity verified.
- Live browser/iPhone testing is not available in this environment; user testing is required.
