======================================================================
VERSION 1.19.3 — Robust Pass-State Repair
======================================================================

PURPOSE
-------
Repair a second, more serious pass-handling failure discovered during
Version 1.19.2 testing in Bot Mode on 4×4×4. The failure could leave the
wrong color active after a pass, cause the bot to play for the human color,
and leave the turn indicator stuck on the wrong player after Undo.

USER TESTING OBSERVATION FROM 1.19.2
-------------------------------------
User tested 4×4×4 in Bot Mode, playing White. Near the end of the game Black
had no legal move and the top status correctly showed Black's turn and
0 Available moves. After the user clicked through the pass message, the bot
then appeared to take over the other color and play for White, after which
the turn did not change back correctly for the next move. User also reported
that pressing Undo after this left the top status saying Black's move no
matter what.

Exact move sequence supplied by the user up to the failure:
F-6-X, F-3-X, F-4-X, F-4-W, E-3-W, F-6-U, B-3-S, A-1-T, C-4-U, C-3-V,
C-2-S, C-3-S, A-2-T, B-2-V, A-2-U, B-4-S, D-3-U, C-4-V, C-4-S, D-3-S,
D-2-S, D-3-V, A-3-S, B-1-S, C-1-S, A-2-V, A-4-V, A-2-S, A-4-T, B-1-V,
D-1-V, C-1-V, PASS, B-4-U

The user explicitly requested a detailed log entry because this issue may need
to be revisited after a break and the log will be used to restore project
context.

DIAGNOSIS / REPAIR
-----------------
The previous pass repair still allowed pass state to depend implicitly on
currentPlayer. That is unsafe because currentPlayer is also the variable used
by normal turn progression and can be changed while the pass overlay is active.

Version 1.19.3 introduces an explicit passPlayer state variable. The pass
player is recorded when a pass is shown and is also stored in Undo/Redo game
snapshots. Dismissing a pass now advances specifically from the recorded
passing player rather than blindly applying opponent(currentPlayer).

Pass behavior is now deliberately consistent for both human and bot players:
- The player who must pass becomes currentPlayer while the pass message is
  displayed.
- updateStatus() therefore reports that player's 0 Available moves.
- The pass message records exactly which player passed.
- Dismissing the message advances to the other player.
- If the other player is the bot, the bot turn is scheduled normally.
- If the other player is the human, the human receives control normally.
- If the bot itself discovers at the beginning of its scheduled turn that it
  has no move, the same explicit pass-message path is used rather than a
  separate automatic state transition.
- If neither player has a legal move, the game completes as before.

This removes the previous ambiguity that could allow the bot to receive the
wrong color's turn or leave the turn state stuck.

UNDO / REDO STATE
-----------------
passPlayer is now included in game snapshots so restoring a state containing
a visible pass restores the identity of the passing player exactly. The bot
turn timer continues to be cancelled before Undo/Redo operations.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules changed.
- No legal-move generation changed.
- No bot evaluation criteria changed.
- No corner strategy changed.
- No Auto-Rotate behavior changed from 1.19.2.
- No board geometry or rendering design changed.
- No change to the five-file Bundle definition.

VERSION / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- Active Bundle references synchronized to 1.19.3.
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files present and nonzero.
- Final ZIP contains exactly index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity verified.
- Live browser/iPhone testing is not available in this environment; user
  testing is required.

NEXT TEST FOCUS
---------------
User should test a bot pass on 4×4×4, confirm the pass message shows the bot's
color and 0 Available moves, dismiss it, then confirm the human can make the
next move. Undo/Redo should also be tested around a pass.



======================================================================
VERSION 1.20.5 — Loaded Sequence Playability Repair
======================================================================

USER TESTING OBSERVATION FROM 1.20.4
-------------------------------------
User confirmed that Version 1.20.4 is working and identified it as a good
fallback version moving forward. During testing, the user then reported that
loading a move sequence allows Undo to work, but the loaded game cannot be
continued with new moves.

DIAGNOSIS / REPAIR
-----------------
The sequence loader reconstructs the board and turn state directly rather
than advancing through playMove(). At the end of loading, it refreshed the
board, legal moves, status, and Undo/Redo controls, but it did not schedule
the bot when the loaded position left the bot as the current player. In Bot
Mode this could leave the game apparently stuck: Undo remained available, but
the human was correctly blocked from making a move because it was the bot's
turn.

Version 1.20.5 now calls scheduleBotTurn() after a sequence has been
successfully loaded. This means:
- If the loaded position leaves the human player to move, the human can
  continue immediately.
- If the loaded position leaves the bot to move, the bot is automatically
  scheduled just as it would be after a normal move.
- Completed loaded games remain complete and do not start another turn.
- Human-vs-human behavior is unchanged because scheduleBotTurn() has no effect
  when Bot Mode is not active.

LOADED PASS REPAIR
------------------
While reviewing sequence continuation, an additional state issue was found in
the PASS loader. A PASS token was being recorded but currentPlayer was not
advanced to the player who receives control after the pass. Version 1.20.5 now
advances currentPlayer to opponent(currentPlayer) when a valid PASS token is
loaded. This makes loaded sequences containing passes continue from the
correct player.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules changed.
- No legal-move generation changed.
- No bot evaluation criteria changed.
- No corner strategy changed.
- No coordinate-system behavior changed.
- No Show 3-Axis behavior changed.
- No Active-Game Confirmation behavior changed.
- No Undo/Redo design changed.
- No board geometry or rendering design changed.

VERSION / RELEASE INTEGRITY
---------------------------
- Active Bundle references synchronized to 1.20.5.
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files present and nonzero.
- Final ZIP contains exactly index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity verified.
- User testing is required to confirm continuation in both Human and Bot
  modes.

NEXT TEST FOCUS
---------------
Load a sequence that ends with the human player's turn and make a move.
Then test a sequence that ends with the bot's turn while in Bot Mode and
confirm the bot automatically continues. If possible, also test a sequence
containing PASS and confirm the turn advances correctly after the pass.


======================================================================
VERSION 1.20.5 — Loaded Sequence Player-Choice Revision
======================================================================

USER DESIGN DECISION
--------------------
Before testing the original 1.20.5 Bundle, the user identified an important
ambiguity in loaded positions: a move sequence records the moves and therefore
reconstructs the board and turn, but it does not know which color the user
wants to control after loading. The user requested that choosing the player
color be an explicit decision made after the sequence is loaded.

LOADED GAME PLAY MODE
---------------------
Version 1.20.5 is revised so that a successfully loaded, still-live sequence
now pauses for a "Choose How to Play" dialog. The user can choose exactly one
of:
- Black (vs Bot)
- White (vs Bot)
- Play as Both

Black (vs Bot) sets Bot Mode with Black as the human-controlled color.
White (vs Bot) sets Bot Mode with White as the human-controlled color.
Play as Both sets Two Player mode, allowing either color to be played by the
user.

The loaded sequence itself still determines the board position and whose turn
is next. The new choice determines who controls each color from that position.
If the chosen mode puts the bot on the current turn, the bot is scheduled
immediately. If the human controls the current turn, the loaded position is
immediately playable by the user.

CLOCK TIMING
------------
The game clock is now held stopped while the player-choice dialog is open, so
the user's available time does not begin running before they have selected how
they want to play the loaded position. The clock starts when the choice is
confirmed.

COMPLETED SEQUENCES
-------------------
If a loaded sequence is already a completed game, no player-choice dialog is
shown because there is no remaining turn to play. The completed position is
left displayed normally.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules changed.
- No legal-move generation changed.
- No bot evaluation criteria changed.
- No corner strategy changed.
- No coordinate-system behavior changed.
- No Show 3-Axis behavior changed.
- No Active-Game Confirmation behavior changed.
- No Undo/Redo design changed.
- No sequence validation rules changed.
- No board geometry or rendering design changed.

RELEASE NOTE
------------
This is a revision of the uncommitted Version 1.20.5 Bundle rather than a
new version number. Version 1.20.4 remains the known-good fallback until this
revised 1.20.5 behavior is tested.

======================================================================
VERSION 1.20.16 — Robust Turn-State Repair Around PASS and Undo/Redo
======================================================================

USER FEEDBACK / ISSUE
---------------------
While testing Version 1.20.15, the user reached a position requiring a PASS.
When the user then used Undo to inspect what had happened, the turn indicator
was incorrectly/stubbornly showing Black's turn. The user recognized this as
the same class of PASS/current-player problem encountered earlier in the
project and requested that the previous PASS-handling repair be revisited.

REFERENCE TO EARLIER PASS REPAIR
---------------------------------
Version 1.19.3 introduced explicit passPlayer state because PASS handling could
previously depend implicitly on currentPlayer. The earlier repair also stored
passPlayer in Undo/Redo snapshots and advanced from the recorded passing player.
This release preserves that design and adds an additional reconciliation step
when restoring history.

ROOT CAUSE / REPAIR
-------------------
Undo/Redo snapshots contain currentPlayer, but currentPlayer can temporarily
represent the passing player while a PASS message is displayed. It can also be
affected by later turn transitions. Relying only on the saved currentPlayer
therefore leaves room for a stale turn value to survive a history restore.

Version 1.20.16 adds deriveCurrentPlayerFromHistory(), which reconstructs the
active turn from the recorded Move Sequence. Coordinate entries alternate the
turn, and PASS entries also advance the turn. Terminal RESIGN/TIMEOUT entries
do not advance it.

When restoring an Undo/Redo snapshot:
- If a PASS message is currently visible, passPlayer remains authoritative so
  the player who passed is shown with 0 Available moves.
- Otherwise, for an active game, currentPlayer is reconciled from the recorded
  move history rather than relying solely on the saved currentPlayer value.
- Completed-game snapshots retain their saved currentPlayer because there is
  no next playable turn to derive.

This specifically strengthens the existing 1.19.3 PASS repair without changing
Reversi rules, legal-move generation, bot strategy, or the meaning of Undo/Redo.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules changed.
- No legal-move generation changed.
- No bot evaluation criteria or strategy changed.
- No board geometry or rendering design changed.
- No coordinate-system behavior changed.
- No loaded-sequence format changed.
- No clock policy changed.
- No Bot-mode move-selection behavior changed.
- The existing explicit passPlayer state remains in use.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
Active project-owned version references are synchronized to 1.20.16 in
index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js, and ReversiBot.js. The HTML CSS/JavaScript
cache-busters are also synchronized to 1.20.16. Historical version references
remain unchanged.

RELEASE INTEGRITY
-----------------
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Active version/cache-buster audit completed.
- Final ZIP checked for exactly: index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity checked successfully.
- Live gameplay testing remains required to confirm the reported PASS/Undo
  scenario.

======================================================================
VERSION 1.20.17 — PASS/Undo Bot-Turn Control Repair
======================================================================

USER FEEDBACK / ISSUE
---------------------
While testing Version 1.20.16, the user reached a position where White had to
PASS. After using Undo to inspect the sequence, the turn indicator incorrectly
showed White's turn at every Undo position. Pressing Redo caused the displayed
turn to alternate between White and Black again, but stopping on a position
that should have been Black's (bot's) turn incorrectly allowed the user to make
the Black move.

The user clarified that the failure is specifically around PASS handling and
Undo/Redo: the restored board, displayed turn, and Bot-mode input control can
become inconsistent.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
Version 1.20.16 correctly added turn reconstruction from Move Sequence history,
but the existing Bot-mode Undo handler still contained an older rule that
forced every restored non-human currentPlayer back to humanPlayer. That rule
was originally intended to make Undo after a normal bot move return control to
the human, but it is incorrect when the restored history position is genuinely
the bot's turn — especially when a bot move was the move immediately before a
PASS by White.

The result was:
- the restored state could correctly identify Black as currentPlayer;
- Undo then immediately overwrote that state with White;
- the turn indicator therefore showed White;
- the board could become human-controlled even though the restored turn was the
  bot's turn.

REPAIR
------
Version 1.20.17 removes that unconditional Bot-mode currentPlayer override.
Undo now preserves the restored currentPlayer exactly as determined by the
history snapshot/reconciliation logic.

In Bot Mode after Undo:
- If the restored turn is the human player, the human can continue normally.
- If the restored turn is the bot player, the bot is automatically resumed from
  that exact restored position.
- A visible PASS state is left authoritative and is not immediately replaced
  by another scheduling action.
- Completed-game analysis remains available and does not restart the bot.

This keeps three things synchronized: the restored board position, the turn
indicator, and which side is actually allowed to make the next move.

The earlier Version 1.19.3 explicit passPlayer repair remains intact, as does
the Version 1.20.16 history-based turn reconciliation.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules changed.
- No legal-move generation changed.
- No bot evaluation criteria or strategy changed.
- No board geometry or rendering design changed.
- No coordinate-system behavior changed.
- No Move Sequence format changed.
- No clock policy changed.
- No loaded-sequence player-choice behavior changed.
- No completed-game analysis behavior intentionally changed.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
Active project-owned version references are synchronized to 1.20.17 in
index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js, and ReversiBot.js. HTML CSS/JavaScript
cache-busters are also synchronized to 1.20.17. Historical version references
remain unchanged.

RELEASE INTEGRITY
-----------------
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Active version/cache-buster audit completed.
- Final ZIP must contain exactly: index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity checked successfully.
- Live gameplay testing remains required for PASS → Undo → correct-turn and
  PASS → Undo → Bot-control behavior.


============================================================
VERSION 1.20.18 — Undo/Redo History-Review Mode
============================================================

USER FEEDBACK / ISSUE
---------------------
While testing Version 1.20.17, the user reached a position where White had to
PASS, dismissed the PASS message, and then pressed Undo. The user observed
that Undo appeared to return to the bot's turn, after which the bot immediately
made the move again and returned the game to the position where Undo had been
pressed. In effect, Undo appeared to do nothing.

DIAGNOSIS
---------
Version 1.20.17 correctly stopped forcing a restored bot turn to White, but it
then immediately scheduled the bot when Undo restored a genuine bot turn.
That made history inspection self-cancel: Undo moved backward to the bot's
position, then the bot automatically replayed the move that had just been
undone.

REPAIR
------
Version 1.20.18 introduces an explicit history-review state for Undo/Redo.
While reviewing history:
- Undo restores exactly one historical position and stops there.
- Redo restores exactly one historical position and stops there while more
  Redo history remains.
- The turn indicator reflects the actual restored currentPlayer.
- In Bot Mode, a restored bot turn remains bot-controlled; the user cannot
  manually make the bot's move.
- No bot move is automatically replayed merely because Undo reached a bot turn.
- When Redo reaches the latest game position, history-review mode ends and
  normal Bot Mode resumes. If the latest state is a genuine bot turn, the bot
  is then scheduled normally.
- Making a valid move while reviewing history exits review mode and returns to
  normal play.

This allows the user to step backward and forward through the game, including
PASS-related positions, without the bot immediately changing the position
being inspected.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules changed.
- No legal-move generation changed.
- No bot evaluation criteria or strategy changed.
- No board geometry or rendering design changed.
- No coordinate-system behavior changed.
- No Move Sequence format changed.
- No clock policy changed.
- No loaded-sequence player-choice behavior changed.
- No completed-game analysis behavior intentionally changed.
- The Version 1.19.3 explicit passPlayer repair remains intact.
- The Version 1.20.16 history-based turn reconciliation remains intact.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
Active project-owned version references are synchronized to 1.20.18 in
index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js, and ReversiBot.js. HTML CSS/JavaScript
cache-busters are synchronized to 1.20.18. Historical version references remain
unchanged.

RELEASE INTEGRITY
-----------------
- JavaScript syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files verified present and nonzero.
- Active version/cache-buster audit completed.
- Final ZIP contains exactly: index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity checked successfully.
- Live gameplay testing remains required for PASS -> Undo -> stable historical
  bot-turn review, Redo traversal, and return to normal Bot Mode at the latest
  position.

Version 1.20.19 — PASS State During Undo/Redo Review

User retested the 1.20.18 Undo/Redo repair with a late-game sequence containing repeated PASS entries. Normal Undo behavior was correct in that it moved backward through alternating positions and prevented manual moves when it was the bot's turn. However, when Undo reached positions represented by a PASS in the move sequence, the turn indicator could remain on Black because the review state treated PASS as an already-completed turn.

User clarified the desired history-review behavior: when Undo reaches a PASS in the recorded sequence, do not display the PASS message again. Instead, show the player whose turn it was at the PASS and show 0 Available moves. This makes the PASS state visible as a board position without interrupting history review; the user can then click Redo to continue forward.

Root cause:
- In normal gameplay, a PASS advances currentPlayer to the opponent after the PASS is recorded.
- Undo/Redo review uses the recorded history to reconstruct currentPlayer.
- For a trailing PASS during review, deriving from the complete history advances past the passing player, so the displayed turn does not represent the PASS position the user is inspecting.

Repair:
- Added applyHistoryReviewTurn() for Undo/Redo review states.
- If the restored move history ends in PASS, currentPlayer is derived from the history immediately before that PASS, identifying the player who had to pass.
- The PASS overlay remains hidden during history review.
- updateStatus() therefore shows that player's turn and the actual legal move count, which is 0 for the PASS position.
- Normal gameplay PASS handling is unchanged.
- Bot automation remains disabled while historyReviewActive is true, so the reviewed PASS state is not advanced automatically.
- Redo continues to return to normal play when the latest position is reached.

Version/cache-buster references updated to 1.20.19.

Release integrity checks:
- Reversi.js syntax check passed.
- ReversiBot.js syntax check passed.
- Bundle contains exactly the five required files.
- All five files are nonzero.
- ZIP integrity check passed.
- Version audit passed across active HTML/CSS/JS/Bot references.
- Live testing required.


============================================================
VERSION 1.20.20 — Repair Loaded PASS Turn-State Validation
============================================================

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.20.19
------------------------
The user attempted to load the exact late-game sequence previously used to
verify repeated PASS handling during Undo/Redo review. The sequence was copied
without modification, but Version 1.20.19 rejected it with:

    Invalid PASS at entry 50.

The user correctly questioned whether the sequence itself was invalid or
whether the game had generated an inconsistent turn state.

DIAGNOSIS
---------
The sequence is valid. The problem is in the Load Move Sequence validator,
not in the recorded move sequence or the underlying Reversi move rules.

At entry 49, D-2-T is a legal Black move. After that move, White has 0 legal
moves while Black still has legal moves. Normal gameplay therefore records a
PASS for White and makes White the current player while the PASS state is
shown.

The loaded-sequence validator, however, left currentPlayer as Black when it
detected that the opponent had no moves. It then reached entry 50 (PASS) and
checked Black's 7 legal moves against the PASS requirement, producing the
incorrect "Invalid PASS at entry 50" error.

There was a second related issue in the same loader path: the existing PASS
handler assumed currentPlayer was already the player who must pass and then
advanced currentPlayer to the opponent. That assumption is only correct if the
previous move-processing branch explicitly changes currentPlayer to the
passing player.

REPAIR
------
Version 1.20.20 repairs the loaded-sequence state machine so it matches normal
gameplay:

- When a move leaves the opponent with no legal moves but the current player
  still has legal moves, currentPlayer is explicitly changed to the opponent,
  and passRequired is set to true.
- The following PASS token is therefore validated against the actual passing
  player's 0 legal moves and the other player's available moves.
- After the PASS token, currentPlayer advances to the player who receives the
  turn, matching the normal gameplay flow.
- The recorded sequence itself is not changed.
- Normal gameplay PASS handling is unchanged.
- The 1.20.19 Undo/Redo history-review behavior is unchanged.

VERIFICATION OF THE USER'S SEQUENCE
-----------------------------------
The supplied 63-entry sequence was independently checked against the current
4×4×4 board rules. Entries 1–49 are legal moves. Entry 50 PASS is a valid
White pass, followed by the repeated valid pattern:

    PASS, B-4-V, PASS, A-4-U, PASS, A-3-U, PASS, C-4-S,
    PASS, D-3-S, PASS, D-4-T, PASS, C-3-S

At each PASS in this ending section, White has 0 legal moves and Black has
remaining legal moves. The final C-3-S ends the sequence normally.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
Active Bundle references and HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters are synchronized
to Version 1.20.20. Historical log entries retain their original versions.

RELEASE INTEGRITY CHECKS
------------------------
- Reversi.js syntax checked with Node.
- ReversiBot.js syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files are present and nonzero.
- Active version references audited and synchronized to 1.20.20.
- Final ZIP contains exactly index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity verified.
- Live testing remains required.

VERSION 1.20.21 — Correct Loaded PASS Turn Ownership
============================================================

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.20.20
------------------------
The user retested the exact late-game sequence and Version 1.20.20 still
reported:

    Invalid PASS at entry 50.

This confirmed that the Version 1.20.20 diagnosis identified the correct area
of the code but did not actually complete the required turn-state repair.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
After a move, the loader correctly detects that the opponent has no legal
moves and that the current player does have legal moves. However, the loader
was setting only passRequired = true while leaving currentPlayer assigned to
the player who just moved. Therefore, when the following PASS token was
validated, the loader was checking the wrong player's legal moves.

For the user's test sequence, entry 49 is D-2-T. That move leaves White with
0 legal moves while Black still has legal moves. The required entry 50 PASS
is therefore White's pass. Version 1.20.20 still had currentPlayer = Black at
that point, causing the same Invalid PASS at entry 50 error.

REPAIR
------
Version 1.20.21 explicitly sets currentPlayer = nextPlayer when a move creates
a required pass. The following PASS token is therefore validated while
currentPlayer is the player who actually has 0 legal moves. The existing PASS
handler then advances to the other player after recording the PASS.

This makes the loaded-sequence state machine match the normal gameplay flow:

    move → opponent has no moves → opponent becomes currentPlayer
         → PASS → other player becomes currentPlayer

No move sequence text was changed. Normal gameplay PASS handling, the
1.20.19/1.20.18 Undo/Redo history-review behavior, and bot strategy are
unchanged.

TEST FOCUS
----------
Reload the exact user-supplied late-game sequence. Entry 50 should now be
accepted as White's PASS, and the subsequent repeated White PASS entries
should load normally through the final C-3-S.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
Active Bundle references and HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters are synchronized
to Version 1.20.21. Historical log entries retain their original versions.

------------------------

- ReversiBot.js syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files are present and nonzero.
- Active version references audited and synchronized to 1.20.21.

  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.

VERSION 1.20.22 — Correct Loaded PASS Validation Condition
============================================================

USER FEEDBACK ON 1.20.21
------------------------
The user performed a hard refresh and confirmed that the deployed site was
serving Version 1.20.21 in the browser. DevTools Sources also showed
Reversi.js?v=1.20.21 and Reversi.css?v=1.20.21, so the previous cache/CDN
diagnosis was ruled out.

The exact late-game sequence still reported:

    Invalid PASS at entry 50.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
A second bug remained in the Version 1.20.21 PASS validation condition.
Version 1.20.21 correctly changed currentPlayer to the player who must pass,
but the validation condition then incorrectly rejected a PASS when that
player had zero legal moves:

    if (!passRequired || currentMoves.length === 0 || nextMoves.length === 0)

For a valid PASS, currentPlayer MUST have zero legal moves, while the other
player MUST have at least one legal move. Therefore the condition was
backwards for currentMoves.

For the user's sequence, after entry 49 (D-2-T):

    currentPlayer = White
    White legal moves = 0
    Black legal moves = 7
    passRequired = true

Entry 50 PASS is valid. The Version 1.20.21 condition rejected it precisely
because currentMoves.length === 0.

REPAIR
------
Version 1.20.22 changes the PASS validation condition to reject only when the
passing player has one or more legal moves, or when the opposing player has
no legal moves:

    if (!passRequired || currentMoves.length !== 0 || nextMoves.length === 0)

This preserves the Version 1.20.21 turn-ownership repair and completes the
loaded PASS state machine.

The exact user-supplied sequence remains unchanged. The expected state at
entry 50 is White PASS with 0 White moves and 7 Black moves. The subsequent
PASS entries should likewise be accepted through the final C-3-S.

No changes were made to bot strategy or the 1.20.18/1.20.19 Undo/Redo
history-review behavior.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
All active Bundle version references and HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters
are synchronized to Version 1.20.22. Historical log entries retain their
original versions.

RELEASE INTEGRITY CHECKS
------------------------
- Reversi.js syntax checked with Node.
- ReversiBot.js syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files are present and nonzero.
- Active version references audited and synchronized to 1.20.22.
- Final ZIP contains exactly index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity verified.
- Live testing remains required.


============================================================
POST-1.20.22 TEST NOTES — Loaded Complete Sequence / Undo-Redo
============================================================

USER TEST RESULT
----------------
Version 1.20.22 successfully loads the user's exact late-game sequence.
The Invalid PASS at entry 50 problem is confirmed fixed.

The user then identified several separate issues during testing. These are
being recorded now and are intentionally NOT being repaired together in this
version. They will be addressed one at a time in subsequent versions.

ISSUE 1 — COMPLETE CUBE HEADER STATE
------------------------------------
After the complete sequence is loaded, the cube is already in a game-complete
state, but the header still says:

    Black's turn
    0 Available moves

Desired behavior: when the cube/game is complete, the header should not show
whose turn it is or the number of available moves. It may instead simply show:

    Game Complete

This should be treated as a display/state-reporting change only; the completed
board itself is correct.

ISSUE 2 — LOADED SEQUENCE DOES NOT ASK FOR PLAYER COLOR
-------------------------------------------------------
After loading a move sequence, the normal prompt asking which color the user
wants to play does not appear.

Desired behavior: after loading a sequence, the user should receive the
appropriate choice of Black or White rather than silently falling into the
current/default mode.

ISSUE 3 — UNDO THROUGH PASSES RECONSTRUCTS THE WRONG TURN
---------------------------------------------------------
After loading the complete sequence and pressing Undo, the header reports
White's turn repeatedly while rewinding through the PASS entries. After the
passes have been rewound, it eventually reports Black's turn.

At that point the user is also able to make a Black move, apparently because
the loaded sequence has fallen into Two Player mode automatically.

Desired behavior: Undo/Redo history review of a loaded sequence should preserve
and correctly reconstruct the intended player/interaction state across PASS
entries, without repeatedly assigning White's turn or unexpectedly changing
the loaded game into an unrestricted Two Player state.

ISSUE 4 — BLANK GAME-COMPLETE MESSAGE AFTER REDO TO END
--------------------------------------------------------
If the user loads the sequence, clicks Undo several times, and then clicks
Redo repeatedly all the way back to the end, a small blank message/overlay
begins to appear. It appears to be the Game Complete message being triggered,
but its text is empty or is being cleared before it can be displayed.

Desired behavior: when Redo reaches the completed final position, the normal
Game Complete message should appear correctly rather than as a blank overlay.

NEXT DEVELOPMENT APPROACH
-------------------------
Do NOT attempt to repair all four issues in one bundle. Treat them as separate
state/display problems and fix/test them incrementally so that the successful
1.20.22 PASS-loading repair remains isolated and easy to verify.

when the user's long late-game sequence is loaded. However, the completed
loaded position did not show the "Choose How to Play" prompt.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
The loaded-sequence loader intentionally returned immediately when
gameCompleteActive became true. That prevented showLoadedSequenceChoice()
from running for a sequence that loaded directly into a completed position.
This also meant the loaded game silently retained the previous/default
Two Player interaction mode, which interfered with later Undo/Redo review.

REPAIR
------
Version 1.20.24 removes the early return after a loaded Game Complete state.
The loaded-sequence player-choice prompt is now shown for both incomplete and
already-completed loaded positions. The user can choose:

    Black (vs Bot)
    White (vs Bot)
    Play as Both

The selected mode is stored by the existing continueLoadedSequence() logic.
A completed position still cannot make a move, and scheduleBotTurn() remains
blocked by the existing Game Complete state.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
- No Reversi rules changed.
- No legal-move generation changed.
- No PASS validation changed.
- No completed-game header behavior changed beyond 1.20.23.
- No Undo/Redo reconstruction changed yet.
- No bot evaluation criteria changed.

TEST FOCUS
----------
Load the exact long late-game sequence. The sequence should load to the
completed position and then display the Choose How to Play prompt. Selecting
a mode should dismiss the prompt without starting an inappropriate bot move.
The remaining Undo-through-PASS and Redo-to-Game-Complete issues are
intentionally left for later versions.

============================================================
VERSION 1.20.25 — Undo/Redo Turn Reconstruction After PASS
============================================================

USER TEST RESULT / ISSUE FROM 1.20.24
-------------------------------------
Version 1.20.24 successfully restored the loaded-sequence player-choice
prompt. The user then tested Undo through the completed long sequence and
reported that the turn indicator repeatedly showed White's turn while
rewinding through the recorded PASS section. At the end of that rewind, the
turn state could become incorrect and, when the loaded game had effectively
fallen back to Two Player mode, the user could make a Black move.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
Undo/Redo snapshots already store currentPlayer at the exact point each
snapshot is created. A snapshot taken before a move that follows a PASS
therefore correctly stores the player who received the turn after the PASS.

The history-review helper was incorrectly overriding that stored state by
re-deriving currentPlayer from moveSequenceHistory. When the restored history
ended in PASS, it treated the trailing PASS as though the review position were
the passing player's visible turn. That was appropriate for an earlier
history-review design, but it is wrong for the snapshots actually used by
Undo/Redo: there is no separate snapshot immediately after the PASS, so a
snapshot whose history ends in PASS is normally the position after the PASS,
with the receiving player's turn.

REPAIR
------
Version 1.20.25 changes only the Undo/Redo history-review turn handling:

- restoreGameSnapshot() remains authoritative for currentPlayer.
- applyHistoryReviewTurn() no longer re-derives currentPlayer from the entire
  move sequence.
- The restored snapshot's currentPlayer is preserved exactly.
- The transient PASS overlay/message is still suppressed during history
  review, as intended.
- Legal moves and the status header are recalculated from the restored board
  and restored currentPlayer.
- Normal gameplay PASS handling is unchanged.
- Loaded-sequence validation is unchanged.
- The bot decision engine and bot strategy are unchanged.

WHY THIS MATTERS FOR THE LONG TEST SEQUENCE
-------------------------------------------
For the supplied sequence, each PASS is followed by a move for Black. The
Undo snapshot immediately before that Black move represents Black's turn after
White's PASS. Version 1.20.25 preserves that stored Black turn instead of
changing it back to White merely because the history text ends in PASS.

The turn should therefore follow the actual saved game positions as Undo
moves backward through the sequence rather than repeatedly reconstructing a
turn from the textual history.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
The following are intentionally left for later versions:

- Any additional loaded-game mode behavior beyond the 1.20.24 player-choice
  prompt.
- The final blank Game Complete overlay observed after Undo followed by Redo
  all the way to the end.
- Any bot strategy or bot-engine development.

TEST FOCUS
----------
Load the same long sequence, choose a specific loaded-game mode (preferably
White vs Bot for this test), then use Undo repeatedly through the late-game
PASS section. Each restored position should show the turn belonging to that
saved position. In particular, a snapshot whose history ends in PASS because
it is immediately before the following Black move should show Black's turn,
not White's.

Then test Redo back through the same positions. Do not treat the final blank
Game Complete overlay as fixed by this version; that remains the next separate
repair after the turn-state behavior is confirmed.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
Active Bundle references and HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters are synchronized
to Version 1.20.25. Historical log entries retain their original versions.

RELEASE INTEGRITY CHECKS
------------------------
- Reversi.js syntax checked with Node.
- ReversiBot.js syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files are present and nonzero.
- Active version references audited and synchronized to 1.20.25.
- Final ZIP contains exactly index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity verified.
- Live testing remains required.

VERSION 1.20.26 — Loaded-Sequence PASS Positions in Undo/Redo
============================================================

USER TEST RESULT / ISSUE FROM 1.20.25
-------------------------------------
Version 1.20.25 changed Undo/Redo so restored snapshots retained their saved
currentPlayer. The user confirmed that this changed the behavior, but reported
that Undo now repeatedly showed Black's turn through the late-game PASS section.
The user correctly observed that the review was skipping the White PASS turns and
showing the board position at the end of each Black move. Because the user had
chosen White vs Bot, White could not make a move at those positions, so normal
turn enforcement was working; the problem was that PASS had no separate history
position for Undo/Redo to stop on.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
Loaded-sequence Undo history was being created only when coordinate moves were
processed. A PASS changes the turn and is recorded in moveSequenceHistory, but
it does not change the board and therefore previously created no Undo snapshot.
For a sequence pattern of Black move -> White PASS -> Black move, the history
contained snapshots for the two Black moves but no snapshot representing the
intermediate White-pass state. Undo therefore skipped directly over White's
turn.

REPAIR
------
Version 1.20.26 makes a loaded PASS a first-class Undo/Redo history position:

- When the loader validates a PASS, it first saves a game snapshot.
- That snapshot represents the board after the preceding move, with the
  passing player as currentPlayer and 0 legal moves.
- The PASS token is then recorded and currentPlayer advances to the player who
  receives the turn.
- The following coordinate move still receives its own normal pre-move
  snapshot.
- restoreGameSnapshot() and applyHistoryReviewTurn() remain unchanged from
  1.20.25; they now have the correct PASS snapshot to restore.
- Normal gameplay PASS handling is unchanged in this version.
- The bot decision engine and bot strategy are unchanged.

EXPECTED UNDO/REDO BEHAVIOR
---------------------------
For the late-game sequence, Undo should now be able to step through the actual
historical turns:

  Black move position
  White PASS position (0 available moves)
  Black move position
  White PASS position (0 available moves)
  ...

When the user is playing as White, the White PASS positions should show White's
turn with 0 available moves, while Black positions should remain non-playable
by the human. Redo should move forward through the same alternating historical
positions.

NO-CHANGE AREAS
---------------
The following remain intentionally separate repairs:

- Final blank Game Complete overlay after Undo followed by Redo to the end.
- Any additional loaded-game mode behavior beyond the 1.20.24 player-choice
  repair.
- Normal gameplay PASS snapshots/Undo behavior.
- Any bot strategy or bot-engine development.

TEST FOCUS
----------
Load the same long sequence and choose White vs Bot. Press Undo repeatedly
through the late-game PASS section. Confirm that the review now alternates
between Black positions and White PASS positions instead of skipping every
White PASS. At a White PASS position the header should show White's turn and
0 Available moves, and attempting a move should remain blocked because White
has no legal move.

Then use Redo to walk forward through the same positions and verify that the
same Black/White-PASS alternation is preserved.

VERSION / CACHE-BUSTING
-----------------------
Active Bundle references and HTML CSS/JavaScript cache-busters are synchronized
to Version 1.20.26. Historical log entries retain their original versions.

RELEASE INTEGRITY CHECKS
------------------------
- Reversi.js syntax checked with Node.
- ReversiBot.js syntax checked with Node.
- All five required Bundle files are present and nonzero.
- Active version references audited and synchronized to 1.20.26.
- Final ZIP contains exactly index.html, Reversi.css, Reversi.js,
  ReversiBot.js, and Reversi_Log.txt.
- ZIP integrity verified.
- Live testing remains required.

VERSION 1.20.27 — BUILD ERROR / REGRESSION POSTMORTEM
============================================================

USER TEST RESULT
----------------
Version 1.20.27 was deployed after Version 1.20.26 had been confirmed by the
user as a good fallback. The user immediately reported that the long, valid
loaded sequence again failed with:

  Invalid PASS at entry 50.

The browser screenshot confirmed that the live page was actually loading
Version 1.20.27 and Reversi.js?v=1.20.27, so this was not a cache problem.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
The Version 1.20.27 Bundle was accidentally built from an older source state
corresponding to the pre-1.20.26 implementation instead of from the confirmed
1.20.26 source.

Specifically, the 1.20.27 Reversi.js had reverted several working repairs:

- The corrected PASS validation condition from 1.20.21 was lost. It reverted
to the invalid condition that rejects a PASS when currentMoves.length === 0.
- The 1.20.26 loaded-PASS Undo snapshot was lost. PASS was no longer saved as a
first-class history-review position.
- The 1.20.24 loaded-sequence player-choice behavior was lost. The loader again
returned immediately for a completed sequence instead of asking how the user
wanted to play it.
- The 1.20.26 history-review turn handling was lost and the older review logic
was restored.

This explains why Version 1.20.27 regressed all the way back to the entry-50
PASS failure even though the intended 1.20.27 change was only supposed to
address the completed-game header.

IMPORTANT PROCESS NOTE
----------------------
Version 1.20.26 is the known-good fallback for the loaded-sequence PASS,
player-choice, and Undo/Redo behavior. Future versions in this repair series
must be branched directly from the exact 1.20.26 source, not from an older
working directory or reconstructed source.

The user was correct to question why an apparently unrelated header repair
could break the PASS loader. The break was caused by the bad build base, not
by an inherent dependency between the header and PASS validation.

BLANK GAME COMPLETE MESSAGE CLARIFICATION
-----------------------------------------
The previously reported small blank message/overlay after Undo followed by
Redo to the end has NOT been fixed yet. It remains a separate unresolved issue.
The intended 1.20.27 repair was the header text only; the blank overlay problem
was never completed as a repair and remains on the issue list.

VERSION 1.20.29 — PRESERVE GAME COMPLETE STATE AFTER DISMISSAL
============================================================

USER TEST RESULT / ISSUE
------------------------
Version 1.20.28 successfully restored the completed-game state after Redo.
The user confirmed that the sequence loaded, Undo worked through the PASS
positions, Redo reached the completed cube, the completion message appeared,
and the header correctly displayed Game Complete.

However, when the user dismissed/clicked through the Game Complete message in
order to examine the completed cube, the header changed back to:

  Black's turn
  0 Available moves

This is incorrect. A completed game must remain identified as Game Complete
even after its completion overlay is dismissed for analysis.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
In dismissPassMessage(), the completion flag was being cleared unconditionally:

  gameCompleteActive = false;

The overlay was correctly removed, but clearing gameCompleteActive caused the
next updateStatus() call to return to the normal turn display. The completed
board therefore appeared to have a current player again even though the game
was over.

REPAIR
------
Version 1.20.29 preserves gameCompleteActive when the dismissed message was a
Game Complete message. The overlay can still be dismissed so the user can
examine/rotate/zoom the finished cube, but the underlying game state remains
terminal. updateStatus() therefore continues to display:

  Game Complete

with no Available moves count.

For an ordinary PASS message, the existing behavior is unchanged: dismissing
the PASS still advances to the opposing player and clears the pass state.

IMPORTANT PRESERVATION
----------------------
This repair is based directly on the confirmed Version 1.20.28 Bundle. No
changes are made to the working 1.20.26/1.20.28 loaded-sequence PASS history,
player-choice, Undo/Redo, or terminal-Redo logic. Bot strategy remains untouched.

EXPECTED RESULT
---------------
After loading the long sequence, selecting White vs Bot, Undoing through the
PASS positions, and Redoing to the end:

- Completion message appears.
- Header says Game Complete.
- Dismissing the completion message removes only the overlay.
- Header continues to say Game Complete.
- Available moves remains blank.
- The finished cube remains available for visual examination.

NEXT WORK
---------
The previously reported blank Game Complete message/overlay behavior remains
a separate issue to investigate if it still occurs. Additional user notes
remain before beginning bot-engine development.

VERSION 1.20.30 — SUPPRESS BLANK COMPLETION OVERLAY DURING REDO
============================================================

USER TEST RESULT / ISSUE
------------------------
Version 1.20.29 was tested successfully. The user confirmed that:

- The long move sequence loads successfully.
- Undo works through the PASS positions, alternating Black and White.
- White PASS positions show White's turn with 0 Available moves.
- Rewinding far enough allows the user, who selected White, to make a White
  move while Black remains unavailable.
- Redo reaches the completed position correctly.
- The Game Complete message appears when the game reaches completion.
- The header correctly says Game Complete at the completed position.
- Dismissing the Game Complete message leaves the header saying Game Complete
  so the completed cube can be examined.

A remaining problem was then isolated: if the user loads the sequence, clicks
Undo a few times, and then clicks Redo repeatedly to return to the completed
position, a small message bubble begins to appear at the end of Redo, but it
contains no text. The user correctly identified this as an attempted
Game Complete message with no message content available.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
restoreGameSnapshot() makes the pass/completion overlay visible whenever the
restored snapshot has gameCompleteActive set:

  passOverlay.classList.toggle('visible', passMessageActive || gameCompleteActive);

During Redo, a terminal board can therefore restore with gameCompleteActive
true while passMessageText is empty. In particular, the completion message
may already have been dismissed while the completed position is being used
for analysis. Restoring that terminal snapshot should not create a new game-
completion event or resurrect an overlay with no text.

The terminal-state protection in redoMove() correctly recognized the completed
board, but it did not suppress the overlay after that recognition. The result
was a blank message bubble at the end of Redo.

REPAIR
------
Version 1.20.30 keeps the completed-game state active when Redo reaches a
terminal board, but explicitly treats that transition as history review
rather than a new completion event.

When Redo reaches a completed board, the repair now:

- keeps gameCompleteActive = true;
- clears any pass state;
- clears passMessage.textContent;
- removes the pass/completion overlay from view.

This means the header remains:

  Game Complete

with no Available moves count, while no blank message bubble is displayed.
The finished cube remains available for examination.

IMPORTANT PRESERVATION
----------------------
Version 1.20.30 is based directly on the confirmed Version 1.20.29 Bundle.
The following behavior is intentionally preserved:

- PASS loading and validation from 1.20.22;
- loaded-sequence player-choice behavior from 1.20.24;
- PASS history positions during Undo/Redo from 1.20.26;
- terminal Game Complete header behavior from 1.20.28/1.20.29;
- dismissal of Game Complete without making the game playable again;
- White-vs-Bot selection and history-review behavior;
- all Reversi rules and legal-move generation;
- all bot engine code and strategy.

NO BOT WORK
-----------
No bot strategy, evaluation, move selection, or Bot Engine behavior was
changed in Version 1.20.30. Bot development remains deferred until the
remaining user-provided game-state notes have been addressed.

EXPECTED RESULT
---------------
After loading the long sequence and selecting White vs Bot:

1. Undo through the sequence as before.
2. Redo back to the completed position.
3. The header says Game Complete.
4. No blank message bubble appears merely because Redo reached the terminal
   position.
5. The completed cube remains available for examination.
6. If the game is subsequently rewound with Undo, normal historical turn
   review continues to work.

NEXT WORK
---------
Version 1.20.30 addresses the previously reported blank message bubble during
Redo. The user has additional notes to work through before Bot Engine
implementation begins. Those notes should be addressed one at a time from
this known-good game-state baseline.

VERSION 1.20.33 — AUTO-ROTATION VALUES AND F-KEY FLIP

----
September 5, 2026

------------

show its other sides without requiring mouse drag. The previous rotation
values made the desired side take too long to come around. The user requested

  Y = 0.006
  X = 0.004
  Z = 0.002

The user also asked whether Three.js supports a 180-degree object rotation and
requested that the F key perform a 180-degree flip around the Y axis.

IMPLEMENTATION
--------------
Auto-Rotate now uses the requested values, with the same existing frame-rate
scaling mechanism:

  cubeGroup.rotation.y += 0.006 * frameScale;
  cubeGroup.rotation.x += 0.004 * frameScale;
  cubeGroup.rotation.z += 0.002 * frameScale;

The F key now rotates the cube group 180 degrees around the Y axis using:

  cubeGroup.rotation.y += Math.PI;

Math.PI radians is 180 degrees. The existing keyboard controls and the
Start Menu-specific keyboard behavior remain unchanged. In particular,
Spacebar continues to toggle Auto-Rotate during normal game play and retains
its Start Menu radio-button cycling behavior while the Start Menu is visible.

IMPORTANT PRESERVATION

Game Complete handling, Start Menu behavior, or Bot Engine decision strategy

4. Spacebar behavior remains unchanged in both Start Menu and normal game play.

============================================================
VERSION 1.20.36 — KEYBOARD ZOOM OSCILLATION REPAIR
============================================================
DATE
----
September 5, 2026

USER REPORT
-----------
The user reported that holding the keyboard Zoom In key (-/+) caused the cube
to zoom toward one limit, then jump back toward the opposite zoom level and
repeat this cycling behavior. Zoom Out showed the same problem in reverse.
The behavior eventually appeared to settle near the final limit after many
cycles.

ROOT CAUSE
----------
This was not a decimal overflow or floating-point accumulation problem. The
keyboard handler was directly multiplying camera.position while ArcballControls
was also maintaining its own camera/animation state. With ArcballControls
animations enabled, repeated direct camera changes could fight the controls'
internal state and produce the observed oscillation during held-key repeats.

IMPLEMENTATION
--------------
Keyboard Zoom In/Out now uses a dedicated adjustKeyboardZoom() helper. It:

  1. Temporarily disables ArcballControls animation/damping for the single
     keyboard zoom operation.
  2. Measures the camera distance from controls.target.
  3. Applies the existing 0.9 / 1.1 zoom factors to that distance.
  4. Clamps the resulting distance explicitly to minDistance/maxDistance.
  5. Repositions the camera along its existing viewing direction.
  6. Calls controls.update() to synchronize the controls state.
  7. Restores the previous ArcballControls animation setting.

This preserves mouse-wheel/pinch zoom and the existing zoom limits while making
held keyboard zoom monotonic and stable.

IMPORTANT PRESERVATION
----------------------
Version 1.20.36 is based directly on the confirmed Version 1.20.35 Bundle.
The Auto-Rotate values, animated F flip, Start Menu keyboard navigation,
sequence loading, PASS handling, Undo/Redo history review, player-color choice,
and Game Complete behavior are intentionally unchanged.

EXPECTED RESULT
---------------
Holding Zoom In should continuously move toward the minimum camera distance
and stop there without cycling. Holding Zoom Out should continuously move
toward the maximum camera distance and stop there without cycling.


============================================================
FUTURE INVESTIGATION — IS 4×4×4 CUBE REVERSI A SOLVED GAME?
============================================================
DATE
----
September 5, 2026

USER INTEREST / FUTURE PROJECT
------------------------------
The user asked whether the current 4×4×4 Cube Reversi variant could potentially
be a solved game. This is NOT an active development task yet. The user explicitly
asked that the idea be recorded for later investigation.

CONCEPT TO INVESTIGATE
----------------------
A solved game is a finite game for which optimal play can be determined from a
specified starting position, with stronger forms of solving determining the
optimal outcome/strategy from every reachable position. For Cube Reversi, the
interesting question is whether the actual reachable state space of the current
4×4×4 rules is small enough to permit exhaustive analysis with practical
computing resources.

PROPOSED FUTURE INVESTIGATION
-----------------------------
When the user is ready, investigate the problem independently of the browser
UI and current Bot Engine. The initial investigation should:

  1. Precisely define the mathematical game represented by the current 4×4×4
     rules, including the 64-cell board, 26 capture directions, starting
     position, pass rules, and terminal condition.
  2. Build a standalone game-tree solver rather than immediately incorporating
     solving logic into ReversiBot.js.
  3. Start from the standard initial position and explore reachable positions
     using memoization/transposition-table techniques.
  4. Measure the number of unique reachable positions and the practical search
     requirements.
  5. Investigate board symmetries as a possible way to reduce equivalent states.
  6. Determine whether the initial position can be exhaustively solved with
     available computing resources.
  7. If a complete solve is impractical, determine how far exhaustive analysis
     can go and use that information to guide the design of the Bot Engine.

SOLVING LEVELS TO KEEP IN MIND
------------------------------
The investigation should distinguish between:

  - Knowing the theoretical outcome from the initial position.
  - Knowing a strategy that guarantees that outcome from the initial position.
  - Strongly solving the game by determining optimal outcomes from all reachable
    positions.

IMPORTANT DISTINCTION
---------------------
The user raised a conceptual question about "perfect play": if a perfect move
is predictable, why would the opponent not simply know the response and make
that move wrong? The intended clarification for future discussion is that a
perfect move is not defined as a move the opponent cannot predict. It is the
move whose eventual consequence is optimal assuming the opponent also responds
optimally. If the opponent has a forced best response, that response is already
part of the evaluation of the original move. Predictability therefore does not
make a move imperfect; the complete decision tree determines whether an
alternative move would actually produce a better outcome against best play.

The user's own observation is worth preserving: if all possible continuations
were known through exhaustive analysis, the apparent paradox disappears because
"best move" and "best response" are evaluated together rather than in
isolation.

STATUS
------
Future investigation only. Do not begin solver development or change the Bot
Engine until the user explicitly asks to pursue this question.
